## Snapshot
Alphabet is the full-stack AI franchise: Google Services (Search, YouTube, subscriptions/devices — ~$90B/qtr, the cash engine), Google Cloud (GCP + Workspace + Wiz security, now the marginal growth driver), and Other Bets (Waymo, etc.). The differentiated edge vs. other hyperscalers is owning the *entire* vertical stack — frontier models (Gemini), custom silicon (TPU, Axion CPU), networking/OCS, data centers, and apps/agents — which management frames as the source of leading power/perf efficiency and ROIC (Q1 FY26, 2026-04-29). Sits at the center of the AI capex cycle as both a demand anchor (its own ~$180-190B C26 capex) and an emerging merchant-silicon supplier (external TPU sales begin late C26). The TPU franchise is the swing variable: Asia supply-chain checks now size total TPU production at ~3.1-3.2mn units in 2026, potentially doubling to 5-6mn in 2027 (~5mn via Broadcom + ~1mn via MediaTek) — alongside AWS Trainium one of the two dominant merchant-ASIC programs (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17). On the model side, Gemini 2.5 Pro is tied #1 on LM Arena and recognized as a leading cost-effective frontier model that has displaced OpenAI on coding/overall leaderboards (JPM OpenAI initiation; JPM Anthropic deep-dive, 2025-09-18).

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) Google Search & Other ads (~$60B/qtr) · 2) Google Cloud (GCP + Workspace + Wiz) · 3) YouTube ads + subs/devices |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Advertisers (Search/YouTube demand engine) + cloud enterprises & frontier labs (OpenAI, Anthropic, xAI/SpaceX) + 350M+ consumer paid subs |
| **Key suppliers** | TSMC foundry/CoWoS · Broadcom & MediaTek (TPU ASICs) · HBM/DRAM (SK Hynix/Micron/Samsung) · Hon Hai racks · own datacenters |

## Current state (Q2 FY26, 2026-07-22)
**NEW 2026-08-18 — SEARCH AS SEE'S CANDIES, AI AS BNSF: Ben Thompson's interpretation of the $80bn equity raise is that Alphabet is deliberately exchanging a smaller percentage margin for a much larger absolute-profit pool.** Search is the near-zero-marginal-cost cash engine; AI is the capital-intensive railroad. The dilution is rational only if the intelligence/white-collar-work TAM becomes large enough to dominate the lower margin. Thompson pairs that with the silicon endgame: [[GOOG]] and [[AMZN]], not the neoclouds, are the most credible long-run threats to [[NVDA]] because they can externalize TPU/Trainium while funding the capital fight at a lower cost of capital (Ben Thompson, *Invest Like the Best*, 2026-08-18).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-17 (/run-inbox) — GOOGLE IS THE COUNTERPARTY ON *BOTH* LEGS OF [[ANTHROPIC]]'S $50BN BUILDOUT, AND ON ONE OF THEM IT IS EXTENDING ITS OWN CREDIT (Epoch AI · Campbell Hutcheson, "Will financing bottleneck AI compute? An Anthropic case study", Gradient Updates, 2026-08-12).**
- **LEG 1 — THE SILICON: Google supplies MORE THAN 1 GW OF TPU SYSTEMS to Anthropic, financed by a $34.5BN DEBT PACKAGE** (led by Apollo-managed funds with Blackstone and global banks; **$30bn carries [[AVGO]]'s support, $4.5bn does not**) raised into a dedicated equipment SPV — the **"AI XPV Platform"** — that borrows, buys the racks and **leases them to Anthropic on a five-year lease**, with lease payments servicing the debt and the racks as collateral; drawn in **~16 stages, ~$24bn expected out by summer 2027**. ➜ **This is a DEMAND-DURABILITY datapoint for the TPU franchise that this page did not previously have in financing form: >1 GW of TPU systems whose payment stream is contractually locked into a five-year lease funded by third-party institutional debt, not by Anthropic's cash flow.**
- 🔴 **LEG 2 — THE CONTINGENT EXPOSURE, AND THIS IS THE PART THAT BELONGS ON GOOGLE'S PAGE RATHER THAN ANTHROPIC'S: on the DATACENTER side, GOOGLE PROVIDES CONDITIONAL SUPPORT PROTECTING INVESTORS AGAINST PART OF THE LOSSES IF FLUIDSTACK STOPS PAYING RENT, across five sites totalling 1,428 MW OF CRITICAL IT and ~$15.183BN OF PROJECT DEBT** (Lake Mariner/TeraWulf 378 MW / $3.200bn · Barber Lake/Cipher 207 MW / $1.733bn · Abernathy/Fluidstack JV 168 MW / $1.300bn · River Bend/Hut 8 245 MW / $3.250bn · Meridian Arc/Next Frontier-Fluidstack JV 430 MW / $5.700bn; debt raised **before** construction began). **At Lake Mariner specifically, Google can pay missed rent and assume the lease, or fund a termination payment applied to the project debt.**
- **Epoch's framing, which is the sentence to carry: "Google places its credit behind a deployment from which it benefits commercially, while institutional investors provide most of the cash."** The mechanism has a name — **"vendor-supported financing"**: suppliers lend their **credit**, not their cash, to deployments they profit from.
- ⚠️ **HOW TO HOLD IT: this is a contingent, unquantified obligation — Epoch says "part of" the losses and gives no cap, no fee and no accounting treatment, so it cannot be sized from this source. It sits alongside the capex/FCF debate already on this page as a SECOND balance-sheet channel through which the TPU push is being funded off Alphabet's own cash flow, and it is a direct counterpart to the [[AVGO]] backstop on the compute leg (max reported exposure $29bn). Track the disclosure at the next 10-Q.** ⚠️ **UNIT DISCIPLINE: the $15.183bn / 1,428 MW implies ~$10.6m of PROJECT DEBT per MW of CRITICAL IT (≈$10.6bn/GW) — a debt-per-watt figure on an IT-load basis, NOT a build cost and NOT revenue per watt; do not compare it to the $30–35bn/GW Alphabet capex-per-GW mark on [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).**
- **Why it matters commercially, not just financially: Anthropic is the anchor external TPU customer behind the merchant-TPU/TPU-aaS thesis on this page (Barclays 07-24: ~$88bn of TPU revenue in 2Q26 backlog, ~$250bn of 2028 TPU-aaS revenue). Epoch shows the mechanism by which that backlog was made financeable — and therefore also the mechanism by which it becomes Google's problem if the tenant's rent stops.**

**🆕 2026-08-17 (/run-inbox) — THE OPEN-WEIGHTS POSITION, MEASURED: GOOGLE IS THE #2 BASE-MODEL SOURCE ON THE HUGGING FACE HUB, GEMMA OUT-DOWNLOADS LLAMA ~3:1 — AND ESSENTIALLY NONE OF IT IS AT FRONTIER SCALE (Hugging Face · Adina Yakefu / Apolinário / Irene Solaiman, "State of Open Models: Summer 2026 Observations", 2026-08-14).**
- **Ecosystem position: Google is the SECOND-LARGEST base-model source on the Hub with 82,506 DERIVATIVES**, behind **Qwen at 151,448** — i.e. Google's open ecosystem is roughly **55% of Qwen's** and comfortably ahead of Llama (see [[META]], where Qwen is **4.7x** the Llama repository count).
- **Distribution, where Gemma is clearly winning the Western contest: GGUF downloads per month — Qwen 39.6m · GEMMA 20.8m · LLAMA 7.5m.** Gemma runs **~2.8x Llama's monthly local-inference volume**.
- ⚠️ **THE QUALIFIER THAT MATTERS FOR HOW THIS IS SCORED: essentially NONE of Google's 2026 Hub downloads are of models above 70B** (the same is true of MSFT and IBM Granite; NVDA 14%, META 9%; against MiniMax ~all, Moonshot 88%, DeepSeek 55%). **Google's open-weights position is a SMALL-MODEL position** — which is consistent with the Hub-wide shape (**models under 1B take 83% of all-time downloads; everything above 100B takes 1%; only 3% of 2026 download volume goes above 70B**) but means Gemma's distribution lead says nothing about frontier capability, and does not offset the Gemini-4 quality question already on this page.

**🆕 2026-08-10 (PM) — GS reads the reorg as a STRATEGY CHANGE, not a talent problem, and stays Buy (Goldman Sachs / Eric Sheridan · Alex Vegliante, "Did Alphabet Just Signal a Shift in its AI Strategy?", 2026-08-10 16:05 ET; **Buy reiterated, 12-mo PT $435**).** Framing the same events this page logged as an exodus, GS's read: *"the recent **reorganization**, AI commentary, and **company-wide capital raising activity (across both debt & equity)** suggest Alphabet is **evolving from a research-led AI organization (purely for the sake of research) into a SCALED AI PLATFORM COMPANY** where **consumer AI efforts strengthen distribution, Google Cloud monetizes infrastructure demand, and frontier research remains important but increasingly SERVES THE BROADER BUSINESS STRATEGY."* ⚠️ **This is the direct counter-frame to the SemiAnalysis 08-06 call ("DeepMind is no longer a frontier lab… odds of reaching SOTA again have dropped to zero") already on this page — and note both agree on the same underlying observation, GCP being the value node; they disagree only on whether the model business being subordinated is a loss.** GS also anchors the debate to the **7/22 earnings report** and dates the investor-debate escalation from there. Cross-ref [themes/custom-asic-tpu.md](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md), [ANTHROPIC.md](ANTHROPIC.md).

**🆕 2026-08-10 — the AI leadership exodus, and the contrarian read (FundaAI Research, 2026-08-10).** The facts as reported: **Jeff Dean, Sanjay Ghemawat, Quoc Le, Oriol Vinyals and several others are leaving**; **Demis Hassabis moves from CEO of Google DeepMind to Chair and Alphabet Chief Scientist**, with **Koray Kavukcuoglu taking on more of the day-to-day operating responsibility.** The market read it as a major brain drain.

FundaAI's dissent — *"The Market Sees Brain Drain; We See a Long-Overdue Organizational Consolidation"* — rests on three claims worth testing rather than accepting:
1. **Many of the departing seniors had already become peripheral to the core Gemini / frontier-LLM effort** over the past year or two — the distinction being between "the people who were historically most important to Google AI" and "the people actually driving frontier-model execution today."
2. **The real problem started *after* Gemini 3's success**, not with these departures: with arguably the leading multimodal model, best-in-class proprietary compute, and Nobel-level recognition for AlphaFold, the organisation concluded the frontier race was caught up, and **redirected its most exceptional talent toward AI-for-Science / drug discovery** — away from the engineering-heavy work of frontier-model scaling.
3. **That was the strategic misread**, on their argument: the path runs **scale compute → scale coding → scale agent harnesses → scale automated AI R&D → recursive self-improvement (RSI)**, and **AI-for-Science should sit on top of the RSI engine, not compete with it** — *"the ultimate form of AI for Science may not be scientists using AI to do science; it may be building an AI scientist whose own research capabilities improve over time."* Their framing of what OpenAI and Anthropic demonstrated is precisely this industrial-scaling path.

**How to use it:** this is the bull case for the reorg — that the bottleneck was organisational rather than talent, and consolidation under Koray refocuses execution on the scaling path. It is also, read the other way, a statement that **Google mis-allocated its best researchers for roughly two years** while competitors compounded — which is a bear datapoint dressed as a bull one. The testable prediction is straightforward: **the next Gemini release cadence and coding/agentic benchmark position**. Cross-ref [ANTHROPIC.md](ANTHROPIC.md), [OPENAI.md](OPENAI.md).

- **Consolidated revenue $119.8B, +24% (+23% cc); operating income +30% to $40.8B and operating margin +2pp to 34.0%.** This was Alphabet's 12th consecutive quarter of double-digit growth. GAAP EPS was $9.11, but a net unrealized equity-security gain contributed $6.26/share after tax; EPS excluding only that disclosed gain was approximately $2.85 (derived from company figures, not a fully normalized EPS). (Alphabet 2Q26 earnings release, 2026-07-22)
- **Search & Other +17% to $63.3B; YouTube ads +13% to $11.1B; Services +15% to $94.5B with 41.8% segment margin.** Search was in line with the +17% buyside bogey and about +0.4pp above JPM's +16.6% estimate using Alphabet's rounded headline growth; YouTube beat JPM's +8.6% / buyside +10% bars by about +4.4pp / +3.0pp on the same rounded basis. Management said popular AI features are driving query growth; World Cup-related YouTube videos reached 1.7B+ unique viewers. (Alphabet 2Q26 release/slides, 2026-07-22; JPM · Anmuth, 2026-07-14; BofA · Fenske, 2026-07-12)
- **Google Cloud +82% to $24.8B, far above JPM's +70.9% and the +70% buyside bogey; operating income $8.8B and margin 35.6% vs 20.7% y/y.** Backlog reached **$514B**, +$52B sequentially from 1Q's $462B. The release attributes the acceleration to enterprise AI solutions, enterprise AI infrastructure and core GCP; it does not disclose the TPU-system revenue contribution. (Alphabet 2Q26 release/slides, 2026-07-22; JPM · Anmuth, 2026-07-14; BofA · Fenske, 2026-07-12)
- **AI adoption anchors:** Gemini Enterprise is used by nearly 90% of the Fortune 100; first-party model APIs process **~22B tokens/minute vs 16B+ last quarter**; Gemini app reached **950M MAU**. These are company-reported usage figures, not analyst estimates. (Alphabet 2Q26 release/slides, 2026-07-22)
- **Capex doubled y/y to $44.9B; operating cash flow was $39.1B and FCF turned negative at $(5.9)B (TTM $53.3B, -20% y/y).** Alphabet repurchased no stock in Q2. It raised $49.6B net through common/preferred equity and $20.3B net through senior notes; long-term debt reached $98.2B vs $46.5B at FY25 year-end. On the release itself the FY26 capex guide was not updated, but **on the earnings call (07-22) management RAISED the FY26 capex guide to $195-205B (from $180-190B)** and reiterated FY27 capex will rise "significantly" (see next bullet); capital intensity remains the core unresolved bear case. (Alphabet 2Q26 earnings release/slides, 2026-07-22)
- **Q2 FY26 earnings-call color — capex guide raised, TPU-system sales began, Gemini 4 pretraining underway (Alphabet "Q2 2026 earnings call", 2026-07-22):** management **raised FY26 capex to $195-205B (from $180-190B)** citing "an acceleration in the delivery of capacity to meet growing demand," and reiterated capex will increase "significantly" again in 2027. **TPU-system sales were recognized for the first time in Q2** (delivered to customer data centers) — a "relatively small portion" this year, ramping as it exits 2026, with the **vast majority of the revenue from existing TPU agreements realized in 2027**; CFO Anat Ashkenazi framed TPU-system sales as a **TAM expansion** (systems sold into customers' own data centers) and declined to break out TPU-specific margins. Given the supply-constrained environment, Google will **expand use of third-party capacity in Q3 as a bridge** while building internal capacity — a modest near-term Cloud-margin headwind (on top of the ongoing Wiz-integration margin headwind). On models: **Gemini 3.6 Flash + 3.5 Flash-Lite** shipped, **3.5 Pro in testing**, and Google has **begun its "most ambitious pre-training run yet" for Gemini 4**; Pichai again acknowledged Google needs to improve on **coding/agentic decoding**. Demand proof: API **~22B tokens/min (from ~16B q/q)**, 9M+ monthly devs, **~500 cloud customers each processed >1T tokens** in the last year (2,000+ enterprises >100B tokens), Gemini Enterprise at ~90% of the Fortune 100, Cloud new-customer acquisition velocity **>2x y/y** with existing customers exceeding commitments by **>50%**, and Marketplace transactions **+7x y/y**. Anti-Gravity (agentic dev platform) at **2.4M weekly active users**. (Alphabet "Q2 2026 earnings call", 2026-07-22) [Transcript](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Google_transcript.html)
- **Q2 FY26 call — CFO 2H outlook + Search monetization machinery (transcript outlook block, folded in 2026-07-23 after the first pass missed it):** CFO Anat Ashkenazi's 2H26 outlook carried two Search-optics flags — **(i) FX flips: Q3 expected to carry a slight FX headwind to consolidated revenue vs a ~+1pp FX tailwind in Q2, "seen primarily in Search and YouTube ads"; (ii) tough comps: "in Q3, we will begin lapping an acceleration in search performance that began in the third quarter last year"** — the first guided Search-deceleration setup of the AI-Mode era (the base steepens: Search +12% Q2'25 → +15% Q3'25 → +17% Q4'25 → +19% Q1'26). Backlog quality color: **"the majority of the backlog is related to typical GCP contracts for a broad mix of customers," with "just over 50%" expected to convert to revenue within 24 months**; and Cloud growth **"accelerated meaningfully even after excluding the impact of TPU system sales"** — a qualitative ex-TPU answer to the gross-backlog bear. Search machinery (Pichai/Schindler): **AI Mode surpassed 1B MAU** (global expansion Oct '25), driving an *incremental* increase in total queries; AI Overviews + AI Mode merged into "one seamless search experience"; **billions of clicks/week sent to websites** via AI features; **AI-Mode response cost at its lowest level since launch**; **AI Max out of beta with ~500k advertisers** — adopters of AI-powered campaigns (AI Max/PMax) see **~+15% more conversions/value at similar ROAS**; Gemini query understanding drove a **20% improvement in relevant-ad matching on shopping** (longer queries "previously difficult to monetize"; Q&A: "billions of net new searches that weren't really monetizable before"); AAA Auto Club case: +17% conversion volume / −11% cost-per-lead; **>50% of SMB customers globally use AI creative tools**; AI Overviews monetization "encouraging" **even as it expands to more commercial queries**; Search mix: retail the largest growth contributor, finance next; **network ads −1% y/y**. (Alphabet "Q2 2026 earnings call", 2026-07-22 — BBG ASR transcript on file)
- **Q2 FY26 10-Q detail (filed 2026-07-23, acc 0001652044-26-000071):** the equity-gain quarter quantified — **$99.0B total gain on equity securities inside OI&E** (o/w **$77.4B unrealized on non-marketable, measurement-alternative holdings** — per Note 3 that book "primarily consist[s] of our investment in a private company" [unnamed — Anthropic-consistent per BofA's 07-16 ~$80bn-reval preview], NOT SpaceX — **plus $21.4B on marketable equity, where the SpaceX shares now sit: $80.0B short-term-restricted + $14.1B restricted through 3Q27 = a $94.1B SpaceX position first quantified**), driving OI&E +$98.0B, pretax $138.8B, **tax provision $26.6B (19.1% ETR; deferred-tax liability jumped $0.9B→$22.8B — essentially the tax on the gain)**, GAAP NI $112.2B / diluted EPS $9.11 on 12,309M WA shares (+16M preferred-conversion effect; **$86M first preferred dividends**, NI-to-common $112.1B). Balance sheet: total assets **$922.0B** (+$218B q/q), cash+marketables **$242.5B**, PP&E net **$321.2B** with **$122.8B gross assets not yet in service** (capacity in flight), **inventory first split out at $10.0B vs $2.4B YE25** (device + TPU-systems build ahead of external TPU deliveries), LT debt **$98.2B** ($101.1B face — $20B USD + ~$31.8B-equiv 5-currency notes issued 1H26), and the **$18.0B 6.25% mandatory convertible preferred** now on the BS. RPO: **total $519.5B / Google Cloud $513.9B** (~50% expected to convert within 24 months) — the exact figure behind the slides' "$514B backlog". 6-mo cash flow: OCF $84.9B, capex $80.6B, **zero buybacks**, common dividends $5.2B; supplemental: **$29.1B of PP&E purchases sitting in payables/accruals** — committed capex running ahead of cash capex. (Alphabet Q2'26 10-Q, 2026-07-23)
- **Q2'26 margins ex one-offs + first TPU-system revenue estimate (Capstone analysis on the 10-Q, 2026-07-23):** the only Q2'26 operating one-off is the **$1.5B PriceRunner principal charge** (G&A, Google Services segment; the rest of the $2.1B award — $581M interest/costs — sits in OI&E). Ex-charge, **consolidated OM = 35.3% vs 34.0% reported**; the Q2'25 base carried its own **$1.4B settlement-in-principle charge** (the Texas privacy settlement, also Services G&A per the Q2'25 10-Q), so the **clean y/y bridge is 33.9% → 35.3% (+1.4pp clean vs +1.6pp reported; clean OI +29.4% on +24.2% revenue)**. Services margin ex-charges 41.75% → **43.4%**; the offsetting recurring drag is Alphabet-level activities (shared Gemini R&D/training) at **−$5.8B vs −$3.4B LY (−4.8pp vs −3.5pp of consolidated OM)**. Clean EPS ≈ **$3.00** ($9.11 − $6.26 company-disclosed equity-gain effect − ~$0.15 PriceRunner; caveat: core ex-gain ETR was an unusually low 11.7% on discrete tax items). **TPU-system revenue recognized in Q2 ≈ $1.2-1.3B central, honest range $0.9-1.6B (~5% of Cloud revenue)** — no figure is disclosed; triangulated two ways: (a) revenue-side — implied organic ex-TPU Cloud growth ~72-73% y/y vs +63.4% in Q1'26 (consistent with the CFO's "accelerated meaningfully even excluding TPU" and JPM's effectively-ex-TPU +70.9% model, JPM 07-14); (b) cost-side — "inventory and other costs" jumped to the **#2 ranked driver** of the +$5.4B other-COGS increase (bracketed ~$0.9-1.5B by the MD&A's descending-order convention; devices ~flat pre-Pixel-launch), grossed up at the ~10-15% TPU markup (expert callback, 2026-04-30). Implies **~30-40k chips ≈ ~35-55MW delivered (~1% of 2026 TPU output)** — genuinely the "relatively small portion" management described, with the $10.0B inventory (+$6.7B built in Q2 alone) staged for the guided 2H26 exit-ramp/2027 recognition wave. Mix math: TPU sales diluted Cloud segment margin ~**−1.2pp** (35.6% reported vs ~36.8% ex-TPU) — expect this drag to GROW as deliveries ramp. Load-bearing assumption is the organic-growth rate (±1pp ≈ ∓$135M on the TPU figure); estimate double-check-audited (verdict PARTIAL, adjudicated: range widened, hard ceiling ~$2.5B at zero-acceleration). (Capstone analysis, 2026-07-23, on Alphabet Q2'26 10-Q)
- **Q2'26 10-Q vs 1Q26 10-Q — meaningful disclosure changes (Capstone diff, 2026-07-23):** (i) **purchase commitments $232.7B → $707.0B (+$474B in one quarter)** — "significant majority" = long-term supply agreements securing production capacity for technical infrastructure/inventory components through 2030; energy service agreements extended to 2-26yr terms with obligations **through 2054** (was 2-20yr/2047) — the contractual shadow of the capex-guide raise + TPU ramp; (ii) **SpaceX stake first quantified and reclassified to marketable: $94.1B** ($80.0B short-term-restricted + $14.1B restricted through 3Q27) — the $21.4B marketable-equity gain; (iii) **the private-company stake (unnamed; Anthropic-consistent): measurement-alternative carrying value $64.1B → $124.3B**, $87.9B remeasured at fair value (Level 2) in Q2 (+$77.4B unrealized gain), **$21.1B of non-marketable purchases in Q2 alone** (Q1: $0.9B), plus a **NEW $20.0B equity-derivative notional** = contingent future funding tranches on operational/financial milestones through 2030 (unconsolidated-VIE future funding commitments $1.1B → $21.9B) — funded + contingent ≈ the ~$40B Anthropic commitment JPM flagged (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 06-16); (iv) **data-center credit-backstop (credit-derivative) notionals $16.9B (YE25) → $43.8B**, terms up to 15yr, while energy-infra financial guarantees ticked $9.0B → $7.6B (terms now through Sep-26) — the off-balance-sheet DC-financing web keeps compounding; (v) **TPU recognition pulled forward** — the Q1 10-Q said deliveries would begin "later in 2026"; Q2 recognized first revenue and added the product-sales/inventory accounting-policy sections + the first-ever $10.0B inventory line; (vi) **PriceRunner/Klarna $2.1B accrued** (the judgment pre-flagged as a $1.5bn risk on 07-01/Redburn came in larger); **Android EC fine now final** (ECJ denied the appeal Jul-26) — $5.2B cash paid July = a Q3 cash event, no new P&L; (vii) **PP&E sitting in payables/accruals $10.6B (Jun-25) → $24.1B (Mar-26) → $29.1B** — committed capex accelerating ahead of cash capex; (viii) GFiber held-for-sale quantified ($7.1B PP&E inside other current assets; close expected late-2026, → 49.99% equity-method stake + $1.5B cash + $2.0B note); (ix) headcount 194,668 → 198,933 (+4,265 q/q — hiring back on); risk factors: no material changes vs the FY25 10-K in either quarter. (Capstone diff of Alphabet Q2'26 10-Q vs Q1'26 10-Q, 2026-07-23)
- **🆕 TPU-as-a-Service — the P&L revisions and the PT mechanic (Barclays · Ross Sandler, "Unpacking TPU-As-A-Service", 2026-07-24).** _The full structure, unit economics and GW ramp for this note are already laid out in the "TPU-as-a-Service / merchant TPU sales sized" bullet under Capstone estimates below — this bullet carries only what that one doesn't: the consolidated estimate changes, the PT mechanic, and a figure discrepancy to resolve._ Consolidated revisions on a full model rebuild: **total Alphabet revenue +12% to $650B in '27 (from $580B)** and **+40% to $950B in '28 (from $680B)**; **EBITDA +7% ('27) / +23% ('28)**; **EPS FY27 $15.40 (from $13.94)** and **FY28 $21.88 (from $15.98)**; effect on consensus — could **lift '28 consensus gross profit ~15% and operating income by more**. **The PT mechanic matters: $425 (OW) is held in level but is now struck on a 2027 multiple rather than a blended 27/28** — so the unchanged PT is not an unchanged view, it is the valuation year moving forward. Per Barclays' own desk, Sandler "**is the only analyst on Wall Street that is modeling out the revenues from TPUs**" (Barclays desk · Justin Kantrowitz, 2026-07-24). ⚠️ **Figure to reconcile before using:** Sandler's evidence line cites "**the $811B (up +$479B Q/Q) in purchase commitments buried in the 10-Q this week**," which does **not** tie to the Capstone 10-Q diff above — that reads **$232.7B → $707.0B (+$474B)** off the filing itself. The sequential deltas nearly agree (+$479B vs +$474B) but the absolute base differs by ~$104B, likely a broader aggregation (operating-lease and/or energy commitments). **Use the $707.0B filing figure as the house number**; treat $811B as Barclays' basis. Worth resolving at the next filing.
- **🆕 The other side of the same print — GOOG -6.9% on 07-23, and the buy-side is treating it as a source of funds (Barclays desk · Justin Kantrowitz, 2026-07-24).** The tape: **GOOG -6.9%** on 07-23 (alongside TSLA -14.5%, SPX -1.2%, NDX -1.9%; Rothschild & Co · Adley, 2026-07-24), and per the FT, "Google burning through cash for the first time since going public decades ago as it raised its spending forecast for AI infrastructure" (@FT, 2026-07-23). Barclays' desk colour is the useful part: **"most investors didn't seem surprised by the reaction in the stock… but the moves in AMZN and META (along with others) was not expected."** On positioning — **"most investors expect the stock to be a source of funds in the near term as we get past the hurdle of seeing negative FCF and knowing that's going to worsen,"** with decent long-only two-way flow and **some rotation of dollars into AMZN**. One hypothesis was floated and then **checked and rejected**: that weakness came from funds owning too much GOOGL across both equity *and* hyperscaler debt and needing to trim the equity leg — Kantrowitz **polled four long-only investors, all of whom said no such dynamic existed at their firm** (worth logging as a debunked mechanism rather than a live one). His own read is the mirror of Sandler's note: **"investors are trying to dig through the purchase commitments and figure out what kind of revenue could be on the come. At times like this, investors seem to only focus on the balance sheet impact and seem to [be] missing the potential ramp in revenues that are coming."** Also flagged: with oil back above $100, names with fuel exposure (**AMZN, DASH, [[UBER]]**) should underperform. Note the sharp divergence in how the same disclosure is read — the balance-sheet lens (negative FCF worsening, capex to $195-205B) versus the income-statement lens (~$88B of TPU backlog, $250B of 2028 TPU-aaS revenue). **This is the central GOOGL debate as of this ingest.**
- **Subsequent: $80B→$85B equity raise** (Jun '26; $30B public + $40B ATM + $10B Berkshire PP at ~$351/$348) for AI infra — largest US corporate equity offering ever (briefing 2026-06-03/06).
- **Token volume / demand proof (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16):** disclosed at I/O 2026 it now processes **~3.2 quadrillion tokens/month across its surfaces (~7x Y/Y)** — JPM's headline evidence that agentic inference is the marginal unit of compute demand growth.
- **Capex/funding scale (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16):** JPM models FY26 capex **$185B** (mgmt raised midpoint +$10B q/q in 1Q), **~$300B FY27E / ~$350B FY28E**, with capex consuming nearly all OCF across the horizon (FY28 near breakeven before dividends). FCF compresses to ~$12B FY26E → **–$27B FY27E**. Funding: ~$51bn senior notes YTD ($20bn USD + ~$31bn equiv. across five FX — ~$10bn EUR, ~$7bn GBP, ~$6bn CAD, ~$4bn CHF, ~$4bn JPY) + the $84.75bn equity raise; LT debt jumped ~$47bn→~$78bn in a quarter; ~$127bn liquidity. JPM's read: Alphabet is "aggressively pre-funding the FY27/FY28 ramp while markets remain receptive" and has "crossed the Rubicon" into a structural funding requirement.
- **Off-balance-sheet exposure (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16):** a growing portfolio of credit backstops on third-party data centers — financial guarantees (max future exposure **~$9bn**) and credit derivatives (**~$28bn**); on default Alphabet can assume/sublease the underlying leases. Plus the **$40bn commitment to Anthropic** and Wiz/Intersect acquisitions cited as the AI strategy expanding beyond the income statement.
- **TPU efficiency (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16):** Ironwood/v7 ramping; Ironwood delivers **~2x the perf/watt of prior-gen Trillium (~30x vs first-gen TPU)**, with well-optimized TPU deployments ~2x+ perf/watt vs contemporary merchant GPUs on inference — the core power-efficiency case in a power-constrained buildout. JPM estimates Google TPU units **1.8M (2025) → 4.5M (2026E) → 8.0M (2027E)**.
- **1Q26 print color (Citi recap, 2026-04-30):** Search +16% XFX / +19% reported (better than the "high-teens" buy-side bar); Google Cloud +60%+; **revenue from Gemini models +800% y/y**; search queries at all-time highs (AI Overviews/AI Mode lengthening queries → more intent), and **AI Max now ~30% of campaigns**; the print drove '27 EPS estimates "way closer to $15" than before (Citi C1Q26 recap, 2026-04-30). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_30_msft_wfc_c1q26_recap_30_apr_26.html)
- **Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter" (2026-06-22):** rates GOOGL **Market-Perform, TP $390**; headline call is the laggard-turned-leader narrative — Google's vertically integrated solution now "neck-and-neck with Microsoft for incremental cloud dollars added Q/Q" (Google added ~$2.4B q/q vs AWS ~$2.0B, Azure ~$2.5B). **Cloud backlog ~doubled q/q to ~$462B** with >50% converting to revenue within 24 months; backlog now includes **third-party TPU hardware sales** (Google's custom chips deployed directly into customer datacenters) — back-end-loaded in 2026 with the bulk of revenue in 2027, and inherently lumpy on shipment timing. Cloud margin ~33% in 1Q26 (vs ~30% 4Q25, ~17.8% 1Q25), with a low-single-digit % op-margin headwind from the Wiz acquisition through the rest of 2026 (Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter", 2026-06-22).

- **Axion custom Arm CPU — silicon franchise widening (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back", 2026-02-09):** Google's custom-CPU line for GCP, announced 2024 / GA 2025. **C4A**: up to 72 Neoverse V2 cores, standard mesh, 8ch DDR5 + PCIe5, large monolithic 5nm die — SemiAnalysis *estimates* (analyst inference from Cloud Next 2024 wafer images, **not a confirmed Google spec**) the die prints 81 cores in a 9×9 mesh with 9 disabled for yield. SemiAnalysis further *believes* (analyst inference, **not confirmed Google roadmap**) a new 3nm die was designed for a 96-core C4A bare-metal instance in preview late 2025. **N4A** (cost-effective scale-out/microservices tier): now in preview, 64 lower-perf Neoverse N3 cores on a much smaller full-custom TSMC 3nm die, "allowing significant volume ramps through 2026" (SemiAnalysis, 2026-02-09). **Vertical-integration trajectory — Axion has moved to Full COT (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21, light read-through folded 2026-07-24):** ~99% of hyperscaler ASIC silicon today runs **Hybrid COT** (customer owns architecture + IP selection, a vendor handles physical implementation) at an estimated **1.2x EDA premium** over a pure vendor-designed ASIC; **Full COT** — where the customer licenses the Synopsys or Cadence stack directly — carries an estimated **1.6x premium**, and SemiAnalysis names **"Google moving Axion in-house last quarter"** alongside Apple M-series and Tesla AI4-AI6 as the Full-COT cohort. One line, but it dates a real step up Google's vertical-integration curve on the CPU side (more EDA/tooling cost carried in-house, less dependence on a design-service middleman) and is the CPU-side analogue of the TPU-COT/MediaTek dual-sourcing already on this page. (SemiAnalysis, 2026-05-21) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html) TPU volumes are the #2 driver of HBM bit demand after Nvidia (via the Broadcom ASIC partnership) — detail on AVGO's page. TPU design philosophy prioritizes *reliability* — deliberately non-aggressive vs merchant GPUs on redline features like compute-in-base-die (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: The Rise and Roadmap of HBM", 2026-02-09). **Read-through (Bernstein · David Dai/Rasgon "The CPU Renaissance? $223bn TAM", 2026-06-17):** independently sizes the Axion program — **Axion C4A = 72 Neoverse V3 cores (TSMC 5nm)**, **~21% of GCP instances**, and **TPU7x runs 1 CPU : 4 TPU** — corroborating the Arm-CPU/head-node ratio on the page (Bernstein's V3 core-arch label differs from SemiAnalysis's V2 characterization above; both retained). [Bernstein CPU](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_on_CPU.html)
- **TPU generation / ecosystem (Barron's, 2026-06-29 / 2026-06-01):** TPU now on its **8th generation** (Barron's, 2026-06-01); **Anthropic is a major TPU customer**; Google formed a **JV with Blackstone to rent out TPU capacity** in the cloud (Barron's, 2026-06-01). Barron's calls the TPU "generally recognized to be the most formidable competition to Nvidia on the market" (Barron's, 2026-06-29).
- **Cloud / Gemini scale (Barron's, 2026-06-29):** Google Cloud posted **63% revenue growth in Q1 2026 — strongest since the 2019 disclosure began** (corroborates the Q1 FY26 print above); **Gemini has 900M users** (Barron's, 2026-06-29). TD Cowen (John Blackledge) sees cloud revenue compounding a **37% CAGR, ~$100B this year → $480B in 2031**, calling Google "just at the starting point of AI driving topline growth at Google Cloud" (TD Cowen via Barron's, 2026-06-29).
- **Frontier-lab read / coding lag (FundaAI "LLM 26H1 Update Part 2", 2026-06-24):** I/O 2026 answered the "scaling wall" thesis with three numbers — **FY26 capex $180–190B (6x 2022’s $31B); AI Mode in Search reached 1 billion MAU in one year; and tokens processed via the Gemini API +7x YoY, from 480T → 3,200T tokens/month**. Gemini Omni (May-19) is the first natively any-to-any model. But **in coding Google sits a layer behind** — Pichai admitted a meaningful gap vs frontier (Flash 3.5 weak in math/coding); it lacks a high-frequency agent entry point like Claude Code/Codex, so it has not yet closed a data flywheel. FundaAI argues that **Google’s compute advantage via in-house TPU is not fully priced in** and that it keeps catching up to Anthropic/OpenAI on the TPU base (FundaAI LLM Part 2, 2026-06-24). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/LLM_Part_2_funda.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/LLM_Part_2_funda.html)
- **AI S-curve / 2027 capex (Barclays · Sandler, 2026-06-01):** the 3 leading labs >$130B revenue 2026 / exit-ARR ~$200B; Google DeepMind seeing similar trends in the API and the paid Gemini base. **Google signaled capex will rise "significantly" again in 2027** — Barclays notes the 2025→2026 capex delta was +$95B; at the same level of adds, FY27 would reach ~$300B (well above consensus), and 28 GW of B200-equivalent in 2027 ≈ ~$1T of capex. On the semis side, **TPU 8i ramping (AVGO on 8i, MTK on 8t)** as evidence of the growing importance of inference silicon (Barclays "AI’s S-Curve Is Steepening", 2026-06-01). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Barclays_U_S_Internet_Semiconductors_AI_s_S_Curve_Is_Steepening.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Barclays_U_S_Internet_Semiconductors_AI_s_S_Curve_Is_Steepening.html)
- **Gen-AI Handbook — hyperscaler/model-vendor thesis (Bernstein "Scaling Intelligence", 2026-06-22):** Google is a full-stack hyperscaler (Gemini via Google Cloud; Claude accessible on all three clouds). Central thesis: **AI is additive to the IaaS-PaaS TAM, not disruptive** — AI runs best where the data lives (hyperscalers), and even software written by AI (Claude/Codex/Cursor) needs Cloud infra (CPU+GPU). Expects **3–5 frontier vendors** with "nuanced" differences between models trained on the same data, with revenue migrating increasingly to agentic apps/platforms on top of the models, and **thousands of small specialized models** preferred by enterprises (less hallucination, lower cost) — a constructive read for Google’s model+distribution+TPU position (Bernstein "Scaling Intelligence: The Generative AI Handbook", 2026-06-22). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_on_Gen_AI_Software.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_on_Gen_AI_Software.html)
- **TPU sales recorded GROSS, inside Cloud backlog (post-1Q26 callback, 2026-04-30):** a key accounting clarification — Google records external TPU hardware sales (e.g. to Anthropic) on a **gross** basis, taking a **~10–15% license-fee markup (~$2–3K/chip on ~$20–21K chips)**, with the full grossed-up amount sitting in the Google Cloud backlog (no capex on these). The expert estimates **~$85B of TPU dollars entered the 1Q26 backlog** (vs ~$11.5B in 4Q25), reflecting ~4GW of Anthropic TPU orders; stripping TPUs out leaves **~$365B of "pure" cloud backlog (+$134B q/q)**, implying ex-TPU cloud growth of ~70%/62% in CY26/27 and a TPU-driven capex-est raise toward **~$290B+ for FY27**. Also: **first quarter with no buyback since COVID** (100% capital-allocation focus on capacity), and the 1Q 10-Q disclosed Google providing **credit backstops to third-party data centers/power infrastructure** tied to these deals. TPU-sale revenue recognition is mostly a **2027** event (expert recap, 2026-04-30). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_30_hyper_wrc_recap_30_apr_2026.html)
- **TPU competitive read-through (BofA/Vivek Arya, 2026-05-19):** the Google/Blackstone-class ~500MW DC vehicle for TPUs is read as a **financing structure to support TPU capex** (not Google OEM-ing chips), and ~80–90% of TPU inference is for Google's own properties (Search/YouTube/AI Mode) — Google Cloud itself likely runs more GPUs than TPUs; one Axion CPU to two TPUs ratio (BofA NVDA setup, 2026-05-19). Earlier Asia-supply-chain checks corroborated TPU V7 ramping in 2026 with V8 (2nm) taping out, and ~15% of TSMC's 2026 capacity going to Broadcom/Google-TPU + AMD (BofA best-ideas, 2026-04-09). [NVDA read](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_19_nvda_vivek_19_may_26.html) · [BofA](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_09_baml_best_ideas_9_apr_2026.html)
- **Google capped Meta's Gemini/Cloud usage on capacity constraints (UBS · Christina Dwyer sales note, citing FT, 2026-07-01):** Google reportedly **capped Meta's use of Gemini AI / Google Cloud** due to Google's own infrastructure capacity constraints — direct evidence the "compute-constrained" language above (cloud revenue "would have been higher" if supply allowed) is binding enough to limit a paying customer, and a data point for how tight the TPU/GPU supply chain is heading into 2H26 (UBS · Christina Dwyer, 2026-07-01).
- **Among the most capacity-constrained hyperscalers (FT, via MS TMT, 2026-07-06):** GOOGL reportedly told META it could **NOT offer all the Gemini capacity META wanted to buy** — a second, sharper framing of the July-1 UBS/FT read: GOOGL is among the **most capacity-constrained hyperscalers** (alongside Anthropic, MSFT and AMZN). Reinforces that Google's own compute is now the binding constraint on external cloud/Gemini demand heading into 2H26 (FT via MS TMT, 2026-07-06).
- **TPUv8i 'Broadfly' positioned to gain scale-up share vs NVDA (SemiAnalysis, via MS TMT, 2026-07-06):** SemiAnalysis argues NVDA's scale-up roadmap slips — **Kyber NVL144 pushed to 2028, NVL72x2 cancelled, and the CPO NVSwitch not landing until Feynman** — leave a gap that **GOOGL's TPUv8i 'Broadfly'** (and AMD's MI500X) can exploit to gain **scale-up advantages over NVDA's Rubin Ultra**. A concrete architecture/timing read that Google's TPU roadmap can press on NVDA's scale-up delays (SemiAnalysis via MS TMT, 2026-07-06).
- **Google–SpaceX compute-services deal — the SpaceX-side view (JPM "SpaceX — The Next Frontier", 2026-07-07):** in its SpaceX (SPCX) initiation, JPM notes **Google (alongside Anthropic and Reflection AI) has reportedly signed a compute-services deal with SpaceX/Starlink** — evidence that SpaceX's ability to deploy dense, large contiguous networked clusters is drawing hyperscaler demand. Crucially for the GOOG read-through, JPM estimates these third-party "neocloud" compute-services deals are **priced at a materially higher $/W than SpaceX's internal Grok monetization (~$3-5/W in 2026-27)** — i.e. Google is paying a premium for first-in-line terrestrial compute. This is the counterparty-side corroboration of the GOOG-side reads already on this page (Google pays SpaceX/xAI ~$920M/month ≈ ~$50/W per MS/CNBC 2026-06-30; BofA 2026-07-07 flags recent Anthropic/Google-SpaceX capacity deals at >$40bn/GW, up to ~$50bn/GW) — all pointing to how tight compute supply is and how much Google will pay to secure it (JPM · Doug Anmuth et al, 2026-07-07).
- **ASIC design-service chain "riding on Google" (Citi · Laura Chen, 2026-07-06):** Citi reads **Mediatek and GUC as "riding on Google"** for AI-ASIC growth into 2027 — a read-through that Google's custom-silicon program is a **key demand driver for the Taiwan design-service chain** (Citi raises Mediatek AI-ASIC revenue to US$18bn'27 / US$40bn'28; see [MEDIATEK.md](MEDIATEK.md)). Corroborates the merchant-TPU/dual-vendor thesis from the design-service supply side (Citi · Laura Chen, 2026-07-06).
- **Search relevancy strong — World Cup drove record query volume (Bernstein, 2026-07-09):** GOOGL Search relevancy remains strong — the **World Cup drove an all-time-high Search query volume**; Search regularly benefits from tentpole/live-sports events (positive for auction density / Search growth). (Bernstein, 2026-07-09)
- **SemiAnalysis "The Future of Meta Superintelligence" (2026-07-09) — a pointed *bear* read on Google's AI positioning ("loser mentality from Google"):** behind the paywall of its MSL progress update, SemiAnalysis delivers an independent negative take on Google — **all of it clearly SemiAnalysis's own opinion/estimate, not disclosed Google data.** It projects the **majority of Google's incremental datacenter capacity over the next 2 years goes to IaaS / 3p API** (not DeepMind), so **DeepMind ends up with less training compute than OpenAI, Anthropic *and* MSL**; and it believes the **$85B of equity Google issued for AI infra is mostly rented out to customers like Anthropic** — "actively enabling its fiercest competitors" ("if they believed a country-of-geniuses-in-a-datacenter was <3 years out, they'd funnel all compute to DeepMind instead"). Product: **Gemini 3.5 Flash** called "a benchmaxxed prop" performing far worse than GPT-5.6 / Opus 4.8 in real-world use; **3.5 Pro "not even Opus level on coding."** Google also **recently lost more RL talent to Anthropic** and its **RL efforts are "too decentralized."** Punchline: **"the race for 3rd is currently between Meta and SpaceX, not Google"** — advice: start having SWEs build RL tasks and redistribute much more compute to DeepMind immediately. **Verbatim framing added on the 2026-07-24 dedup pass (previously paraphrased only):** SemiAnalysis's opening frame is that frontier AI "has increasingly felt like **a two horse race between OpenAI vs Anthropic**. Google had a brief moment in the spotlight with **Gemini 3 Pro and Nano Banana, but they've since faded dramatically.** Despite their **Windsurf acquisition, they're far from a compelling agentic coding product**, and **3.5 Flash is a benchmaxxed prop that performs far worse than GPT 5.6 and Opus 4.8 in real world scenarios** (much less Fable and 5.6). **3.5 Pro is not even Opus level on coding**" — with Microsoft judged to have "completely blown their early lead with GitHub Copilot," Chinese labs "too compute poor," and Meta framed as the most plausible third entrant. **Log it as the harsh, independent, attributed view it is — this page does NOT adopt it.** It sits directly against what the page argues elsewhere: Gemini is the clear #2 and gaining on gen-AI web-traffic share (9.1%→27.8% over 12 months, @Similarweb 07-15), Gemini app hit 950M MAU with DAU tripling y/y and AI Mode 1B+ MAU (Q2'26 call), Google is the only lab shipping full any-to-any multimodal (@emollick 07-13), and the Q2 print delivered Cloud +82% with a $514B backlog. The part of the SemiAnalysis critique the Q2 call itself *conceded* is the narrow one: **the coding gap is acknowledged by management**, 3.5 Pro is still only "in testing," and Gemini 4 pretraining has only just begun — so treat the piece as a well-informed hostile read on **coding/agentic model quality and RL organisation**, not as a verdict on the franchise. Also from the same note, a supply-side datapoint relevant to Google's compute sourcing: **SpaceXAI is selling ~$26B a year of GPUs to Anthropic/Google** — the counterparty scale behind the ~$920M/month SpaceX rental already on this page. Sharpens the existing bear reads (coding lag — FundaAI 2026-06-24; GCP "EBTIT"/rented-margin — SemiAnalysis 2026-05-27; DeepMind brain-drain — Barron's 2026-06-29). (SemiAnalysis "The Future of Meta Superintelligence", 2026-07-09) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_META.html)
- **What Google paid SpaceX, and why it deliberately passed on selling that compute itself (SemiAnalysis "Meta Compute: Everyone Wants To Be A Cloud", 2026-07-02 — folded 2026-07-24):** the counterparty-side pricing of the SpaceX deal already on this page, from a database of hundreds of GPU cloud deals a year. SpaceX's **Google** deal is priced at **4x peers' revenue per MW** (the Anthropic deal at 3x) — and "**SpaceX's pricing on the Google deal is even higher than what we observe on the on-demand or short-term rental market**"; because the cost structure is roughly the same, the profit-per-MW gap is wider still. Structure: **3 years with a 90-day bilateral cancellation option — "effectively a 3-month deal with automatic renewal"** (which is exactly the mechanism MS/Nowak models when it assumes GOOGL terminates early after Dec-31, 07-23). The strategic read is the interesting half for this page: SemiAnalysis argues the financing burden excludes every neocloud from that premium short-tenor segment, and that **all three top hyperscalers could have served it but passed because each had a higher-value long-term option — for Google, "TPUs and Vertex (now Gemini Enterprise Platform)"** (Microsoft: OpenAI IP for equity + compute; Amazon: Bedrock/Trainium adoption), leaving "only two companies that can truly capitalize on this new market: Oracle and Meta." So the same fact pattern that makes Google a **premium payer** of ~$50/W to SpaceX is framed as a deliberate choice to monetise gigawatts through the higher-value TPU-aaS/Vertex stack instead — a direct third-party corroboration of the merchant-TPU thesis Barclays modelled on 07-24, and the reason the SPCX opex drag (~$1bn/$3bn 3Q/4Q per MS) should be read as a bridge cost rather than a strategic concession. (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-02) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_02_ENC_Meta_Compute_Everyone_Wants_To_Be_A_Cloud_email.html)
- **Google joins the hyperscaler shift from cashflow funding to debt (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop Unleashes the AI Project Trinity", 2026-07-06 — folded 2026-07-24):** a one-line but well-timed third-party marker on the capital-structure turn: "up to now the majority of AI buildouts have been primarily cashflow funded by the hyperscalers such as Google, Amazon, Meta, Microsoft, Oracle. Over the last year, that's started to turn with **Oracle then Meta, and now even Google turning to debt**." Dated three weeks before the Q2'26 print at which LT debt printed **$98.2B (from ~$16B y/y)** and FCF went **−$5.9B** — i.e. the external observation and the filing agree. The framing around it is the constructive side of the same coin: SemiAnalysis models AI debt financing scaling to a **~$7.1T outstanding credit market by 2029** (second only to the ~$13T US MBS market) against annual AI capex **>$2T in 2028**, with the binding constraint moving 2025 datacenter capacity → early-2026 chip production → **mid-2026 financing**. Read against the on-page bear cluster (@Hedgeye's ~$700bn big-5 AI debt, @zerohedge's "GOOGL 56s the most shorted IG bond," MS modelling an **$80bn debt raise by end-'27**): the debt is real and rising, but the market being tapped is projected to grow into it. (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-06) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_06_ENC_Nvidia_GPU_Debt_Backstop_Unleashes_the_AI_Project_Trinity_Capital_Offtake_and_Datacenters_email.html)
- **Networking philosophy + supply-chain credibility — a mild relative positive (SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21 — folded 2026-07-24):** in a piece about Meta's over-engineering, Google is the vertically-integrated benchmark: **"Google treats networking as a software problem, and builds its own transport rather than buying one"** (versus Amazon avoiding RoCE entirely with EFA on commodity Tomahawk silicon, Oracle "building large, functional networks out of simpler hardware," and Meta which "paid for best-in-class, and then the definition of best-in-class changed"). The read-through worth holding: because Meta's frequent design pivots and cancelled design wins have eroded supplier trust, **"some suppliers favor focusing on Amazon or Google designs due to Meta's frequent reshuffling"** — i.e. Google's roadmap stability is now a competitive advantage in securing scarce supply-chain prioritisation, which matters directly for the TPU ramp (Broadcom/MediaTek, CoWoS allocation, HBM4) laid out in the TPU section. Small, but it is the supply-side complement to the demand-side TPU bull. (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-21) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_21_ENC_Metas_Infrastructure_Team_Needs_A_Culture_Reset_email.html)
- **JPM US Internet 2Q26 Preview — reiterates OW, Dec-26 PT $460, GOOG/L top pick #1 (JPM · Anmuth "US Internet 2Q26 Preview", 2026-07-14):** JPMe 2Q26 segment growth Search **+16.6%** / YT Ads **+8.6%** (below buyside +10%) / **Cloud +70.9%** (vs cons +64.2%, buyside 70%+); FY26/27/28 revenue **$492.7B / $604.5B / $735.1B**, GAAP EPS **$14.05 / $14.34 / $17.31** (buyside FY27 bar $15.00+); GAAP op-margin ~34.7% FY26. - **JPM 2Q preview call — Google still Anmuth's "favorite name overall" (JPM · Anmuth 2Q preview call, 2026-07-14):** the AI-monetization proof is by segment — **Search can grow ~17%, Cloud "at least 70" (70-75%) and exit the year in the 80s** (some buyside toward 90), with the backlog a key line (the big Anthropic deal drove the 2Q surge). On the creeping "Gemini is falling behind" view: acknowledges a model-cadence lag but expects **Gemini 3.5 Pro shortly**; on search-disintermediation, "the discussion isn't dead" but is on "life support" — the AI-mode transition is being managed well, and **there are still no ads in Gemini (a lever Google can pull)**. - **MS "Hyperscale capex to $1.4T" podcast — Google adds the MOST compute (Morgan Stanley · Nowak "Hyperscale capex to $1.4T", 2026-07-14):** Nowak sizes Google's footprint **~5 GW (2025) → over 30 GW by 2028** — the most capacity added of any hyperscaler — increasingly TPU-weighted (cost edge: **TPUs ~20s/watt vs GPUs heading to ~$50/watt**). ~10 GW+ of TPU seen in 2028 (Broadcom + MediaTek triangulation), of which MS assumes Google **sells ~3 GW externally** and runs the rest on-prem. Medium-term Nowak **leans Amazon over Google**: Cloud already modeled +70%+ (less upside vs where the buy-side sits) and Google carries **more compute-capacity-constraint risk** (the ~$50/watt SpaceX rental deal is the tell), so a tactical Cloud/Search miss could hit the stock. - **Archera expert call — Google's TPU "well" is the differentiated inference capacity (Archera expert call, 2026-07-13):** an ex-AWS operator's channel read — because of the breadth/depth of deployed TPUs and the models they support, **Google has been "one click ahead" on inference-SKU availability** despite likely fewer GPUs than Amazon net-net, and **the majority of Claude production PTU tokens have (as of start-2026) been sold through Google Vertex** (Google's TPU well hasn't run out even for leading-edge Opus/Fable models that look "too big to serve" out of Amazon's back end). Serving production Vertex demand off TPU leaves Google's GPU fleet unencumbered. Also: Google raised H200 list pricing at the start of the quarter and moved to one-year commitments; is reworking cloud from partner-led to a direct model (squeezing partner margin, Amazon-style); and Axion is less widely deployed than AWS Graviton, leaving Google **more exposed to rising RAM pricing** than Amazon. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_13_archera_13_jul_26.html)
- **GCP go-to-market still the enterprise laggard — net-new from the raw transcript (Jefferies-hosted cloud-expert call · "Aran K", 2026-07-13, the transcript behind the Archera read above):** color not previously logged — building an enterprise sales motion has been GCP's persistent weakness ("Google went through this in GCP… it took them forever… still a disaster compared to the other two"), so GCP still lacks the big-bank multi-year-software relationships AWS/Azure have; both **Google and Amazon are prioritizing inference capacity over accelerating Rubin deployment** (both see inference as the bigger near-term opportunity, unlike neoclouds racing to deploy Rubin first); startup GPU rental rates are **up ~30-50% vs Q4**.
