- **TPU go-to-market: pitching against Nvidia-native clouds; Nvidia counters with financing (@theinformation, 2026-07-13):** The Information reports **Google is pitching TPUs as a cheaper, more reliable alternative to Nvidia GPUs and targeting cloud providers built around Nvidia hardware**; Nvidia is responding with **financial guarantees that could help those clouds fund new data centers** — a sharpening of the merchant-TPU competitive push and NVDA's defensive response (@theinformation, 2026-07-13).
- **TPU v10 "Icefish" I/O die → Samsung 2nm; compute on TSMC 1.4nm (@jukan05, 2026-07-15):** Samsung Electronics is **considering outsourcing the back-end design of the I/O die for Google's 10th-gen 2nm TPU codenamed "Icefish"** (surveying DSPs ADTechnology/Gaonchips/Alphachips amid a 2nm staffing crunch). Detail: Icefish is **co-designed with MediaTek, MP as early as 2028**; the **compute die goes to TSMC 1.4nm while the I/O die (compute↔HBM data transfer) goes to Samsung 2nm** — the first concrete split showing Samsung foundry inside the TPU BOM. Sharpens the existing "Samsung foundry engagement for TPU I/O die / TPU v10 Icefish" catalyst (Rothschild, 2026-06-18) (@jukan05, 2026-07-15).
- **Green-power procurement scaling (@TheTranscript_, 2026-07-14):** Google signed a **fixed-price virtual PPA for 100% of the initial output of Arkansas's Steel River Energy Center — its largest US solar-plus-storage project** — incremental evidence of the power-procurement build-out backing the capex ramp (@TheTranscript_, 2026-07-14).

- **The financing structure behind the TPU push, quantified — $44bn of off-balance-sheet data-center lease guarantees, up from $6.5bn nine months ago, plus equity warrants in developer deals (The Information · Dakin Campbell, "How Google Is Using Wall Street Financing Techniques to Expand Chip Sales," 2026-07-26):** the most concrete number yet on the mechanism this page has been circling since the "bridge capacity" and "$50bn/GW vs $16bn/GW" debate opened on 07-24. Google **disclosed in the Q2'26 filing week that it has agreed to cover as much as $44 billion of lease payments on data centers owned by others if the tenant defaults** — a figure that "has risen from **$6.5 billion as of the end of September**," i.e. a **~6.8x step-up in roughly three quarters**. The Information's read on why: the guarantees "reflect Google's efforts to **supply Anthropic and others with its alternative to Nvidia's AI chips**" — Google is underwriting third parties' lease obligations so those third parties can build the shells that host Google TPUs, and it takes **equity warrants in some data center developer deals** in exchange. Three things this changes on the page: (1) it gives the merchant-TPU thesis (Barclays/Sandler's 07-24 external-TPU revenue model) an explicit **credit-support cost** that does not appear in capex or on the balance sheet as debt; (2) it is the same customer-concentration exposure the buy-side is already pricing through 2028 EPS — [[ANTHROPIC]] is named as the beneficiary, so a contingent-liability channel now sits *alongside* the revenue-durability question the UBS desk flagged 07-24 ("2028 EPS all over the map… some as low as $14"); (3) the **warrants** are the first evidence Google is being paid in equity optionality for the risk, which cuts the other way. Logged as disclosure + press interpretation — the **$44bn and $6.5bn are Google's own disclosed figures**; the "to sell TPUs" motive is The Information's attribution, not a Google statement. (The Information, 2026-07-26)
- **🆕 Google Cloud read-through from the Microsoft preview — what the +82% was actually made of, and Google as the Street's cautionary tale on capex (UBS · Karl Keirstead, "Microsoft Corp. — Update on Azure, Capex and M365", 2026-07-26).** A Microsoft note, but the Google datapoints in it are specific and worth carrying. **(1) The composition of the Cloud beat.** UBS's structural argument is that hyperscalers benefit materially "from their role as front-ends to the models and 1P products (such as Claude Code) from OpenAI and Anthropic," and it names Google as the clearest evidence: "**This was very evident in the 2Q26 results from Google Cloud, with revs growth of +82% boosted by core demand but also by the sale of Gemini model access, Gemini Enterprise subscriptions and even TPU hardware sales.**" That is a *four-part* attribution of the +82% — core demand, model-API access, Gemini Enterprise subscriptions, and TPU hardware — and it sits directly on the page's open "how much of the beat was core vs TPU" debate (mgmt: TPU impact "fairly small"; Capstone estimate ~$1.2-1.3B, ~5% of Cloud). **(2) A datapoint against the token-deflation bear, with a caveat UBS itself supplies.** On whether token optimization and open-model routing are already denting API revenue growth: "**This was certainly not evident in Google Cloud's 2Q26 results (growth accelerated to +82%) but much of the excessive enterprise token spend appears to be in coding use cases for which Gemini models are less dominant.**" So the acceleration is real evidence *against* the drag — but UBS argues Google may simply be under-exposed to the workload where the optimization is happening, which is the same coding gap management conceded on the call. **(3) The Street is punishing capex signals, and Google is the named precedent.** UBS: "We're eyes-wide-open to the reality that the **Street is not rewarding the stocks of AI infra providers that signal higher capex spend** and/or don't post upside to cloud revs growth and margins (**see Oracle and Google**)"; investors are nervous Microsoft "may need to inch up capex estimates again, **much like Google did on its call this past week**"; and on the set-up, "this was true into both the **recent Google and Oracle prints and capex raises from both were not greeted with applause**." **(4) The equity raise, priced as the differentiator.** In arguing Microsoft is unlikely to issue equity: "**The Google and Oracle equity transactions** have raised questions as to whether Microsoft may be forced to raise external capital… **Google did so at what was an all-time share price high.** Microsoft shares are almost 30% off its highs" — i.e. UBS reads Google's June raise as opportunistically well-timed, a mildly constructive framing of the same event the page carries as a dilution overhang. **(5) Customer/partner checks naming Google.** A Microsoft partner: "**We're really pushing Google to diversify against Microsoft**… Microsoft made some painful changes to us on EA rebates that lost us pure margin. Second, **Google has come out with some really attractive incentives.**" A customer: "Azure spend will contract 5-10% driven by the shift away from M365 and OpenAI. **Google is increasing 20% per year because of Workspace but also their simulation tools, TPUs, and algorithms.**" UBS also cites "strong overall hyperscaler demand signals (**including from the Google Cloud results**) for both CPU as well as GPU-based workloads." **(6) The two-sided frontier-lab framing.** UBS notes the consensus view that "an AI model market dominated by three frontier lab giants (**Anthropic, OpenAI and Google**) creates risk for the hyperscalers, with too much economic power concentrated in too few customers" — and that Satya Nadella's intra-quarter blog flurry is a deliberate "resistance to the growing control/power of a few frontier AI model providers (Anthropic, OpenAI and Google)." Google is the only name on both sides of that trade: a disintermediation threat to third-party hyperscalers *and* itself a hyperscaler materially monetizing model-API access. (UBS · Karl Keirstead, 2026-07-26) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ued48088.html)

- **Jeff Dean on agent capability and the next ML-development loop — direct, qualitative, and excerpt-only.** Dean said agent-based systems are now capable on longer-running coding tasks and that calling them roughly “junior engineer” level is “pretty spot on,” depending on the definition. He said the ability to handle more complex work has improved faster than he expected and is beginning to extend beyond coding. His 2027 prediction is more automation of ML development itself: systems running many experiments and decomposing work into subproblems to improve their own capabilities. **Scope caveat:** the user-supplied transcript ends mid-sentence at 1:51, while the published episode runs 57 minutes; this records only the supplied excerpt and should not be read as a summary of the full interview. (Jeff Dean, interviewed by Diana Hu, Y Combinator Startup Podcast, “The 1% Rule for Building in AI,” published 2026-08-01) [Excerpt](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_01_Y_Combinator_Jeff_Dean_1_Percent_Rule_excerpt.html)

- **🆕 The self-improvement datapoints — AlphaEvolve is the cleanest disclosed instance anywhere of AI optimising the infrastructure that trains AI, and Google's AI-written-code share has tripled in eighteen months (FUNDA "Deep|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05).** **(1) AlphaEvolve: Google used Gemini to evolve algorithms, "speeding up a key kernel in Gemini's own training by 23% and continuously RECOVERING 0.7% OF GOOGLE'S GLOBAL COMPUTE."** ⚠️ **Read the 0.7% literally and carefully — it is a continuous, compounding recovery of Google's *entire* fleet, not a one-off saving on one training run, and it is the only quantified example on this page of compute being manufactured by software rather than bought. Against a FY26 capex guide of $195-205B, 0.7% of fleet is a real number expressed as an efficiency line nobody is modelling.** FUNDA's use of it is the structural one, and it is the argument that matters for this page's capex debate: it is evidence that the *algorithmic* input to AI R&D is becoming **endogenous to compute** — "AlphaEvolve-style systems suggest algorithmic improvement can itself be produced at scale by compute and models together," which is one leg of FUNDA's claim that RSI systematically reduces the scarcity of every input except compute. **(2) AI-written code at Google is now 75% of new code — a figure that moved 25% → 50% → 75% over eighteen months.** ⚠️ **The series is the datapoint, not the level: three prints on the same disclosed metric, doubling then compounding, is a rate-of-change read on internal R&D velocity that no external model has. Context FUNDA supplies for calibration: Anthropic's company-wide figure is 70-90% and frontline-engineer accounts at both Anthropic and OpenAI put it near 100%, so Google is inside the pack rather than ahead of it — this is not a competitive-differentiation datapoint, it is an industry-condition one.** **(3) The compute-commitment side, and it is a number this page has been circling without: FUNDA cites the Anthropic-Google compute partnership at "UP TO ONE MILLION TPUs" as one of its examples of multi-track commitments that do NOT pencil on a current-ARR payback calculation.** ⚠️ **This page carries the same relationship in GW (~4GW of Anthropic TPU orders, expert callback 04-30), in dollars (~$40bn commitment, JPM 06-16; $21.1bn of non-marketable purchases + a $20bn equity-derivative notional, Q2'26 10-Q) and in backlog (~$85bn entering 1Q26) — the unit count is new and should be cross-checked against the GW figure before either is used in a model, since 1mn TPUs at v7/v8 class power is materially more than 4GW. Logged as FUNDA's citation, not adopted as a house number.** (FUNDA "Deep|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Funda_on_RSI.html)

## TPU / merchant-silicon (Asia supply-chain, Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17)
- **Volumes inflecting:** total TPU production ~3.1-3.2mn units in 2026 (some checks as high as 4mn, viewed as a stretch), potentially **doubling to 5-6mn in 2027** — ~5mn from Broadcom + ~1mn from MediaTek. With AWS Trainium, TPU is one of the two dominant merchant ASICs; total ASIC + ASIC-server units both seen +50%+.
- **CoWoS allocation reset higher** for TPU 2027 projects (TSMC AP7/AP8 fully loaded); slot estimates raised to ~130k from a prior 110k. Broadcom 2nm TPUs cited as a large 2027 TSMC driver.
- **v8 generation:** "Zebrafish"/"Sunfish" v8 on 2nm, larger than Ironwood (v7), pricing roughly **$23-34K** (≈2x prior gen). MediaTek's v8e ("Zebrafish") taped out with good performance; MediaTek reiterates ~$1bn 2026 TPU revenue, ~1mn units across 2026-27 (~15% of 2027 TPU shipments), ~$4K dollar-content/chip; follow-on v9 work with Google still early-stage. EMC gaining CCL share toward 50-70% on V6/V7.
- **Packaging:** Google evaluating Intel **EMIB-T** for TPU v9, citing CoWoS capacity constraints and power-delivery advantages in the HBM4 era (more visible EMIB ramp 2H26).
- **HBM procurement shifting Broadcom→MediaTek (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23):** in prior TPU generations Google bought only conventional DRAM itself and **relied entirely on Broadcom for HBM** procurement; now **MediaTek (TPU V8/V9) is increasingly handling Google's HBM directly** and is in talks with Samsung on HBM3E/HBM4E. Google **attempted to procure HBM directly itself but found it too challenging**, so for the next ~3 years HBM procurement stays split between **Broadcom and MediaTek** (the cited ~20% Google figure includes both, all flowing to Google). The driver is cost: Broadcom charges Google for HBM in the BOM, MediaTek does not — pushing share toward MediaTek (Conor corroborates: rising HBM cost is "the key reason" Google wants MediaTek) (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23; Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
- **Memory pull:** Google's 2026 HBM + DRAM demand reportedly **double** prior plan; vendors flag TPU demand "much stronger than original plan," with Google repeatedly raising forecasts — a key reason memory checks revised 2026 ASIC+GPU growth to ~40% (from 25-30%), though prior cycles saw later downward revisions (a risk flag).
- **Rack-level build-out:** Hon Hai (Foxconn) "aggressively" expanding US capacity for Google, moving from L6 components to full L10-11 compute trays/racks; Google also adopting BBU in next TPU server and shifting toward ±400VDC/HVDC power architecture (OCP).
- **TPU unit forecast raised (UBS · Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01):** Google TPU chipset units raised to **4.1mn in 2026, rising to 9.0mn in 2027**; MediaTek expected to support **4mn units of TPU v8t capacity in 2027** — a higher unit trajectory than the earlier Jefferies Asia-supply-chain read above (~3.1-3.2mn 2026 → 5-6mn 2027), both retained as distinct source estimates (UBS · Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01).
- **HBM-equivalent TPU unit forecast raised (UBS "Memory Semis Monthly" · Nicolas Gaudois, 2026-07-03):** in raising its HBM industry demand forecast, UBS revises its **HBM-equivalent Google TPU unit estimate to 4.2m units for 2026E and 9.1m units for 2027E (2027 raised)** — part of tweaking up HBM procurement for Google alongside AMD/AWS. Sits just above the UBS · Sunny Lin (07-01) figure of 4.1mn 2026 → 9.0mn 2027 (a different UBS note/analyst) and well above the earlier Jefferies Asia-supply-chain read (~3.1-3.2mn 2026 → 5-6mn 2027) — all retained as distinct source estimates. (UBS "Memory Semis Monthly", 2026-07-03)
- **TPU V9 packaging — EMIB-T dual-source intent, gated on Intel yield (Fubon "TSMC — Expecting GM upside going forward", 2026-07-08):** Fubon's checks confirm **Google initially planned to use Intel's EMIB-T for TPU V9 production in late-2027/2028** — i.e. dual-sourcing TPU packaging beyond TSMC CoWoS — and **asked Intel to lift EMIB-T yield to 95%+ before production**. Fubon sees some EMIB-T yield improvement but judges hitting 95%+ by mid-2027 "very challenging," so it thinks Google **ultimately relies on TSMC's 2028 CoWoS expansion** for TPU V9. Corroborates the EMIB-T-for-v9 evaluation already on this page (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17) and sharpens it with a yield bar + a fallback-to-TSMC conclusion (Fubon, 2026-07-08). ⚠️ **_SUPERSEDED BY FUBON ITSELF ON 2026-07-28 — the fallback-to-TSMC conclusion is reversed: "it is difficult to reach Google's target with TSMC alone, and Intel's supply is a must by 2028." Original 07-08 text retained above as the dated prior mark; see the 07-28 bullet below and the Changelog._**
- **🔄 FUBON REVERSES ITSELF ON TPU V9 PACKAGING — "Intel's supply is a MUST by 2028," and the reason is a four-compute-die V9 that more than doubles capacity consumption (Fubon "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates", Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang, 2026-07-28):** twenty days after concluding that Google would fall back on TSMC's 2028 CoWoS expansion, **the same house now says the opposite**, and the reversal is driven by demand, not by an improved yield view. Fubon's checks: "**Despite market concerns on Google's capex and negative FCF, we see Google has a very aggressive target in 2028 to deploy its in-house design TPUs. Based on our checks, Google plans to have 12-15mn TPUs in 2028. Entering 2028, Google's TPUs will enter the V9 generation with four compute dies, suggesting that their capacity consumption will more than double in 2028 vs. 2027… we think it is difficult to reach Google's target with TSMC alone, and Intel's supply is a must by 2028.**" **The mechanism is the part to carry: it is not that EMIB-T got better, it is that a four-compute-die V9 more than doubles packaging-capacity consumption per unit, so the volume simply does not fit inside TSMC's book.** On Intel's side Fubon is now constructive on capacity if not on the learning curve: "**although there is still a lot of noise on Intel's EMIB-T progress, especially its learning curve improvement, we see Intel becoming more positive in capacity expansion**" — **EMIB capacity ~10-12k/month by end-2026, planned to double to 24-25k/month by end-2027**, with Intel's CFO flagging that a great portion of the announced capex increase is earmarked for advanced packaging such as EMIB-T (Ibiden's upcoming earnings call and any capex raise named as the next tell). Fubon's TSMC baseline for context: **CoWoS capacity 180kwpm by end-2027 and 220kwpm by end-2028** (with potential upside as TSMC slows SoIC to prioritise CoWoS; the 4Q28 SoIC forecast cut from 90k to 70k), and ASE FoCoS raised to 50kwpm. ⚠️ **What this does to the three-way v9 disagreement already on this page: it becomes 2-vs-1 in favour of EMIB-T.** Fubon has moved from the CoWoS camp to the Intel camp, joining **UBS Asia (07-27 — v9 is Intel's flagship first major external EMIB-T product, ~90% packaging / ~50% substrate yields on MediaTek v9 prototype runs, Ibiden capacity the gate)**, and leaving **JPM · Gokul (07-08 — "the next-version TPU is NOT using SOIC, it stays on CoWoS," packaged by Qualcomm or MediaTek)** as the remaining CoWoS holdout; **UBS's own semis desk (Arcuri SemiBytes, 07-27) still calls EMIB-T "a process we think still suffers from very low yields and immaturity."** The divergence is narrower but **still not resolved, and no view is adopted** — note that Fubon's reversal reaches the EMIB-T conclusion from the *demand* side while explicitly conceding the yield/learning-curve doubt it raised on 07-08 has not been answered. ⚠️ **Basis flag on the 12-15mn figure:** this page already carries "**12-15 million TPUs in 2028 on a chip/die basis ('based on chips')**" from Jefferies · Sherman Chang's 07-17 post-TSMC call with Fubon — the same number, from the same house, now restated as "**12-15mn TPUs**" alongside a V9 that carries **four compute dies per package**. Those two readings are not reconcilable at face value (12-15mn *dies* implies ~3-4mn V9 packages; 12-15mn *packages* implies 48-60mn dies), and the page keeps both verbatim rather than picking one. For calibration, UBS's 07-27 EMIB-T model has **2.3mn Google chipsets in 2028** at four XPUs per package — i.e. an Intel-only slice consistent with the *package* count being in the low single-digit millions. **Also worth noting against the sell-side's own framing on this page: Fubon calls the 2028 target "very aggressive" *despite* — explicitly — "market concerns on Google capex and negative FCF," i.e. the supply chain is being asked to build for a plan the equity market is currently penalising** (GOOGL -7.8% w/w on the first negative-FCF quarter). Stock-pick coda from the same note: Fubon is "more confident in the CPU supply chain, SPE supply chain, as well as **TPU's very strong momentum in the following years**," with **MediaTek its top TPU pick**. (Fubon, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html)
- **ASIC revenue guide / architecture detail (JPMorgan · Gokul Hariharan, MediaTek note, 2026-07-01):** Google has guided **US$7-12bn of 2027 ASIC/TPU revenue**, implying **~10-15% share of a US$70-80bn 2027 ASIC TAM**. Google is still using a **semi-Chain-of-Thought (semi-CoT) model for TPU v10** (not a full-CoT design) and is **not embracing CPO (co-packaged optics) packaging** for TPUs in the v10 generation (JPMorgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-01).
- **TPU v8 volume update — "Sunfish" ~960k FY, shipments 4Q-weighted (Morgan Stanley · Charlie Chan, 2026-07-08):** MS's TPU tracking has **"Sunfish" full-year volume ~960k units** with **shipments largely in 4Q (slightly pushed out)**, while **"Zebrafish" 4Q26 volume ramp is unchanged** — a modest timing slip on Sunfish against an intact Zebrafish ramp. (Morgan Stanley · Charlie Chan, 2026-07-08)
- **Broadcom-side TPU read — partnership intact, "V9" a Taiwan-PR label, A8 "still in the Fab" (JPMorgan · Harlan Sur "Hock Tan @ JPM", 2026-07-07):** on the Hock Tan call, Broadcom framed its **5-yr Google TPU partnership as fully on track and intact**, and confirmed it expects to **retain the majority TPU volume share** through the agreement. Hock pushed back on the "V9" nomenclature — **"we don't call it V9…it's cooked up by the PR agency in Taiwan [for] MediaTek"** — Broadcom internally calls it only "next-gen to V8" and lets Google name it publicly. On the competing **Google-COT / MediaTek A8 TPU**, Hock: "Last I heard it's **still in the Fab**." A supplier-side counter to the aggressive Taiwan MediaTek-share checks — read alongside the MediaTek-side bull reads elsewhere in the complex (Broadcom is talking its own book). (JPMorgan · Harlan Sur "Hock Tan @ JPM", 2026-07-07)
- **TPU demand accelerating — Google asking aggressively for wafer + CoWoS (Goldman Sachs "TSMC preview", 2026-07-09):** GS flags **strong Google TPU demand acceleration** — over the **past ~3 months Google has been asking aggressively for wafer capacity and CoWoS allocation** at TSMC, a key driver of GS raising its 2027/28 TSMC capex/CoWoS numbers. On the back of it GS **raised its MediaTek Google-TPU ASIC revenue estimates** (2027E to ~US$20bn; see [MEDIATEK.md](MEDIATEK.md)) — i.e. Google's own accelerating TPU pull is the demand engine behind the higher merchant-ASIC marks across the Taiwan design-service chain. (Goldman Sachs "TSMC preview", 2026-07-09)
- **Next-gen TPU packaging = CoWoS (not SOIC), Qualcomm/MediaTek packaged (JPMorgan · Gokul "TSMC 2Q26 preview", 2026-07-08):** Gokul's TSMC 2Q26 preview confirms **Google's next-version TPU is NOT using SOIC — it stays on CoWoS**, packaged by **Qualcomm or MediaTek** (depending on the vendor). Separately, the **in-house ARM CPUs from Google, Amazon and Microsoft are still on simple BGA packaging** today, with a chance follow-on generations move to a CoWoS architecture later — a read-through for Google's Axion CPU packaging roadmap and consistent with the EMIB-T-vs-CoWoS debate already on the page. (JPMorgan · Gokul "TSMC 2Q26 preview", 2026-07-08)
- **🆕 TPU v9 packaging — the THIRD view, and it goes the other way: UBS has EMIB-T winning v9 (UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Timothy Arcuri et al, "Can Intel challenge TSMC's advanced packaging leadership? — Intel's EMIB-T vs TSMC's CoPoS", 2026-07-27).** UBS's advanced-packaging deep-dive makes **Google TPU v9, developed by MediaTek, the flagship first major EXTERNAL EMIB-T product** — "we expect Intel to be selective in client engagement, prioritizing internal products and Google TPU v9, which is being developed by MediaTek and set for mass production in 2028" — and adds that **"Intel should prioritize focus on ramping up the key external products like Google TPUs in 2028-29 at its own facilities with vertical integration to ensure delivery."** _Note UBS carries two dates in the same note: EMIB-T "is slated for mass production in late 2027, with Google's TPU v9 expected to be its first major external product," versus v9 "set for mass production in 2028" in the stock-implications section — both retained verbatim, not reconciled._ **The yield picture, which is the load-bearing part:** UBS's industry checks, explicitly **"based on trial production runs of MediaTek's TPU v9 prototype,"** put **EMIB-T packaging yields at ~90%** (headline: **"~90-92% validation yields"**) but **EMIB-T "substrate" fabrication yields at only ~50%, vs >80% for HPC ABF substrates** — and UBS states that for HPC products "packaging yields ideally should improve to the high 90% range, considering the substantial value of the compute chips and HBMs being integrated," with experts believing yields "may not be fully optimised yet even with the initial production ramp slated to begin in late 2027-28." **The offset UBS leans on is capacity, not yield:** Ibiden's **¥220bn Gama plant (announced Feb-2026), EMIB-T mass production late-2027 with a meaningful ramp in 2028** — and "**our checks suggest that demand from Google TPU programs alone could largely absorb the planned capacity, increasing the likelihood of additional capex announcements as demand visibility improves.**" Ibiden is the primary EMIB-T substrate supplier, Unimicron a potential second source if volumes exceed expectations (its EMIB-T "yields remain below optimal levels"). **Sizing (UBS Figures 5/16, the stress-test scenario) — read the axis carefully:** the "Google TPU" line is **Intel EMIB-T packaging revenue attributable to Google TPU, not TPU revenue**, and it starts in 2028: **US$5,003mn (2028E) / US$8,374mn (2029E) / US$13,159mn (2030E)** on **2.3mn / 3.5mn / 5.0mn units at EMIB-T content of US$2,175 / US$2,393 / US$2,632 per unit** (the rows tie exactly: 2.3 × $2,175 = $5,003). The unit basis is **chipsets (packages), not dies** — UBS's 2030 total of 6.0mn units (5.0mn Google + 1.0mn others) is the same "~6mn chipsets… with four XPUs per package ≈ 24mn AI accelerator dies" the figure header describes. In UBS's **lower case (Figure 6/17, stronger EMIB-T success)** the Google TPU line runs **$5,003 / $10,766 / $17,106mn on 2.3 / 4.5 / 6.5mn chipsets**. **And the durability caveat, which matters as much as the win: "TSMC's share could further increase if Google's new TPUs (eg. v10) return to CoPoS and adopt CPO integration"** — i.e. UBS itself frames the EMIB-T win as potentially **one generation, not structural**, and notes CoPoS should remain "the preferred platform for customers prioritizing the highest performance and execution reliability." ⚠️ **This page now carries THREE incompatible views of TPU v9 packaging and the divergence is deliberately left open** — (i) **Fubon (2026-07-08)**: Google asked Intel for 95%+ EMIB-T yield, "very challenging" by mid-2027 → Google **falls back to TSMC 2028 CoWoS**; (ii) **JPMorgan · Gokul (2026-07-08)**: the next-version TPU is **not on SOIC, it stays on CoWoS**, packaged by Qualcomm or MediaTek; (iii) **UBS (2026-07-27)**: v9 **is** the flagship external EMIB-T product, with ~90% packaging yield already demonstrated on MediaTek's v9 prototype and Ibiden capacity, not yield, the gating item. Note also UBS's own semis desk sits on the opposite side of its Asia team — **Arcuri's 07-27 SemiBytes calls EMIB-T "a process we think still suffers from very low yields and immaturity"** (intra-quarter row, same date). No view is adopted; the resolution is a live watch item into the v9 production decision. (UBS, 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_on_adv_packaging.html)
- **AVGO stays the majority TPU supplier ~80%, MediaTek "real but not disruptive" (Morgan Stanley, via @firstadopter, 2026-07-14):** MS pushes back on the aggressive MediaTek-share checks — "**MediaTek participation is real, but not disruptive: AVGO should remain the majority TPU supplier over time, with ~80% share**," and calls the bearish 50%-share/eventual-displacement calls "premature." A supply-split counterweight to the Taiwan MediaTek-share bulls, aligned with Broadcom's own Hock-Tan framing (07-07) (Morgan Stanley via @firstadopter, 2026-07-14).
- **"Very aggressive" 2028 TPU target — 12-15M chips, split Broadcom + MediaTek (Jefferies · Sherman Chang "Update with Fubon post-TSMC 2Q earnings", 2026-07-17):** per Jefferies supply-chain checks (a post-TSMC-2Q-earnings call with the Jefferies TSMC analyst + Fubon), **Google plans to ship 12-15 million TPUs in 2028 on a chip/die basis** ("based on chips") — described as a "very aggressive TPU target." The TPU program is split between **MediaTek and Broadcom** as ASIC partners (the exact vendor mix was asked but the answer was truncated in the source — both are involved, mix unspecified). Context: strong server-CPU + custom-ASIC demand, with custom ASICs consuming TSMC CoWoS-L capacity. (Jefferies · Sherman Chang "Update with Fubon post-TSMC 2Q earnings", 2026-07-17)
- **Vertical-integration edge quantified by Google's own architect (Google Principal Architect / Office of the CTO Arthur Khessin, expert call, 2026-06-23):** Google allocates **100% of its acquired Nvidia GPUs to GCP external customers** and runs **all internal services (Search / YouTube / Gmail / Gemini) exclusively on its own TPUs** — an "incredible distinction" vs MSFT (GPUs → OpenAI + internal) and AWS (GPUs → services, Trainium → Anthropic). Networking + AI accelerators together are **60%+ of total data-center capex**, and Google's in-house advantage in both lets it "dictate prices" and capture higher margin; the shift to token-based managed-AI consumption (Gemini API) abstracts the infra and lets Google monetize its cost-efficient TPUs at better margin ("a much, much better business model"). Corroborates the existing "~80-90% of TPU inference is for Google's own properties" read (BofA/Arya, 2026-05-19). Industry framing from the same call: a **1GW AI DC campus costs $30-40bn** (>50% / ~$20bn compute, 30-35% facility); the mix shifts from ~80% training to **~80% inference within ~3 years**; hyperscalers are compressing the hardware-refresh cycle from 4-5yr to **~2yr** on power-efficiency gains; **HBM is a "huge cost driver"** (SK Hynix/Samsung/Micron triopoly), liquid cooling now mandatory, scale-up optics (LPO/CPO) an emerging cost; and physical bottlenecks (electrical grid/gear on 7-9yr timelines, no major grid relief before 2028-29, skilled labor) may **temper aggressive 2027-28 buyside capex deployment**. (Arthur Khessin expert call, 2026-06-23). [The AWS-side Trainium read-through from this same call is on the AMZN page.]
- **Optical/DSP supply read-through (BTG-hosted LightCounting · Bob Wheeler, 2026-06-25):** Google is the **primary near-term user of Marvell's 1.6T DSPs** — near-term 1.6T DSP demand is split between Google (via Marvell) and NVIDIA (internal DSPs), leaving Broadcom with less immediate 1.6T share despite a competitive product (Broadcom expected to gain share longer-term). A supply-side datapoint on Google's in-house networking stack (which Khessin, above, cited as a cost edge). (BTG-hosted LightCounting · Bob Wheeler, 2026-06-25)
- **TPU-as-a-Service / merchant TPU sales sized — the reference on external TPU sales (Barclays · U.S. Internet, "Unpacking TPU-As-A-Service", 2026-07-24, OW):** Barclays models Google selling TPUs **directly to customers as a merchant vendor** through off-GCP SPVs — "TPU-aaS" — via two vehicles: (1) the **Blackstone-Google JV** (0.5 GW online '27, run by ex-Google infra exec Ben Treynor Sloss, "plans to scale significantly"), and (2) the **Broadcom-Blackstone-Apollo AI XPV Platform** (up to **20 GW through 2028**, of which Barclays estimates **~15 GW is TPU**, the rest other custom ASICs incl. OpenAI). Combined external TPU-aaS capacity: **1.4 GW '26 / 3.7 GW '27 / 11.5 GW '28**. **Unit economics:** Google sells each TPU ~**$20k** to the SPV at ~**25% GM** (~$5k GP/unit) in '26, ASP rising **$20k→$33k→$53k** ('26/'27/'28); ~**700k units/GW** in '26 (~$14B rev/GW). **External TPU revenue: $20B '26 / $67.7B '27 / ~$253B '28** (GP $5B/$16.9B/$63.2B) — at '28 that is **~35% upside to consensus Alphabet revenue and ~15% to gross profit**. Barclays est **~$88B of TPU revenue already in backlog as of 2Q26**, ramping to $300B+ cumulative; cites the **$811B purchase commitments (+$479B Q/Q) in the 2Q26 10-Q** and **$275B+ backlog increase over 6 months** as evidence of scale. Model rebuild raises Google **Cloud revenue +16%/+46%/+128% ('26/'27/'28E)** and Cloud OI +5%/+18%/+70%; flags the asset-light structure may **reverse some future capex growth** (downside to Barclays' $500B '28 capex est). (Barclays · U.S. Internet, 2026-07-24) — companion semiconductor-team report on the AVGO side; see [AVGO.md](AVGO.md), [ANTHROPIC.md](ANTHROPIC.md), theme [custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).

## Capstone estimates (house model)
_Source: Capstone official model (`Google Modelo oficial.xlsx`, 2026-06-05). Model years 2025/26/27 (≈CY)._

| | 2025 | 2026E | 2027E |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| Revenue ($bn) | 403 | 505 | 641 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;— y/y | — | +25% | +27% |
| EPS — diluted ($) | 8.41 | 11.80 | 16.20 |
| Operating margin | ~32% | ~36% | ~37% |
| Capex ($bn) | 91 | 183 | 310 |
| FCF ($bn) | 33 | -3 | -55 |
| **GW (TPU/compute)** | — | **~4.6** | **~7.75** |

**House read:** EPS 2027E **$16.20 above consensus (~$14.5)**; capex rises to $310bn (~48% of revenue) pushing FCF negative (–$55bn '27E); compute base ~4.6 → 7.75 GW. (Capstone official model, 2026-06-05)

## Debate / thesis
- **Capstone model (house view):** revenue $403B / $505B / $641B (2025/26/27E, +25%/+27%); diluted EPS $8.41 / $11.80 / $16.20 — 2027E **above consensus ~$14.5**; operating margin ~32%→37%. Capex ramps $91B→$183B→$310B (23%→48% of revenue), turning FCF negative (–$3B 2026E → –$55B 2027E). (Capstone official model, 2026-06-05)
- **Bull:**
  - **"Best asset on Earth," won the AI Game of Thrones** — beauty-pageant Q1 print, TPU bull case now the *base* case, cloud guided to 80-90% growth next ~7 quarters per MS backlog file (Nowak, MS, 2026-05-08). Cloud → high-40s% of Alphabet EBIT by '28/'29.
  - **TPU merchant ramp now quantified externally** — Asia supply-chain checks corroborate the merchant-silicon thesis with hard unit numbers (~3mn 2026 → 5-6mn 2027) and ASP step-up to $23-34K, validating GCP backlog optionality and a credible second leg beyond GOOG's own consumption (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17).
  - **GS (Eric Sheridan) PT $450, owned/liked** — Q1 drove a positive revision cycle: ~30%+ cloud revenue revision in C27/28, mid-teens consolidated OI revision; ~$14 GAAP EPS '27 / ~$17 '28 (buy-side whisper $15+/$17.50-18+); sum-of-parts supportive >$450; custom-silicon mix + non-AI workload reaccel support durable cloud margins high-20s/mid-30s (GS, 2026-04-30).
  - **TPU ROIC arbitrage** — GOOGL renting ~110k GB300 from SpaceX at ~$50/watt implies line-of-sight to higher-monetizing products; MS model implies +27% upside to GCP growth at $25/watt monetization (Nowak, MS, briefing 2026-06-17). BG2 corroborates the SpaceX deal is priced "a lot higher" — Google paying SpaceX a large premium for terrestrial compute (~$50/GW/yr-class monetization vs Anthropic ~$22-23), partly a call option on first-in-line space compute; Clark Tang's analysis ranks xAI's Google cloud deal as generating **more operating profit per GW than Anthropic, Meta, Google or OpenAI** (BG2 podcast, 2026-06). Barclays (Sandler) OW $405, AI Search = "biggest change in 25 years," higher CTR/coverage (briefing 2026-05-29). Citi (Josey) Buy $447 on AI Search ad formats (briefing 2026-05-21). BofA: "most comprehensive full-stack AI offering of any company" (briefing 2026-06-03). SpaceX 2015 stake = ~$131B embedded gain at rumored IPO (briefing 2026-06-04).

Bottoms-up capacity build: MediaTek adds **1GW/1.5GW of TPU capacity in 2027/2028**, plus **+2GW of NVDA GPUs in 2028**, for **~9GW of net on-prem compute added in 2028 (~7GW TPU + 2GW NVDA GPU)** — GOOGL retains ~5GW of the AVGO-built TPUs for its own workloads and sells ~4GW externally on a first-party basis. **Core (pre-1P-TPU) Cloud** monetizes at **$15/$18 per watt in 2027/2028**, yielding **$152B/$229B of total Core Cloud/GCP revenue** (Cloud segment growth +106%/+44% in 2027/2028); **first-party TPU sales** are forecast to add **$62B/$79B of GOOGL Cloud revenue in 2027/2028** at a 20% GM base case (~$20B revenue per GW). MS raises its **2027 capex estimate to $350B** on better visibility into the 2028 TPU business; Total Revenue/EPS revised +4%/+1% in 2027/2028 on better Cloud growth and faster Search growth (+8% vs +7% prior) from GenAI/engagement gains (Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak via Skyler Risom, 2026-06-30).

PT $475, ~40% upside from a $341.77 close.

[Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_01_brent_jeff_ai_1_jun_26.html)
  - **Anthropic stake optionality** — Google has invested >$3bn and reportedly owned ~14% of Anthropic equity (as of 11-Mar-2025); also Anthropic's original cloud/TPU provider and a Vertex AI distributor — embedded equity + compute pull-through (JPM Anthropic deep-dive, 2025-09-18).
  - **HISTORICAL / BACKFILL — where the TPU-Anthropic relationship stood in Sept-2025 (SemiAnalysis "Amazon's AI Resurgence: AWS & Anthropic's Multi-Gigawatt Trainium Expansion", 2025-09-03; folded 2026-07-24 — ~11 months old, **do NOT read as current**):** useful as the provenance of today's TPU-aaS story, not as a live datapoint. At the time SemiAnalysis wrote that **Google Cloud was one of Anthropic's first major investors ($300M round late-2022) and its preferred cloud partner in 2023 and 2024, before the expanded AWS deal**, and that it believed "**most of Anthropic's skyrocketing inference needs are served by Google Cloud**," calling "**having the world's best inference system (TPU)**" a key competitive advantage. It also flagged that "**the TPU ramp for Anthropic in 2026 is huge, and there are unique aspects to their deal**" — written ~10 months before the Q2'26 print at which Google began recognising TPU-system sales with Anthropic as the anchor customer, so this is the early public marker on the arc the page now tracks via Barclays' TPU-aaS model (07-24). Two structural points from the same note that still read: Google (alongside Anthropic via Trainium/Annapurna) was named one of only **two labs benefiting from tight hardware-software co-design**; and on procurement, **Google runs its HBM through Broadcom, which stacks additional margin on it**, unlike Amazon which procures HBM directly — a standing cost-structure disadvantage inside the TPU BOM versus Trainium, relevant as HBM4 pricing tightens. Dated Sept-2025; every current TPU/Anthropic figure on this page (backlog, GW, unit volumes, TPU-aaS revenue) supersedes it and none of it was used to revise a 2026 number. (SemiAnalysis, 2025-09-03) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2025_09_03_ENC_Amazons_AI_Resurgence_AWS_Anthropics_Multi-Gigawatt_Trainium_Expansion_email.html) Search-remedies overhang resolving favorably (benign DOJ outcome), also a positive read for the Apple/Gemini partnership path (Bernstein initiation, 2025-09-15).
  - **🆕 The RSI frame — why the capex line should be read as an OPTION rather than an ARR payback, and why owned compute plus native self-improvement is the competitive structure that follows (FUNDA "Deep|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05).** The single most useful thing in the note for this page is not a number, it is a reframing of the exact calculation the bears run. FUNDA's argument: the market is evaluating AI **through an application lens** — looking for "the next coding" and checking whether current revenue covers capital expenditure — while the industry's own objective is AGI via **recursive self-improvement**, so capex is a **dual investment in current cloud returns AND an option on having sufficient compute when the RSI threshold arrives.** Its own words on the arithmetic: "Measured against a payback period on current ARR, the four hyperscalers' roughly $725B of annual guidance, Stargate's $500B and 9GW plan, and **multi-track compute partnerships such as Anthropic's with Google (up to one million TPUs) still do not close, which is precisely the calculation skeptics are running. What that calculation leaves out is the strategic dimension.**" ⚠️ **FUNDA's aggregate capex basis (~$725B for the four largest, +~77% y/y, "absorbing the majority of their operating cash flow") is a secondary aggregation and already sits BELOW the desk trackers this wiki carries from the prints (JPM: ~$750bn 2026, +80% y/y, 07-30). The number is not the contribution — the framing is, and it is the first coherent statement on this page of why a payback model on 2026 ARR is the wrong test rather than merely a harsh one.** **The competitive-structure leg reads directly onto the TPU/first-party-stack thesis:** if the frontier iterates monthly and the rate of iteration is itself rising, "distillation from black-box outputs alone may be insufficient to replicate a frontier lab's complete learning mechanism, data pipeline, and experimentation stack — distillation can transfer *what a model knows*; *how a model gets better* is harder to copy," so **"staying competitive in an RSI regime requires native self-improvement capability: your own flywheel, your own experimental stack, your own compute."** That is the strongest available third-party argument for the vertically-integrated case this page already carries from MS (07-27: Alphabet capturing both the landlord and tenant spread on the same gigawatt) and from Google's own Principal Architect (06-23) — and it is also the argument that makes the TPU an **entry ticket** rather than a margin story. FUNDA adds a third demand category the capex debate on this page does not model at all: **"experiment compute" — architecture search, data mixture, hyperparameter sweeps and ablations sitting underneath every production training run, where "fewer chips does not only mean slower training; it means an order-of-magnitude fewer attempts," and once models propose and execute experiments autonomously "the ceiling on experiment compute demand becomes a function of available capacity."** ⚠️ **Carry FUNDA's own discipline with the bull case, because it is what makes the note usable: "strategic necessity is not the same thing as shareholder return… an arms race that is individually rational can compress industry-level returns, because when everyone must expand, the cost of expansion gets bid up." Its scenario ladder: RSI threshold ~2027 (most likely; low risk), 2029-or-later (a capex digestion period where utilisation and depreciation decide who is hurt first; "warrants serious weight"), or longer/unsolved (spend beyond genuine inference demand faces impairment; tail).** ⚠️ **This is a framework, not a datapoint — it changes no number, rating or PT on this page. It is filed on the bull side because it is the most rigorous available answer to the "capex vs ARR does not close" bear, and because it explicitly refuses to promise the return.** (FUNDA, 2026-08-05) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Funda_on_RSI.html)
- **Bear:**
  - **Multiple, not fundamentals** — Nowak ranks GOOG #3 of MAG (Meta>Amazon>Google): "hard to re-rate Google five turns on a $5T cap," especially with large AI IPOs (OpenAI/Anthropic) competing for capital; no near-term dry powder for *multiple* expansion vs. AMZN where Trainium third-party optionality is unpriced (MS, 2026-05-08). GOOGL now trades **23.5x forward P/E vs 20.4x for the S&P 500 — down from ~30x in Feb 2026** after the ~15% drawdown from $400+ May highs to a $341.77 close (Barron's, 2026-06-29). _[Superseded: the prior page multiple was ~24x NTM / ~23x FY26 (Bernstein comp table, 2025-09-15) — moved to Changelog 2026-06-30.]_ Still a Mag7-low multiple with no obvious re-rate catalyst per the bear case.
  - **ChatGPT-vs-Search substitution overhang** — the perception that ChatGPT is eroding Search dominance is a "material overhang" on shares; OpenAI is the first material alternative to core Search at the model layer, and OpenAI's push into Workspace integrations (Gmail/Docs) extends the rivalry into productivity (UBS OpenAI deep-dive). Frontier-model commoditization (GPT-4 now ~95th on LM Arena) cuts both ways for any single provider's moat (JPM OpenAI initiation).
  - **Ad-tech antitrust** — Eric Seydoux/Stratechery frames the FTC/DOJ ad-tech loss as structural: Google's power is the long tail of search advertisers it leverages to control publishers; the network/AdX business is small and already a shrinking, actively declining part of Google, so a forced spin may be less consequential than headlines imply (Stratechery — Eric/ADS). Still, the guilty finding and remedy uncertainty are live overhangs.
  - **GCP margin quality — the "EBTIT" flag (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic Growth & Bedrock Mix", 2026-05-27):** SemiAnalysis argues Google Cloud's record-high margin and >60% Y/Y growth overstate the true economics — an "EBTIT" margin (Earnings Before Training, Interest and Taxes) because **DeepMind/Gemini training costs are booked in "Alphabet-Level Activities," not GCP** ($5.4B in 1Q26, up from $3.0B 1Q25; $10B+ run-rate sitting outside the segment while all Gemini-API revenue flows into GCP at above-average margins). Margin may also have been flattered by one-off Broadcom→Google→Anthropic TPU-sale royalties. The bear read: unlike AWS (the only CSP with a "true rising" margin trend on its Bedrock/TaaS mix), GCP's margin optics don't fully reflect the cost of competing on models — and Google's simultaneous supply constraints across cloud/hardware/models mean Vertex-as-Claude-distribution lags Bedrock's capacity adds. Complements the existing gross-TPU-backlog-inflation point (expert recap, 2026-04-30) with a P&L-margin angle. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_Bedrock.html)
  - **Capex / FCF / dilution** — C27 capex revised to ~$300B (MS, +57% y/y, Altimeter data, briefing 2026-06-13); FCF only $10.1B in Q1, buybacks shrinking, depreciation accelerating; $85B raise = "Mag7 morphing from cash machines into capital-intensive giants — weighs on multiples regardless of top-line" (Vital Knowledge, briefing 2026-06-02). Net share issuance historically tied to lower forward returns (Spectra/Donnelly, briefing 2026-06-03). **Buyside bogeys into the 07-22 print (BofA · Fenske buyside poll, 2026-07-12): 2026 capex $203.0B / 2027 $304.7B / 2028 $362.1B / 2029 $379.9B — the 2027 poll-avg (~$305B) sits *below* MS's $350B model; Big-5 2026-28 capex intensity has moved from 37.5% consensus (mid-April) to ~46.5% on buyside bogeys. Segment bogeys: Q2 rev +21%, Search +17%, YouTube +10%, Cloud +70%; FY26/27 GAAP EPS $14.65/$15.57.** **Hyperscaler AI-debt overhang: Google/Amazon/Microsoft/Meta/Oracle have together piled up ~$700bn of debt as AI data-center spend surges (@Hedgeye, 2026-07-13)** — a sector-wide framing of the capital-intensity turn (though Google's own funding has skewed toward the equity raise + senior notes rather than pure leverage).

DeepMind a net talent donor in the lab wars: per SignalFire, employees are ~11x more likely to leave DeepMind for Anthropic than vice versa (JPM Anthropic deep-dive, 2025-09-18). Pressure attributed to **top AI-researcher departures to Anthropic** and **Mag-7-wide AI-ROIC concerns**, i.e. the de-rating is a sentiment/positioning issue layered on top of (not contradicting) the raised Cloud/TPU estimates and $415 PT (Morgan Stanley Internet team, 2026-06-30). Separately, MS (via Skyler Risom, citing CNBC) notes **GOOGL reportedly pays SpaceX/xAI ~$920M/month for compute capacity (implied ~$50/W)**, cited as evidence of how tight GPU/TPU compute supply is and feeding directly into the TPU-retention math above (Morgan Stanley, 2026-06-30).

Labeled clearly as SemiAnalysis's opinion/estimate; see the Current-state bullet (2026-07-09). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_META.html)

- **GOOGL is the only name that sits on BOTH sides of MS's new GenAI ROIC frameworks — and MS prices both legs (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", Brian Nowak CFA / Stephen C Byrd / Adam Wood, 2026-07-27; Industry View Attractive):** the answer to the ROIC question this page has been litigating since the 07-22 capex raise, argued from unit economics rather than sentiment. MS is bullish "hyperscalers **AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT** and model developers **GOOGL and META**" — **Alphabet appears in both lists, the only name that does**, because it runs GCP (framework #1) and Gemini (framework #2) off the same fleet. All three frameworks standardise on **1GW of NVDA GB300 — 410,256 chips, $39bn all-in capex/GW ($23bn IT on a 5-yr life + $16bn non-IT on a 15-yr life, ~$4mn per GB300 rack per MS's Asia semis rack BoM) → ~$6bn/yr of depreciation, 75% utilisation, $7.6bn/GW of total opex (IT dep $5bn + non-IT dep $1bn + energy/other $2bn), 21% tax.** **(1) Hyperscaler GPU rental (IaaS) — the GCP leg:** **$8.50/hr → $22.9bn revenue/GW, $15.3bn incremental EBIT (~67% incremental margin), $12.1bn NOPAT, ~31% ROIC**, band **23% → 39%** across $7.00-$10.00/hr; MS: "we remain bullish **AWS, GCP and Azure**." **(2) Model-enabled API on owned infrastructure — the Gemini leg, and the best returns of the three:** 65% of capacity to inference, **2,750 tokens/sec/GPU**, **$1.75/mn blended → $30bn revenue/GW, $22.7bn EBIT (~75% margin), $17.9bn NOPAT, ~46% ROIC** (band 19% → 63%), with "**Google Gemini**" named first among the public model labs. **(3) The same API on 3P/rented infrastructure at $7.75/hr → $40.5bn revenue less ~$28bn compute = $12.6bn EBIT (~31% margin), $10.0bn NOPAT, ~25% NOPAT margin** (band -9% → 32%) — the framework that prices the **~$50/W SpaceX arrangement** already on this page as a bridge cost, and quantifies why Google chose to monetise its own gigawatts through TPUs/Vertex rather than sell short-tenor capacity (the SemiAnalysis 07-02 read). **Put together, MS's numbers say Alphabet captures the landlord spread AND the tenant spread on the same gigawatt — ~31% ROIC as the IaaS provider plus ~46% ROIC as the owner-operator model lab — which is the cleanest published statement of the vertical-integration argument Google's own Principal Architect made on this page (06-23: 100% of purchased Nvidia GPUs to external GCP customers, all internal services on TPUs, "a much, much better business model").** ⚠️ **Carry MS's own caveat, because it is exactly what the 07-22 print showed:** "reported near-term incremental margins will remain below that range" on (a) prior-generation chip mix while GB300 scales, (b) traditional cloud workloads in the mix, (c) opex not in the analysis — so the ~67% is steady-state GB300-only and does not contradict the 35.6% reported Cloud OI margin or the 3Q third-party-capacity drag. Methodology worth carrying for the TPU debate specifically: **the frameworks are built on GB300 and MS explicitly notes that "while custom ASICs and prior GPU generation have lower costs… we believe chip pricing/hour and token throughput are also lower"** — i.e. MS is *not* claiming these are TPU economics, and a TPU-based version of framework #1 would flex both revenue and cost down. Also: the **2,000-3,500 tokens/sec/GPU** range is not a hardware constant (DeepSeek-V4-Pro 1.6T, FP4, 8K/1K I/O at ~50 tokens/sec/user); **larger models price higher but throughput lower, so higher token pricing does not scale revenue/GW proportionately**; and training cost is charged by **discounting the share of compute available for revenue-generating inference while still bearing all the costs** — the right way to think about Gemini 4 pretraining against Cloud ROIC. MS's corollary is a warning as much as a bull point: these returns are "an invitation for competition… further new entrants/open source offerings," which keeps the premium on product and compute-efficiency innovation and on **the value of compute infrastructure assets**. (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260727_2024.html)
- **The refreshed MS risk-reward page for GOOGL — $400 held after the post-print cut, with the full scenario ladder (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", 2026-07-27):** **Overweight, PT $400.00** = **~24x the average of MS's $15/$18 '27/'28 EPS**, implying **~1.6x PEG, a ~35% premium to GOOGL's peer median** — no drift, this is Nowak's 07-23 post-print mark reiterated four days later, and the PEG framing is net-new to this page. **Bull $450** (~26x avg '27/'28 bull EPS of ~$16/~$19 — faster innovation, Search/GCP acceleration, YouTube and Cloud becoming bigger and higher-margin contributors); **Bear $225** (~15x avg ~$14/~$16 — global ad growth slows, expense discipline fails, "New AI products create greater than expected margin pressure due to **lower monetization rates and increased compute intensity**"). **Both scenario levels are net-new on this page, which previously carried only the $400 base.** **Spot $319.74**; mean consensus PT **$420.48** (range **$220-$515**); consensus rating distribution **89% OW / 11% EW / 0% UW**; options-implied (24 Jul 2026) **P(>$450) ~13.1%, P(>$400) ~23.5%, P(<$225) ~15.3%**. ⚠️ **Note the positioning asymmetry, and it is the opposite of META's and AMZN's on the same exhibit set:** the market prices GOOGL above MS's base at **~23.5%** against only **~15.3%** below the bear — the only one of MS's four internet names where the base case is more likely than the bear. Read against the 07-22 sell-off and the -7.8% week, that is a meaningful statement that the drawdown is seen as capex-driven de-rating rather than thesis impairment. Key earnings inputs: **total operating income (GAAP) 129,039 (2025) → 176,395 (26e) → 227,461 (27e) → 280,882 (28e)** — **+36.7% / +29.0% / +23.5%**, which is the sharpest contrast on the internet complex with META's +3.0% 2028 OI growth on a comparable capex build. **⚠️ UPDATED 2026-08-13 — MS's 08-12 exhibit set raises [[META]]'s 2028 GAAP operating income to 111,210 from 104,841 (and cuts FY26 to 89,133 from 92,216), so META's 2028 OI growth is now +7.5%, not the +3.0% recorded above from the 07-27 note. The contrast NARROWS but survives: GOOGL +23.5% vs META +7.5% in 2028, on MS's own numbers, with GOOGL's operating-income series itself unchanged. Old value retained inline as the 07-27 mark; see Changelog.** (MS also shows a separate "GAAP Operating Income (Loss)" line at **137,054 → 183,870 → 234,661 → 287,432** — two operating-income series in the same table, carried verbatim, not reconciled.) MS vs consensus FY Dec-2026e: revenue **500,611 vs cons 495,765** (range 475,342-519,818); EBITDA **241,667 vs cons 232,729** (MS at the top of the 223,773-241,667 range); net income **255,100 vs cons 234,369** (range 158,271-268,656); EPS **20.62 vs cons 19.37** (range 12.30-21.92). ⚠️ **Two housekeeping flags: (a) MS's FY26e net income of 255,100 exceeds its own EBITDA of 241,667, which on the face of it requires large below-the-line gains — consistent with the ~$80bn Anthropic-revaluation benefit BofA already models on this page (07-16), but MS does not label it, so it is logged as-is and not "corrected"; (b) MS's $15/$18 '27/'28 EPS sits below BofA's FY27 $14.70/FY28 $18.12 on '27 and roughly in line on '28, and well below the house model's $16.20 for 2027E** (Capstone official model, 2026-06-05) — so the house remains above MS on the out-year. Risk-reward themes: Secular Growth Positive, New Data Era Positive. Ownership: Inst. owners 55.9% active, HF sector long/short 2.6x, net exposure 12.3%; **MS Alpha Models 4/5 BEST (24m) / 3/5 MOST (3m) — the weakest quintile scores of MS's four internet names**, consistent with Nowak's standing "hard to re-rate Google five turns on a $5T cap" relative-underweight framing already on this page. (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260727_2024.html)

- **Positioning caution into the 2Q print (Bernstein TMT · Tyler Seidman, 2026-07-14):** "GOOGL sentiment fades into 2Q print" — a sentiment/positioning-caution flag heading into the 07-22 earnings, i.e. the setup has de-risked somewhat vs the strong fundamental estimates. Reads alongside MS's own "worst-performing internet name over the past month" positioning note (06-30) (Bernstein · Tyler Seidman, 2026-07-14).
- **Chris Wood (Jefferies) removes Alphabet from two global portfolios (2026-06-25).** Christopher Wood removes Alphabet from both his global long-only and international long-only portfolios, rotating the capital into SK Hynix and Samsung Electronics (hardware / "picks and shovels"). The rationale is NOT a fundamental bear call on Google's business but a *Jevons Paradox portfolio rotation*: cheaper AI tokens (GLM-5.2, open-source Chinese models) → more compute demand → the hardware layer (DRAM, GPU) outperforms the model/software layer. Worth noting as a contra-indicator: Chris Wood is historically a buy-and-hold macro/tech investor who rarely rotates out of core long-term holdings. The removal reflects a view that GOOG's AI optionality is already partially captured and that the near-term marginal alpha is in semiconductor hardware. Neutral-to-negative on positioning; fundamental thesis intact (Jefferies / Christopher Wood "GREED & fear: The mother of all cycles", 2026-06-25). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Chris_Wood.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Chris_Wood.html)
- **MS (Stephen Byrd) sees Google Cloud as a hyperscaler winner of the model-routing era (2026-06-26).** MS's thematic research head argues that hyperscalers building sophisticated model-routing/orchestration capabilities — including Google Cloud — are net beneficiaries if "orchestration layers can cobble together multiple lower-cost models to achieve the same result as a single frontier model." However, the same argument is also the bear case for Google's first-party Gemini models if orchestration commoditizes frontier-level output. Net: positive for Google Cloud, ambiguous for DeepMind/Gemini business specifically (Morgan Stanley / Stephen Byrd "Tokenmaxxing discussion", 2026-06-26). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_26_stephen_byrd_on_tokenmaxxing.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_26_stephen_byrd_on_tokenmaxxing.html)
- **Token-optimization read-through — neutral:** UBS is **neutral** on hyperscalers re: token optimization — enterprise down-tiering to cheaper SLMs/open-source Chinese models (Qwen, Z.ai, Kimi, DeepSeek) could weigh on frontier-model API revenue growth and AI price sensitivity could limit near-term GPU pricing, but the hyperscalers are already multi-model and face a wall of AI demand. For GOOG the down-tiering risk is within the frontier suite (cheaper Gemini tiers vs the premium model) rather than a platform threat, and being multi-model is framed as an edge "similar to the advantage secured by the likes of Snowflake and MongoDB that were multi-cloud while AWS and Microsoft were pushing their native databases" (UBS "The Ramp in Token Optimization" / Keirstead-Arcuri, 2026-06-23).

## Catalysts / what to watch
- **🆕🔴 GEMINI 4 — now the single highest-stakes event on this page.** The model's release is what the 2026-08-05 leadership overhaul has to be judged against. **Two independent negatives to test: The Information reports the next flagship model is DELAYED while Anthropic/OpenAI ship coding-and-cybersecurity-strong models (2026-08-06), and @alexeheath's sources describe internal sentiment on Gemini 4 as "MUTED… not expected to push frontier AI forward the way Fable and Sol just did" (via @firstadopter, 2026-08-06). @firstadopter's stated stake: "Both Sundar and Demis have been pushing the narrative that Gemini 4 is looking good. If it is NOT SOTA, they will have serious credibility problems with the Street."** Unverified relay sourcing — but it is the falsifiable version of the "coding gap" management conceded on the Q2'26 call.
- **🆕 Koray Kavukcuoglu's first public outing — The Information's AI Agenda Live, San Francisco, 2026-09-23.** Billed topics include integrating frontier models across Search and the multi-billion-user surfaces, autonomous coding models "capable of writing and improving their own code" (direct collaboration with Sergey Brin), and — most useful for this page — **"Rationing the Backbone: how Google's highly exclusive executive council decides who gets access to scarce, high-stakes AI chip capacity"** (The Information, 2026-08-06). The internal-vs-merchant TPU allocation question, on the record, from the person now running GDM.
- **🆕 Further researcher attrition** — the compounding risk, not the initial event. Track against the run already on the record: Noam Shazeer → [[OPENAI]], John Jumper → [[ANTHROPIC]], Jeff Dean (+ reportedly Ghemawat/Vinyals/Le) → own startup (The Information, 2026-08-05/06).
- **🆕 The four RSI progress indicators — a falsifiable, industry-level tracking set that is cheaper to monitor than any single quarter's ARR (FUNDA "Deep|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05).** FUNDA's argument is that the capability line, not the application layer, is the variable that decides the capex debate — and it names the four series to watch, all of which it says are currently accelerating: **(1) flagship release interval** (~27-33 months for GPT generations in 2023-24 → 24-58 days for Anthropic flagships mid-2026); **(2) pre-training-complete → shippable** (~7 months for GPT-4 → ~1 month for GPT-5.5, i.e. post-training and evaluation being automated); **(3) new model → price cut** (months-to-a-year → 3 weeks and up to −80% on GPT-5.6, i.e. inference optimisation largely done by AI); **(4) METR capability doubling time** (~7 months pre-2023 → ~89 days from 2024, with Claude Opus 4.5 at a 320-minute task horizon in METR's Jan-26 update). **The Google-specific item to hold against these: Gemini 4.** This page already carries the delay report and the "muted" internal chatter (08-06); the indicator set is what turns that from sentiment into a measurable — if Google's own release cadence and pre-training-to-ship interval do not compress on the same curve, the coding/agentic gap management conceded on the Q2'26 call is a structural lag rather than a one-model miss. **The named commercial trigger to watch is continual learning** — FUNDA's claim is that it is "the acknowledged core research focus today," that it is what unlocks enterprise adoption (shifting the TAM reference from a ~$1T software budget to a >$10T wage bill), and that it is also the thesis's genuine soft spot: "if continual learning alone takes a decade rather than two or three years, both the adoption curve and the value of strategic investment shrink materially." (FUNDA, 2026-08-05) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Funda_on_RSI.html)
- **Q2 FY26 print (resolved 2026-07-22; bull won operationally)** — Search +17% met the buyside bar, YouTube +13% beat, and Cloud +82% / $514B backlog cleared the +70% bogey; offset by $44.9B capex and $(5.9)B FCF. The call then RAISED FY26 capex to $195-205B (from $180-190B) and began recognizing TPU-system sales (small in Q2, 2027-weighted); C27 capex magnitude remains unquantified ("significant increase"). [Review](../GOOG/GOOG_2Q26_earnings_review_2026-07-22.md)
- **C27 capex number** — guided "significant increase"; Street already at ~$300B (MS). Magnitude vs. FCF/dilution is the swing factor.
- **New inference chip "Frozen v2"** — The Information (2026-07-20) reports a new Google-designed chip to run its AI models "much more efficiently" — a custom-silicon efficiency play beyond the TPU line; watch for confirmation/timeline on the print. **Specified 2026-07-24 (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, "Blayne's Bytes"):** the internal nickname is **"Frozen v2"** and the mechanism is that it **hardwires elements of Gemini directly into silicon, reducing the number of calculations and the amount of data movement required to answer a query**. Google engineers reportedly project it could serve **6-10x more tokens per unit of power vs the latest TPUs**, with **deployment targeted for C28**. Curtis adds the two constraints that matter before this gets modelled: Frozen is described as "**more experimental rather than a replacement for the TPU program**," with **production volumes targeted for C28 and expected to run well below TPU levels**, and **flexibility limited to Gemini versions that keep the same core architecture** — i.e. a model-specific ASIC that obsoletes when the architecture changes. On the supply chain: "**this could potentially be a MRVL design (unconfirmed) — just would caution on the volumes**" (see [MRVL.md](MRVL.md)). Read as a **cost-per-token option, not a TPU-roadmap event**: 6-10x tokens/watt on even modest volumes matters for inference unit economics at a moment when Google's own FCF is the market's problem with the story, but it does nothing to the 2026-27 capex line.
- **External TPU / merchant-silicon ramp** — supply-chain checks now point to ~3mn 2026 → 5-6mn 2027 units, v8 at 2nm/$23-34K, MediaTek $1bn 2026 target, EMIB-T evaluation for v9, and Hon Hai full-rack build-out (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17). Blackstone TPU JV (500MW by '27), neocloud-style structures (MS Credit, briefing 2026-05-22); Samsung foundry engagement for Axion (~2028) + TPU I/O die work as TSMC advanced nodes tighten (Rothschild, briefing 2026-06-18; Jefferies on TPU v10 "Icefish," 2026-06-12).
- **Gemini app monetization / ads-in-AI-Mode** — management "not rushing"; format that works in AI Mode expected to port to Gemini app (Q1 FY26).
- **Apple/Gemini deal economics** — GOOG as Apple preferred cloud provider + powering next-gen AFM (Q4 FY25); benign DOJ remedies open the partnership path (Bernstein, 2025-09-15); economics TBD. At WWDC 2026 Apple gave Gemini a **dedicated keynote slide** as the foundation grounding Siri/Apple Intelligence with world knowledge (replacing the prior bolt-on ChatGPT framing) — Counterpoint reads Google as much deeper into agentic experiences (redesigned search box, Spark background-task automation, anti-gravity 2.0 dev tools) while Apple takes the more conservative path; Google One AI tiers now include a new **$100/month** plan reserving the latest Gemini features (Citi/Counterpoint WWDC, 2026-06-15). Overhang: EU DMA (July deadline) third-party-sensor-access demand hits both Apple and Google. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_15_aapl_wwdc_citi_15_jun_26.html)
- **Dow Jones Industrial Average inclusion (Barron's, 2026-06-29):** Alphabet added to the DJIA effective **2026-06-23**, replacing Verizon — a passive-flow / franchise-recognition milestone. Barron's historical aside (labeled as *Barron's own precedent, not GOOG data*): Nvidia fell 21% over the six months after its own 2024 Dow addition before later recovering (Barron's, 2026-06-29).
- **TD Cowen PT $475 (Barron's, 2026-06-29):** John Blackledge sets a $475 target, ~40% upside from a $341.77 recent close, on cloud compounding 37% to $480bn by 2031 (TD Cowen via Barron's, 2026-06-29).
- **Axion volume ramp through 2026 (SemiAnalysis, 2026-02-09):** N4A (64-core Neoverse N3, TSMC 3nm) in preview and sized for "significant volume ramps through 2026"; a *SemiAnalysis forward framing* (speculative, **not a confirmed Google roadmap item**) holds that Google will eventually design Axion CPUs as **head nodes in TPU clusters powering Gemini** (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back", 2026-02-09).
- **Waymo** — 500k+ autonomous rides/week, 11 US cities, $16B round; international (UK/Japan) expansion.

## Risks
- **Search ad concentration** — bulk of revenue/profit from advertising; AI-driven query-behavior shift could pressure CTR/CPC mix even as volume grows (10-K FY25; "intense competition"). ChatGPT substitution is the headline overhang (UBS OpenAI deep-dive).
- **Antitrust / regulatory remedies** — active US suits on ad-tech, Search distribution, Android/Play Store; ad-tech guilty finding with remedy uncertainty (Stratechery — Eric/ADS); Search-distribution DOJ remedies appear benign (Bernstein, 2025-09-15). UK CMA Search/data-portability orders.
- **Klarna antitrust judgment (Rothschild & Co Redburn US Morning, 2026-07-01):** Google (Buy-rated at Redburn) was ordered to pay **Klarna $1.5bn** in an antitrust judgment. Separately, Google is arguing **fair use** in AI-training litigation involving **UMG/WMG/Spotify** — an unresolved overhang on training-data legal exposure (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 2026-07-01).
- **Capex digestion / depreciation drag** — accelerating depreciation + energy/DC opex pressure P&L; ROIC depends on backlog converting and AI monetization scaling.
- **Intel's EMIB yield/capacity is the single biggest risk to the 2028 TPU ramp — not vendor choice, not demand (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), "industry highlights & checks update", 2026-08-03).** Because Fubon believes **TSMC alone cannot supply 12-15mn TPUs** ("TSMC's production bottleneck"), **Intel's supply becomes "a must" by 2028** — which makes Intel's EMIB **production yield and capacity expansion the gating variable on Google's own AI roadmap**. Asked what could break the sell-side's $35-52bn 2028 custom-ASIC estimates, Shang answers with this and only this: **"Intel's production is the biggest risk… if Intel is unable to enter production, that means MediaTek, they need to go back to TSMC. And I don't think TSMC has the capacity to support Google to do 12 million to 15 million. So when that happens, we will probably see some downside."** ⚠️ **Note what this rules out as the offset: he does NOT see the volume reallocating to Broadcom or being in-housed via Alchip/GUC — the downside case is the 2028 TPU number itself coming down, not a change of vendor.** Yield detail (lab vs production rates) and capacity-doubling plans on [INTC.md](INTC.md). **Second, distinct gate added the same day (Arthur's ODM checks): L11 TPU rack assembly.** Hon Hai's capacity learning curve is slow, so it likely takes **the V8 rack system in 2027**, and "Google is very aggressive for 2027, 2028, so he need more supplier" — i.e. Google must qualify **additional ODMs** for rack assembly to hit 2028. This sits downstream of both the wafer and packaging constraints the page already tracks and has not been logged before. Third, timing risk on the silicon itself: **V9 is not yet taped out** (expected end-2026) for a **Q1'28** launch, so the 2028 numbers are pre-tape-out.
- **Compute supply chain** — power, land, advanced-node (TSMC CoWoS), HBM/DRAM and packaging (ABF, T-glass) constraints; TPU upside is gated by whether the supply chain can meet doubled memory/CoWoS demand (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17).
- **Frontier-model / talent leakage** — model leadership not guaranteed (Shazeer departure; DeepMind a net talent donor to Anthropic, JPM, 2025-09-18; MoE-vs-TPU debate); model commoditization risk (JPM OpenAI initiation).
- **Capital-markets fragility / drawdown (Barron's, 2026-06-29):** the stock dropped ~15% from May highs of $400+ to a $341.77 close on AI-brain-drain fears + the equity raise, including a **$225bn single-day market-cap loss — which Barron's calls the worst-ever one-day loss for any company** (Barron's, 2026-06-29).
- **AI-researcher brain drain (Barron's, 2026-06-29):** John Jumper left Google DeepMind for Anthropic; Noam Shazeer left *again* for OpenAI — a talent-leakage overhang at DeepMind (Barron's, 2026-06-29; see also Rothschild, 2026-06-18).
- **Equity-raise dilution (Barron's, 2026-06-29):** the **$85bn equity raise** to fund AI infra is a dilution/capital-intensity overhang (Barron's, 2026-06-29; corroborates the $84.75bn figure in Current state).
- **Chinese-model competition (Gavekal via Barron's, 2026-06-29):** Will Denyer (Gavekal Research) flags DeepSeek, Alibaba, Minimax and Z.ai as "increasingly important players" and "a clear threat to U.S. model developers" (Gavekal Research via Barron's, 2026-06-29).
- **YouTube brand-ad softness** and FX (tailwind moderating to ~1pt in Q2 from 3pt in Q1).
- **Internal AI-ethics friction / Pentagon deal (@Hesamation, 2026-07-14):** a senior Google DeepMind researcher plus 600+ employees reportedly asked Sundar Pichai not to put their AI models on classified military networks; Google signed the Pentagon deal anyway — a reputational/talent-culture overhang (breaks DeepMind's original 2014 no-military-use condition). Unverified employee-letter claim; flagged as a watch item, not a confirmed fundamental. (@Hesamation, 2026-07-14)
- **Copyright-training litigation widening (@eleconomista, 2026-07-14):** book publishers including **Hachette** sued Google alleging unauthorized use of copyrighted works to train its AI models — adds to the existing UMG/WMG/Spotify fair-use litigation as a training-data legal overhang. (@eleconomista, 2026-07-14)

FUNDA's read: the algorithmic input to AI R&D is becoming endogenous to compute** (FUNDA "Deep\|LLM", 2026-08-05) | **⚠ qualitative positive** — supports faster capability and research loops, but is industry-level rather than Gemini-specific and does not resolve the product-level coding gap; excerpt ends mid-sentence. **⚠→✓ UPGRADED on the evidence, NOT on the conclusion (08-06): Dean's qualitative claim now has two disclosed, quantified Google instances behind it (AlphaEvolve 23%/0.7%; the 25→50→75% AI-code series), so the research-automation loop is confirmed as REAL AND COMPOUNDING at Google. It still does not close the gap the row was opened for — FUNDA's own calibration puts Anthropic company-wide at 70-90% and frontline engineers at both Anthropic and OpenAI near 100%, i.e. Google is inside the pack, not ahead of it. Research-process automation is an industry condition; Gemini 4 is still the only thing that settles the model-quality row above.** |

One offset: UBS reads the June equity raise as well-timed — "Google did so at what was an all-time share price high"** | **✗ contests** (the capex raise was the sell-off trigger; FCF −$5.9B, funding-short positioning, SPCX margin drag; MS cuts EPS + PT as investment outweighs revenue — and UBS now uses Google as the cross-sector cautionary tale for what a capex raise costs the stock. Capital intensity stays the swing; the only constructive nuance is that the equity was raised at the highs). **08-03 (Fubon PRIMARY transcript) puts the supply chain on the other side of this row, explicitly: "despite market concerns regarding Google's CAPEX and negative free cash flow, we observe that Google has a very aggressive target to deploy 12 to 15 million TPUs in 2028" — i.e. the people being asked to build the capacity are being asked for MORE at the exact moment the equity market is punishing the capex line. Dates now attached: V8 in production Q4'26, V9 in Q1'28 (chip not yet taped out — expected end-2026), 4-5mn of the 2028 target allocated to MediaTek on a DEMAND basis only. ⚠️ But the same call adds a THIRD gate downstream of wafers and packaging, new to this page: Hon Hai's L11 TPU rack-assembly learning curve is too slow, so it gets the V8 rack in 2027 and Google must qualify ADDITIONAL ODMs to hit 2028. So the capex is not just a funding question — spending it requires TSMC CoWoS, Intel EMIB *and* rack assemblers, and Fubon's checks say the third one is short too.** |
| **Competition / model** | Gemini 3.6 Flash + 3.5 Flash-Lite shipped; 3.5 Pro in testing; Gemini 4 pretraining begun ("most ambitious yet"); coding gap acknowledged; still supply-constrained on token demand | Jefferies (Wong): call responses on frontier/agentic-coding "fairly disappointing," silence on 3.5 Pro "deafening," tokens/min +38% q/q (decel from +60% in 1Q) · Barclays (Kantrowitz): Gemini-4 delay of 4-6 months "a tough hurdle," burden of proof now on Google to ship a leading model · MS (Nowak, 07-23): needs the Gemini 3.5 Pro + Gemini 4 suite to reassure investors it stays on the frontier, but mgmt's flagged **monthly model-ship cadence at Gemini 4** is "notable" (a positive nuance) | **⚠→✗** (model cadence the soft spot — 3.5 Pro AWOL, Gemini 4 pushed, coding gap unresolved; token growth decelerating; MS's monthly-cadence note the one forward positive) |
| **🆕 Open-weight models & the token-price deck (NEW theme — first framed on this page)** | Not addressed as such on the Q2'26 call; management's frame is capacity-constrained token demand (**~22B tokens/min**, from ~16B q/q) and a monthly model-ship cadence at Gemini 4 | **MS (08-12) is the first house to price it, and it splits Alphabet's two legs in opposite directions. ON THE CLOUD LEG: open-weight adoption does NOT break hyperscaler ROIC — "COMPUTE IS THE SCARCE RESOURCE," cheaper tokens arrive with "SUBSTANTIALLY MORE VOLUME," "EBIT DOLLAR GROWTH MATTERS MORE THAN PERCENTAGE MARGINS HERE," and open models monetise through four paths: Vertex token revenue (a possible loss leader — "LIKE MILK IN A GROCERY STORE"), accelerator rental, RAG-driven storage/database revenue, and security — generating "MORE CORE CLOUD INFRASTRUCTURE ATTACH PER WORKLOAD." GOOGL stays OW explicitly on lowest-cost-to-serve through custom silicon. ON THE MODEL LEG: open weights "create PRICING COMPETITION ACROSS THE MODEL LAYER" and compress incremental unit economics, which is why Flash-class efficiency now ranks alongside frontier position and why adoption/ecosystem ("SIMILAR TO OTHER OPEN BUILDS LIKE ANDROID") becomes the defence. 🔴 The quantified edge for Alphabet sits in the deck rather than the prose: MS's model-API framework earns ~46% ROIC on OWNED infrastructure, but at the bear end of the same deck on RENTED infrastructure it runs NEGATIVE — $23bn revenue/GW (at $1.00/mn and 3,500 tok/s/GPU) against $25bn cost of compute/GW at $7.00/hr. Alphabet is the only large lab that owns both the fleet and the silicon it runs on.** 🆕 **08-14 (Hugging Face, "State of Open Models: Summer 2026"): the ecosystem leg of that defence now has numbers, and they are two-sided. Google is the #2 base-model source on the Hub with 82,506 DERIVATIVES (Qwen 151,448) and Gemma does 20.8m GGUF downloads/month vs Llama's 7.5m — the strongest Western open-weights distribution position. ⚠️ But essentially NONE of Google's 2026 Hub downloads are of models above 70B, so the "Android of models" analogy is currently earned at the SMALL-MODEL end only.** | **✓ confirms** on the Cloud leg — this is the vertical-integration argument stated in unit economics rather than in narrative, and it is the same conclusion GS reached from the margin side on 08-12 ("it's a volume game… the agnostic player"). **⚠ nuances** on the model leg: MS is telling the market to stop scoring Gemini on the frontier flag alone — a lower bar in one sense, but an UNMEASURED one, because Google discloses no Flash-level throughput, pricing or mix |
