| **08-12** | **Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak (with Julian Herrera / Gregory Gao / Nikhil Javeri / Kavya Narayanan) — "How Could Open-Weight Models Impact GenAI ROIC?" · Internet · North America, Industry View Attractive** | **margem / produto** | **bull** | ★★ **THE SENTENCE THAT MATTERS MOST FOR THE GEMINI DEBATE ON THIS PAGE, and it reframes it: because open-weight models push token prices down, "this is why NEW GOOGLE GEMINI FLASH MODELS (ITS MORE EFFICIENT MODELS) ARE JUST AS IMPORTANT AS GEMINI 4 BEING ON THE FRONTIER LATER THIS YEAR, IN OUR VIEW."** ➜ **This page has priced Gemini 4 as the single binary that settles the model row (07-22 call, the 08-06 "muted" relay, Barclays' "burden of proof"). MS is explicitly saying the frontier flag is now only half the scorecard — in a price-deflating token market, ARCHITECTURE EFFICIENCY AND THROUGHPUT carry equal weight, and Flash is where Google competes on that axis. A muted Gemini 4 alongside a strong Flash line is a materially different outcome from a muted Gemini 4 alone, and nothing on this page currently distinguishes the two.** ★ **THE MECHANISM: open-weight models "create pricing competition across the model layer," multiple US labs have already released lower-priced offerings ([[META]]'s Muse Spark 1.2 last week), and lower token pricing means "LOWER INCREMENTAL UNIT ECONOMICS FOR MODEL PROVIDERS… putting a HIGHER IMPORTANCE ON TOKEN THROUGHPUT, ADOPTION AND BUILDING AN ECOSYSTEM (SIMILAR TO OTHER OPEN BUILDS LIKE ANDROID)."** ★ **AND WHY GOOGL IS STILL OW — the note ends on a named preference: "investors should remain focused on the hyperscaler (or owner of compute) that has the LOWEST COST TO SERVE (through CUSTOM SILICON, unique 1P models/offerings and/or other software/hardware advances). **WE REMAIN OW AMZN AND GOOGL FOR THESE REASONS.**" The throughput levers MS attributes to hyperscalers specifically are ① proprietary hardware — "custom silicon via TPUs/Trainium" — and ② software: "hyperscalers have the DEEPEST ENTERPRISE FOOTPRINTS and thus the HIGHEST VOLUME OF REQUESTS TO BATCH." Request batching as a scale advantage is net-new to this page.** ★ **THE ARITHMETIC UNDERNEATH, and the reason Alphabet's vertical integration is the point: on the LAB layer MS models a base of **~$1.75 per million tokens** and **2,000-3,500 tokens/sec/GPU** (benchmarked on open-weight DeepSeek-V4-Pro 1.6T running on GB300), still generating **~20-60% ROIC on 1GW of OWNED GB300**. But on RENTED infrastructure the same deck breaks at the bear end: revenue/GW of $23bn (at $1.00/mn and 3,500 tok/s) against a cost of compute/GW of $25bn at $7.00/hr — **the model layer runs NEGATIVE when it does not own the metal.** Alphabet is the only large lab that owns its fleet and its silicon, which is the quantified version of the vertical-integration argument this page has been making qualitatively.** ★ **The hyperscaler IaaS table, reprinted unchanged as evidence the ROIC band survives the open-weight deck: 1GW GB300 (410,256 chips, 75% utilisation, $7.6bn/GW opex, 21% tax) at $7.00 / $8.50 / $10.00 per GPU-hr → revenue/GW **$18.9 / $22.9 / $27.0bn**, incremental EBIT margins **60 / 67 / 72%**, NOPAT **$8.9 / $12.1 / $15.3bn**, **ROIC 23 / 31 / 39%**. Open-weight monetisation for the cloud layer runs through four paths: serverless API tokens (**Vertex** named), accelerator rental, RAG-driven storage/database revenue, and security — with token revenue on open models framed as a possible loss leader, "LIKE MILK IN A GROCERY STORE," that pulls "MORE CORE CLOUD INFRASTRUCTURE ATTACH PER WORKLOAD."** 🔴 **REFRESHED GOOGL RISK-REWARD — MS'S NUMBERS DID NOT MOVE; CONSENSUS CAME TO THEM. OW, **PT $400** (~24X the average of $15/$18 '27/'28 EPS; ~1.6X PEG, ~35% premium to peer median), **bull $450** (~26X ~$16/~$19), **bear $225** (~15X ~$14/~$16) — all three unchanged vs 07-27, as are the driver lines (total GAAP operating income **129,039 → 176,395 → 227,461 → 280,882**; the second "GAAP Operating Income (Loss)" series **137,054 → 183,870 → 234,661 → 287,432**, still carried unreconciled). What moved: spot **$319.74 → $343.80**; mean consensus PT **$420.48 → $421.45** (range $220-$515 → **$240-$515**, i.e. the Street-low bear came UP $20); rating distribution unchanged at **89% OW / 11% EW / 0% UW**.** ★★ **THE CONSENSUS CATCH-UP, which is the single most useful new fact here: MS vs consensus FY Dec-2026e is now revenue **500,611 vs 497,728** (was vs 495,765), EBITDA **241,667 vs 233,030** (was vs 232,729), net income **255,100 vs 251,893** (was vs 234,369), **EPS $20.62 vs $20.47 (was vs $19.37)**. MS held its estimates and the Street closed the EPS gap from **~+6.5% to ~+0.7%** in three weeks — so the "MS is above consensus on Alphabet" argument has essentially stopped being a source of edge. Consensus net income moved +$17.5bn.** ★ **POSITIONING, and the asymmetry got MORE favourable: options-implied **P(>$450) ~17.3% (from 13.1%), P(>$400) ~28.5% (from 23.5%), P(<$225) ~12.4% (from 15.3%)** — GOOGL remains the only one of MS's internet names where the market prices the BASE case as more likely than the bear, and the spread widened from ~8pts to ~16pts. Hedge-fund exposure came DOWN: sector L/S **2.6x → 2.4x**, net exposure **12.3% → 11.4%**; inst. owners 55.9% active. MS Alpha Models unchanged at **4/5 BEST (24m) / 3/5 MOST (3m)** — still the weakest quintile scores of MS's internet names.** Follows "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC" (07-27, on this page) and "Global Thematics: Weighing In: Open-Weights Models & 3 States of the World" (08-03); companion note "What Did We Learn and What Matters Next… AMZN/GOOG/META from Here" (08-11) is referenced but NOT on this page. (Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak, 2026-08-12) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260812_2000.html) |

They are doing deals with [[SPCX]]. They're doing deals with other unnamed third parties, basically sort of NEO CLOUDS… which is a drag on margins because they have to pay a VERY HIGH PRICE in the case of SpaceX for that compute."** GS models **a margin step-down "at least towards the middle of next year" before more modest expansion resumes**, and is deliberately not underwriting the 50-55%+ incremental margins the buy-side is extrapolating. **Positioning: GS is "very bullish on the hyperscalers at these levels"; on Google — "we like Google a lot as well, and increasingly the RISK REWARD ON GOOGLE LOOKS VERY ATTRACTIVE" — but [[AMZN]] is preferred as "the cleanest pure play" on value migrating to the AI infrastructure layer.** Also confirms the mix-shift mechanic that lifted margins recently: the low-margin **[[ANTHROPIC]]/[[OPENAI]] TRAINING workloads (~15% EBITDA, GS estimate) are lapping**, and broader/non-AI adoption is carrying growth. Fubon says Google goes to Intel because TSMC CANNOT supply; Bernstein says TSMC is CHOOSING not to defend: "TSMC won't build capacity to defend share. Instead, TSMC strategically lets CoWoS proliferate so that its front-end service won't have a downstream bottleneck."** ⚠️ **Same outcome, opposite mechanism, and the difference matters for how durable Google's second source is: a capacity shortfall closes when capacity arrives; a deliberate concession does not. Logged as FUNDA's citation, NOT adopted.** **THE FRAMEWORK, which is the actual contribution: the market is evaluating AI "through an application lens, looking for the next killer use case and checking whether current revenue covers capital expenditure," while the industry's objective is AGI via recursive self-improvement — so capex is a DUAL investment in current cloud returns AND an OPTION on having sufficient compute when the RSI threshold arrives, and "compute becomes the least compressible constraint and the deepest competitive moat." For competitive structure: if the frontier iterates monthly, "distillation from black-box outputs alone may be insufficient to replicate a frontier lab's complete learning mechanism, data pipeline, and experimentation stack… staying competitive in an RSI regime requires native self-improvement capability: your own flywheel, your own experimental stack, your own compute" — the strongest third-party statement of the vertical-integration/TPU case on this page. Also introduces a demand category absent from every capex model here: EXPERIMENT COMPUTE (architecture search, data mixture, hyperparameter sweeps, ablations), where "fewer chips… means an order-of-magnitude fewer attempts."** ⚠️ **FUNDA's discipline retained, because it is what makes the note usable: "strategic necessity is not the same thing as shareholder return… an arms race that is individually rational can compress industry-level returns." Scenario ladder: RSI ~2027 (most likely), 2029+ (capex digestion; "warrants serious weight"), longer/unsolved (impairment; tail). Also carried: FUNDA's capex basis is ~$725B for the four largest in 2026 (+~77% y/y, absorbing the majority of operating cash flow) — a SECONDARY aggregation that sits BELOW JPM's ~$750bn desk tracker already on this page (07-30); use the primary.** **NOT re-added to the body: Google Cloud +82%, which FUNDA cites as return support and which this page already carries from the 2Q26 release.** (FUNDA "Deep\|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Funda_on_RSI.html) |

| 08-03 | **Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), "industry highlights & checks update" — the PRIMARY call transcript behind the 2028-TPU-target row below** | supply | **bull** | ⚠️ **Machine transcript (ASR) — names garbled; corrected inline on first use.** **The relay already gave you the headline (12-15mn TPUs in 2028, four-compute-die V9, 30-35mn compute dies, Intel a must). The primary adds the ROADMAP DATES, the VENDOR SPLIT, and — the genuinely new leg — a RACK-ASSEMBLY bottleneck at the ODM that this page did not carry at all.** **Verbatim on the target, unchanged: "despite market concerns regarding Google's CAPEX and negative free cash flow, we observe that Google has a very aggressive target to deploy 12 to 15 million TPUs in 2028… entering 2028, Google's TPU will advance to the V9 generation, and in the V9 generation, it has four compute dies. So that suggests Google's capacity consumption in 2028 will be more than doubled compared to 2027. In compute die calculation, the total compute dies will likely reach 30 to 35 million."** ⚠️ **Note the framing matters for this page's capex/FCF debate: Fubon presents the 12-15mn target as evidence AGAINST the market's Google-capex-retrenchment worry, i.e. the supply chain is being asked to build for more, not less, at exactly the moment the equity market is punishing the capex line.** **Net-new (1) — ROADMAP DATES: "the V8 will be in production by Q4 this year and the V9 will be in early 28, I think Q1'28."** So V8 volume starts **Q4'26** and V9 lands **Q1'28**, with the V9 chip **not yet taped out — "I think it will be tape out by the end of this year"** (which is also why every 2028 number on this page should be treated as pre-tape-out). **Net-new (2) — VENDOR SPLIT, demand-side only: "from the demand side I think Google will probably allocate 4 million to even 5 million for MediaTek"** of the 12-15mn ([[MEDIATEK]]) ⚠️ **but Fubon is emphatic that this is a DEMAND allocation, not a production forecast — "in the supply chain, we don't yet have the real data point for 2028 capacity allocation… from the production side, we should still closely monitor Intel's expansion, TSMC's expansion." Do not read 4-5mn as a shipment estimate.** 🔴 **Net-new (3) — THE ODM / L11 RACK-ASSEMBLY BOTTLENECK, from Arthur's checks, and it is a Google-specific execution risk this page has never logged: "Hon Hai show[ed] us the… major of L11 TPU assembling. But because his capacity extension speed, the learning curve is not so much. So Hon Hai probably will be get the V8 rack system for TPU for 2027… Google is very aggressive for 2027, 2028, so he need more supplier to help to do L11 TPU rack assembling."** ⚠️ **Read it as: Google's incumbent rack assembler is learning-curve-limited, so Hon Hai gets the V8 rack in 2027 and Google must ADD ODMs to hit 2028 — a supplier-qualification gate sitting downstream of both the TSMC-CoWoS and Intel-EMIB gates this page already tracks. Silicon capacity is necessary but not sufficient; someone has to build the racks.** **Net-new (4) — the memory-despec check covers Google explicitly: ODMs (Hon Hai and "Weichuang" [ASR-garbled Taiwanese ODM]) looking at 2027 specs "don't see any spec change for memory… including the Google, AWS."** Relevant because the standing bear objection to the memory up-cycle has been that hyperscalers despec to absorb cost inflation; the buyers of the parts say no, and they are budgeting at full rack prices ([[NVDA]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md)). **Net-new (5) — the "supply-constrained yet selling TPUs externally" tension, and the answer given is a DEFERRAL, which is how it should be recorded: asked how Google can be supply-constrained and simultaneously sell TPUs to third parties (inventory-build risk?), Shang does not answer from his own checks — he points to TSMC management's earnings-call line, that "the production plan is based on… discussion, negotiation with all the customers to make sure there is no overbooking, double booking… and the chip won't become inventory."** ⚠️ **Log the limits with it: this is a foundry's account of its own order book, offered second-hand by a supply-chain analyst, about a customer's end-demand. It is not an independent check and it does not address the specific question of whether externally-sold TPUs compete with Google's internal allocation. The tension stays open.** Reconfirmed without change: **Google plans 400G/448G SerDes in V10** (and see [AVGO.md](AVGO.md) — Fubon has seen Broadcom's V10 design, 8 [2]nm compute dies / 6,000W TDP, and has NOT seen MediaTek's); **TSMC alone cannot meet the target ("TSMC's production bottleneck"), making Intel's supply "a must" by 2028**, with Intel EMIB capacity/yield the single biggest swing factor — Intel detail on [INTC.md](INTC.md), MediaTek share/ASP/margin on [MEDIATEK.md](MEDIATEK.md), TSMC capacity on [TSM.md](TSM.md). (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), "industry highlights & checks update", 2026-08-03) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_03_sherman_update_03_08_26.html) |
| 08-03 | **Jefferies/Fubon group call (Sherman Shang) — THE 2028 TPU TARGET, AND WHY IT NEEDS [[INTC]]** | produto | **bull** | 🔴 **"GOOGL [has a] VERY AGGRESSIVE TARGET TO DEPLOY 12-15MN TPU IN 2028. V9 has 4 COMPUTE DIES — implies consumption to MORE THAN DOUBLE in 2027. Total compute dies to reach 30-35MN. DIFFICULT TO REACH THIS TARGET FROM [[TSM]] ALONE — makes INTC'S SUPPLY A MUST BY 2028."** **The capacity arithmetic behind it: Intel's EMIB capacity reaches 10-12k/month by end-2026 with a plan to DOUBLE to 24-25k/month by end-2027; Fubon's checks show Intel "being MORE AGGRESSIVE in capacity expansion (doubling in both 2027/28)," with positive commentary from Unimicron and Ibiden expected to follow with 2027/28 capex.** ⚠️ **The 30-35mn total-compute-die figure is the number to carry rather than the 12-15mn unit figure — the v9 four-die architecture is what makes this a packaging-capacity story rather than a wafer story, and it is why the [[MEDIATEK]] allocation and the Intel EMIB-T ramp are now the two gating items on Google's own AI roadmap.** **NET-NEW CAUSAL EXPLANATION, from the trade press: "Google turned to Intel for EMIB packaging BECAUSE NVIDIA PRE-BOOKED OVER 50% OF TSMC'S COWOS PACKAGING FOR THE NEXT 3 YEARS," with market researchers "putting TPU demand at 15 MILLION BY 2028, MORE THAN NVIDIA'S CURRENT 13 MILLION PER YEAR" (@dnystedt, 2026-08-02, 506 likes).** ⚠️ **Media-sourced and the 13mn NVDA comparison is loose (Fubon has NVDA total chip output at 12.4mn in 2027) — but the displacement logic is the important part: the EMIB-T decision reads as a capacity constraint at TSMC rather than a technology preference, which is more durable than a yield race and less durable than a technical moat. Also: [[TSM]] is now working on its OWN in-house EMIB solution, "still very early stage, no production timeline yet."** **MediaTek side of the same call: CoWoS allocation flagged for upside (137.7k wafers 2027), custom-ASIC share 17-18% 2027 → 30-32% 2028, and — the surprise — 400G SerDes ready in 2H27, "potentially for V10," a year earlier than Fubon expected.** Cross-file [[INTC]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[TSM]], [[AVGO]], [[NVDA]], [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md). (Jefferies/Fubon · Sherman Shang, 2026-08-03) |
| 08-02 | **J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan — GUC upgrade to OW (read-through note)** | produto | **bull** | **★ The best-sourced account yet of how Google is RE-ARCHITECTING ITS SILICON SUPPLY CHAIN, and it is a deliberate disaggregation of the merchant-ASIC model. "Google's SYSTEMATIC EMBRACE OF A CUSTOMER-OWNED TOOLING (CoT) MODEL for its AI ASICs is creating more opportunities for design service vendors such as GUC and Alchip, likely in the TPU v10 family."** The stated end-state: **"through the evolution from TPUv8 to v10 families, the BLACK-BOX MODEL FROM US FABLESS VENDOR (where IP, design service, manufacturing and packaging value add are all bundled together AT HIGH MARGINS) will be BROKEN DOWN SYSTEMATICALLY in favor of a disaggregated approach."** ⚠️ **That is a direct strategic attack on [[AVGO]]'s economics at its single largest ASIC customer, stated by the analyst who covers the Asian supply chain rather than inferred from the tape.** **For TPU v10 Icefish, JPM's checks describe SEVERAL engagement models under one RFQ: (i) the PRIMARY version still semi-CoT (Google's in-house team on the compute die; [[MEDIATEK]] supplying SerDes, D2D, packaging design, integration and foundry support); (ii) compute-die BACK-END design outsourced to GUC/Alchip on a Turnkey-3 model; (iii) a full break-up where "only IP being sourced by Google separately… with FOUNDRY PROCUREMENT BEING HANDLED DIRECTLY BY GOOGLE, SIMILAR TO THE AMAZON TRAINIUM APPROACH."** ⚠️ **Option (iii) is the one to watch: if Google buys wafers from [[TSM]] directly, the ASIC vendor's revenue line shrinks toward pure IP+services and Google captures the manufacturing margin — the same logic by which Google already buys its HBM directly. JPM's own ranking of who wins: MediaTek takes "the highest value capture… in the next 3-4 years," GUC/Alchip benefit "somewhat," and for NON-TPU projects "the opportunity for a full-CoT approach could be even higher, given lower design complexity and IP dependence, AS ALREADY ILLUSTRATED FOR THE GOOGLE AXION CPUs."** **THE AXION CPU RAMP IS THE NET-NEW HARD NUMBER SET, and it is bigger than the page carried: Google "indicated that it would DOUBLE THE NUMBER OF PHYSICAL CPU HOSTS PER SERVER, moving to its custom Axion Arm-based CPUs" — the CPU:TPU ratio has gone to 1:2 from 1:4 from TPU v8 onward, with Axion 2 now pairing with TPU v8t and v8i. On 9.6mn v8-series TPU units, that implies ~4.8mn units of Axion 2 demand across 2026-27. GUC ships ~1mn in 2026 (~US$1bn, ~half its revenue) leaving ~3.8mn for 2027; the next-generation Axion ramps early 2027 at ~2x the current ASP; JPM models Google CPU revenue at GUC reaching ~US$4.6bn by 2028 (~70% of GUC revenue), 4x the 2026 level. Axion 3 is described as a TWO-DIE PACKAGE vs Axion 2's one die.** ⚠️ **Two constraints named on Google's own side: "a downside risk to this simulation is [[TSM]]'s CAPACITY SUFFICIENCY," and JPM flags potential share loss to SAMSUNG FOUNDRY in the 2028-29 window — i.e. Google's CPU roadmap is now large enough that leading-edge allocation, not design, is the binding constraint. A named catalyst is "an increase in 3NM ALLOCATION AT TSMC FOR GOOGLE CPUs IN 2027."** Second-source corroboration the same day: **Nomura · Aaron Jeng upgraded GUC to Buy calling Google CPU alone a US$3.8BN 2027 opportunity — "nearly as big as 2027 consensus estimate for the whole company" — and noted GUC's new Chairlady, at the 31 July earnings meeting, "DID NOT DENY the company's opportunity in Google's TPU (which from our industry survey MIGHT BE TPU v.10)."** Cross-filed [[MEDIATEK]], [[AVGO]], [[TSM]], [[ARM]], [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md). |

| 08-01 | **"Frozen V2" — a name and an architecture for the Gemini-optimised chip, and it DITCHES CoWoS** | produto | **bull** | ⚠️ **A press relay that appears to put technical detail on The Information's 07-31 scoop logged directly below: "Google's 'FROZEN V2' TPU HARDWIRES SRAM ONTO SILICON, DITCHING [[TSM]]'s CoWoS PACKAGING FOR GEMINI"** (wccftech, via Susquehanna · Hosseini Tech Hardware weekly, 2026-08-01). **If accurate, the design choice is the story: hardwiring SRAM on-die instead of attaching HBM through advanced packaging trades total memory capacity for bandwidth, latency, power and — critically — SUPPLY CHAIN INDEPENDENCE. It would remove a Gemini-inference part from both the CoWoS queue and the HBM queue simultaneously.** ⚠️ **Read the read-throughs carefully because they run in several directions at once, and none of them is established yet: (a) NEGATIVE for [[TSM]] CoWoS demand and for HBM suppliers ([[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[MU]]) at the margin, IF this is a volume part rather than a specialised one; (b) it is the natural engineering answer to the cost pressure the [[MEDIATEK]] call flagged — Google buys its HBM directly, so Google carries the HBM cost inflation itself and has the strongest incentive of any ASIC customer to design it out; (c) it CUTS AGAINST MediaTek management's own statement on the 07-31 call that "CSP still requiring HBM instead of any solutions so would still be the mainstream in the near term" — logged as a live contradiction between a vendor's characterisation of its customer and a report about that customer; (d) it does NOT obviously bear on the v9/v10 accelerator line MediaTek and [[AVGO]] compete for, since an SRAM-only inference part is a different product class from a training accelerator.** **NOT ADOPTED — single press relay, no company confirmation, no volume, no date, and the naming ("Frozen V2") is unconfirmed against Google's own designations. Logged as the highest-priority item to verify on this page, because if it is a volume Gemini-serving part it changes the packaging and memory read-through from Google's capex.** Cross-filed [[TSM]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[AVGO]], [[themes/cowos-packaging]]. |

Google has agreed to cover as much as $44bn of lease payments on datacentres owned by others if tenants default — up from $6.5bn at end-September — to drive sales of its TPUs as an [[NVDA]] alternative to [[ANTHROPIC]] and others.** Structure: **off-balance-sheet, notional $43.8bn but only $815m carried at fair value**; covers **~2.4 gigawatts across roughly 10 not-yet-complete projects** (former crypto miners — Hut 8, TeraWulf, Cypher — with **Fluidstack typically leasing, filling with TPUs and subletting to Anthropic**). It lets developers secure cheaper debt and build faster; **Google often takes equity warrants in return (14% of TeraWulf, 5.4% of Cypher).** **Balance-sheet context: Alphabet debt rose to $98bn at June 30 from $23.6bn a year earlier; it issued equity for the first time in over two decades; and it posted its FIRST-EVER NEGATIVE FREE CASH FLOW QUARTER as a public company — with a further $24.1bn of backstops and $85.2bn of yet-to-commence leases still to come.** Rothschild rates GOOG **Buy**. Market context from the same note: a Goldman/Blue Owl/Skadden panel pegged the AI buildout at **$7.5tn over five years with over $1tn raised YTD (vs $555bn in all of 2025)**, while the IG bond market shows **'digestion issues' — NVDA, [[SPCX]] and [[AMZN]] each sold $25bn within weeks, widening spreads 20-40bps** — and the panel's key point was that **power and permitting, not capital, are the binding constraints.** (Rothschild & Co · Rowan Adley, 2026-07-27) |

| **08-05/06** | **🔴 GOOGLE DEEPMIND LEADERSHIP OVERHAUL — Hassabis to chairman/Alphabet chief scientist, Kavukcuoglu takes GDM, JEFF DEAN LEAVES (The Information · Erin Woo, 08-05/06; Reuters via @firstadopter; UBS TECH desk; Bernstein TMT; MS TMT; Redburn)** | **capital / competicao** | **mixed → bear** | **★ THE EVENT OF THE WINDOW. Stock −4% on the day. The facts, then the two readings, because the desks split hard and the split is the useful part.** **FACTS: Sundar Pichai announced (1) Demis Hassabis steps aside as Google DeepMind CEO to become its CHAIRMAN and CHIEF SCIENTIST OF ALPHABET; (2) Koray Kavukcuoglu — GDM CTO and Google's Chief AI Architect — takes over GDM as Senior Vice President, "directly leading day-to-day operations… overseeing Gemini model development, frontier research"; (3) longtime chief scientist JEFF DEAN LEAVES to co-found a startup.** Reuters detail via @firstadopter (08-05): **Hassabis "will explore research and strategy related to societal impacts of AGI and will have FEW DIRECT REPORTS," and said in his memo he would spend more time with ISOMORPHIC LABS**, where he remains CEO. @firstadopter also reports **"Dean, Sanjay Ghemawat, Oriol Vinyals and Quoc Le — some of Google's most famous and lauded AI and engineering leaders — left the company to launch a startup called DISCOVERY LOOP"** ⚠️ *(the multi-person departure and the startup name are press-relay, not company-confirmed — The Information's own item names only Dean)*. **Context stack: Noam Shazeer left for [[OPENAI]] earlier this summer; John Jumper, Hassabis's co-Nobel laureate on AlphaFold, recently left for [[ANTHROPIC]]** (The Information, 08-06). — **THE BEAR READ:** @firstadopter, 08-05: **"Anyone who thinks that Jeff Dean, Sanjay Ghemawat, Oriol Vinyals, and Quoc Le leaving, and Demis Hassabis losing operational control isn't an earthquake-level negative event for Google is delusional."** The Information's own framing is closer to this than to the shrug: **"The changes are likely to strike a blow to morale at Google DeepMind, which has already seen a string of departures in recent weeks, and could complicate Google's efforts to catch up in the race to develop the best AI models, particularly if more researchers leave as a result. Google has fallen behind in the AI model race, PARTICULARLY IN CODING. Its next flagship model has been DELAYED as rivals Anthropic and OpenAI have released new models that are strong on coding and cybersecurity"** (The Information · Erin Woo, 08-06). **CNBC (via @firstadopter, 08-06) adds the mechanism and it lands directly on this page's TPU-monetisation thesis: "not everyone is celebrating inside of Google… Some researchers have grown frustrated over ACCESS TO THE COMPUTING CAPACITY they need to pursue ambitious projects WHILE WATCHING GOOGLE CLOUD SELL TPUs TO OUTSIDE CUSTOMERS, INCLUDING [[ANTHROPIC]]," plus bureaucracy ("layers of approval required to move research into products").** ⚠️ **That is the merchant-TPU strategy creating an internal-talent cost — a second-order effect the TPU-as-a-Service bull case (Barclays/Sandler) does not price.** — **THE SHRUG:** The Information's Martin Peers, same day: **"In reality, today's news doesn't mean much. Hassabis wasn't the one running DeepMind on a day-to-day basis. That was really Koray Kavukcuoglu… As for Dean, my colleague Erin Woo tells me the chatter internally is that DEAN HAD BECOME LESS CENTRAL TO GOOGLE'S GEMINI EFFORTS, even if he remains revered by employees."** Peers reframes the real question as **succession: Hassabis had been on the short list to succeed Pichai alongside Cloud chief Thomas Kurian — "if Hassabis prefers the role of scientist to that of divisional CEO, he'd hardly want to be Alphabet's CEO."** — **DESK READS:** MS TMT (Wigg, 08-06) frames the debate as **"GOOGL debate / frontier 'brain drain' vs infrastructure winner"** — i.e. the bull case is being re-underwritten on TPUs/Cloud rather than on model leadership. Redburn (08-06) logs it flatly: **"Google (Buy): Deepmind leadership reshuffle; Jeff Dean + Sanjay Ghemawat depart for own startup."** UBS TECH desk (Ruple, 08-06): **"GOOG −4% weighed on megacap sentiment after announcing a sweeping AI leadership overhaul."** @dylan522p's DeepMind contact, deadpan: **"Everytime Googlers leave, the TPU ecosystem grows stronger" — "Deepmind friend coping so hard in my DMs rn"** (08-06). @jukan05, 08-06: **"Google today feels like Meta when it was assembling MSL, while Meta today feels like Google when it was rolling out Gemini 3."** — **NET FOR THE PAGE: this does not change a number, and no broker changed a rating or PT on it in this flow. It changes the burden of proof on ONE thing — Gemini 4. The page already carried "coding gap acknowledged" and "silence on 3.5 Pro deafening" (Jefferies, 07-22) as the open model-quality question; the shake-up plus the muted-Gemini-4 chatter converts that from a quarter-to-quarter miss into a leadership-and-capability question. Kavukcuoglu speaks publicly at The Information's AI Agenda Live in SF on 2026-09-23 — a dated, checkable venue for the first real read on the new structure.** |

| 07-23 | **Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak — post-2Q26 CALLBACK (hosted by Tom Wigg + Skyler Risom; PRIMARY transcript)** | capital | **mixed** | **PARTIAL DUPLICATE of the "4 Learnings and 2 Catalysts" row directly above — same PT ($400, OW) and same $375bn 2027 capex. This row carries ONLY what the written note does not.** ★ **WHY THE STREET-HIGH 2027 CAPEX WENT UP AGAIN ($350bn → $375bn), in Nowak's own words, and the reason is a margin-of-safety judgement, not inflation: "26's number is going up because they're getting delivery of SOME MORE CHIPS… it's NOT INFLATION, they are getting access to some chips and racks SOONER than they thought… The reason why I took up 27 is because we take up this year and we look at the IMPLIED CAPEX NEXT YEAR to both handle chips coming on in 28 as well as PRE-BUILD for future chips. IT WAS GETTING LOW. It was getting to the point where it was ONLY 10 TO 15% OF THE CAPEX NEXT YEAR WAS FOR CHIPS TO BE DELIVERED BEYOND 28 — and that's MUCH LOWER THAN WHAT WE SEE AT THE OTHER HYPERSCALERS."** ★ **AND A SECOND, MORE INTERESTING REASON THAT CUTS AGAINST THE MERCHANT-TPU THESIS: "in our 350 previously, we were assuming they were going to SELL 4 GIGAWATTS OF TPU TO THIRD PARTIES IN 28. And the more I listen to them about capacity constraints, I DO WONDER IF THEY'LL PERHAPS USE MORE OF THE TPU GIGAWATTS ON PREM, WHICH MEANS THEY HAVE TO BUILD FOR THAT."** ⚠️ **A merchant-TPU bull should note this: the capex take-up is partly a bet that FEWER TPUs get sold externally.** ★ **ON GEMINI — the clearest statement of what management actually did to the 3.5 Pro timeline: "they DID PUSH OUT THE TIMELINE… they TALKED DOWN THREE AND A HALF. What that does is it PUSHES OUT WHEN WE COULD SEE THE MODEL THAT WILL REALLY RE-RATE SENTIMENT BACK TO A LOW-20s MULTIPLE. Timeline on four… we're pre-training now. The typical cadence would be fourth quarter. MY GUT IS PROBABLY LIKE NOVEMBER, DECEMBER."** He does NOT read it as a frontier retreat ("I don't think their focus on the frontier has changed at all"). ★ **ON SEARCH: "we think search DECELERATES DOWN TO 13 BY THE TIME YOU GET TO THE FOURTH QUARTER just given the comp structure… you're going to go from comping 12 to 17." There's a LEGAL CHARGE in there… there's no nefarious thing you have to put in the model next quarter or next year."** (Corroborates the $1.5bn PriceRunner charge already in Current state.) ★ **SPACEX MECHANICS: all of it books through GOOGLE CLOUD; "it begins to build in September, ramps over the course of September as capacity comes on, and then by the beginning of October… THE RUN RATE IS $920 MILLION A MONTH" held through 4Q and 1Q, "and we are assuming that Google OPTS OUT AFTER THE SIX-MONTH MINIMUM."** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_23_nowak_2q26_google_callback.html) |

Full model rebuild: total revenue **+12% to $650B '27 (from $580B)**, **+40% to $950B '28 (from $680B)**; **EBITDA +7%/+23%**; **EPS FY27 $15.40 (from $13.94), FY28 $21.88 (from $15.98)**; could lift **'28 consensus gross profit ~15%** and OI by more. Frame in Sandler's words: **"TPU-aaS is where Google takes inventory and sells TPUs directly to customers as a merchant vendor"** — initially with **[[ANTHROPIC]]**, potentially others later. **Capital-efficiency kicker worth isolating:** the asset-light structure "may reverse some future capex growth (potentially downside to our $500B '28 estimate)" — the first argument on this page that the merchant model **relieves rather than compounds** the FCF problem, which is the direct counterweight to the capex-raise bear case. **Ecosystem winners Sandler names:** Anthropic (vertical integration, **less exposed to restrictions around hyperscaler service agreements**), **[[AVGO]]** (substantial new revenue pool + diversification beyond Google), **Blackstone and others** (scale up AI-infra investment vehicles), and **Fluidstack** (massively expands as a colo/managed-service "smart hands" provider outside Google's walls — Sandler reads its recent fundraise as signalling **intent to become a TPU neocloud**). ⚠️ His evidence line cites **$811B purchase commitments (+$479B q/q)**, which does not tie to the filing's **$707.0B (+$474B)** — use $707.0B as the house figure. |
| 07-24 | Barclays desk · Justin Kantrowitz (Consumer/Internet, NOT research) | valuation | mixed | **Buy-side positioning colour after the -6.9%.** "Most investors didn't seem surprised by the reaction in the stock yesterday but **the moves in AMZN and META (along with others) was not expected.**" Positioning: **"most investors expect the stock to be a source of funds in the near term as we get past the hurdle of seeing negative FCF and knowing that's going to worsen"** — decent long-only two-way flow, with **some dollars rotating into AMZN**. **A mechanism floated and then debunked:** the theory that funds owning too much GOOGL across *both* equity and hyperscaler debt were trimming the equity leg — Kantrowitz **polled four long-only investors, all said no such dynamic existed at their firm.** His own view is the mirror of Sandler's note: "investors are trying to dig through the purchase commitments and figure out what kind of revenue could be on the come… **investors seem to only focus on the balance sheet impact and seem to [be] missing the potential ramp in revenues that are coming.**" Separately flags oil >$100 as a headwind for **AMZN / DASH / [[UBER]]**. Also notes Barclays is "the only [house] modeling out the revenues from TPUs." |
| 07-24 | Rothschild & Co · Rowan Adley (TMT Daily) | valuation | bear | Tape record for the post-print session: **GOOG -6.9%**, TSLA -14.5%, **SPX -1.2%, NDX -1.9%, SOX -54bps (very choppy), IGV -2.2%**; US TMT momentum **+2.2%** (shorts/software underperforming). **"Asian memory stocks v weak overnight, dragging the KOSPI -5.7%. I didn't see much specific newsflow there, so maybe it was just a reaction to the US session, or even the Anthropic datapoint"** — Adley's TickerTrends read that **Anthropic ARR sequential growth has slowed significantly** (unverified alt-data; see [[ANTHROPIC]]). Macro: oil back above $100, US 10yr 4.7% approaching 4.8% resistance; NDX support at 28,196. Relevant because Anthropic is the anchor TPU-aaS customer — an ARR wobble at Anthropic is now a GOOGL backlog-quality question, not just an Anthropic question. |
| 07-24 | @FT / @CNBC | capital | bear | Mainstream framing of the same print: **"Google burning through cash for the first time since going public decades ago as it raised its spending forecast for AI infrastructure"** (@FT). CNBC: "Shares of Alphabet and Tesla fell in premarket… after both firms signalled increased AI spending, **unnerving investors worried about the mounting costs of the artificial intelligence boom**" — Alphabet ~-4% premarket, Tesla -5%+. Also of note: **"7 of the 10 most shorted bonds in the IG market are hyperscalers, with 6+% AA GOOGL 56s the most shorted bond"** (@zerohedge, 07-22) — the capital-structure expression of the same bear case. |
| 07-26 | UBS · Karl Keirstead ("Microsoft Corp. — Update on Azure, Capex and M365") | demanda | mixed | **A Microsoft note that is the best independent decomposition of Google's Cloud beat so far.** UBS attributes the print in four parts: **"the 2Q26 results from Google Cloud, with revs growth of +82% boosted by core demand but also by the sale of Gemini model access, Gemini Enterprise subscriptions and even TPU hardware sales"** — its structural point being that hyperscalers earn materially from acting as "front-ends to the models and 1P products (such as Claude Code) from OpenAI and Anthropic." **On the token-deflation bear it cuts both ways: "This was certainly not evident in Google Cloud's 2Q26 results (growth accelerated to +82%) but much of the excessive enterprise token spend appears to be in coding use cases for which Gemini models are less dominant"** — acceleration is real evidence against the drag, but possibly because Google is under-exposed to the workload being optimized. **On capex, Google is the Street's cautionary tale: the "Street is not rewarding the stocks of AI infra providers that signal higher capex spend… (see Oracle and Google)"**, MSFT "may need to inch up capex estimates again, **much like Google did on its call this past week**," and "capex raises from both [Google and Oracle] were **not greeted with applause**." **On funding, a mildly constructive read of the June raise: "The Google and Oracle equity transactions have raised questions as to whether Microsoft may be forced to raise external capital… Google did so at what was an all-time share price high"** (MSFT ~30% off its highs). **Customer/partner checks:** "**We're really pushing Google to diversify against Microsoft**… Google has come out with some really attractive incentives"; a customer cutting Azure 5-10% says "**Google is increasing 20% per year because of Workspace but also their simulation tools, TPUs, and algorithms.**" Also names Google with [[OPENAI]] and [[ANTHROPIC]] as the three frontier labs whose direct enterprise relationships concentrate power away from hyperscalers — the one risk on which Google sits on both sides. (UBS · Karl Keirstead, 2026-07-26) |
| 07-27 | UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Timothy Arcuri et al ("Can Intel challenge TSMC's advanced packaging leadership?") | produto | mixed | **The third and most EMIB-T-positive view of TPU v9 packaging, and it contradicts the two already on the page.** UBS makes **Google TPU v9 — developed by [[MEDIATEK]] — the flagship first major EXTERNAL product for [[INTC]]'s EMIB-T**, with Intel expected to be "selective in client engagement, prioritizing internal products and Google TPU v9… set for mass production in 2028" and to "prioritize focus on ramping up the key external products like Google TPUs in **2028-29** at its own facilities." (The same note elsewhere says EMIB-T is "slated for mass production in **late 2027**, with Google's TPU v9 expected to be its first major external product" — both dates carried, not reconciled.) **Yields, from trial production runs of MediaTek's TPU v9 prototype: EMIB-T packaging ~90% (headline "~90-92% validation yields") but EMIB-T substrate fabrication only ~50%, vs >80% for HPC ABF substrates** — and UBS concedes HPC packaging yields "ideally should improve to the high 90% range." **The offset is capacity, not yield: Ibiden's ¥220bn Gama plant (Feb-2026), EMIB-T MP late-2027 / meaningful ramp 2028 — "demand from Google TPU programs alone could largely absorb the planned capacity."** **Sizing (stress-test scenario, Figures 5/16): Intel EMIB-T packaging revenue attributable to Google TPU of US$5,003mn (2028E) / $8,374mn (2029E) / $13,159mn (2030E) on 2.3 / 3.5 / 5.0mn chipsets at $2,175 / $2,393 / $2,632 EMIB-T content each** (2030's 6.0mn total chipsets = the "~24mn accelerator dies at four XPUs per package" in the figure header); **lower case $5,003 / $10,766 / $17,106mn on 2.3 / 4.5 / 6.5mn chipsets.** **Durability caveat from UBS itself: "TSMC's share could further increase if Google's new TPUs (eg. v10) return to CoPoS and adopt CPO integration"** — the win may be one generation, not structural. ⚠️ Read against **Fubon (07-08)** — 95%+ yield bar "very challenging," Google falls back to [[TSM]] 2028 CoWoS — and **JPM/Gokul (07-08)** — next-gen TPU stays on CoWoS, Qualcomm/MediaTek-packaged; **and against UBS's own semis desk the same day (Arcuri, 07-27), which calls EMIB-T "a process we think still suffers from very low yields and immaturity."** Three views, none adopted. (UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Timothy Arcuri et al, 2026-07-27) |

**Quarter synthesis (Q2'26 →):** the print was an operational bull — Cloud **+82% / $514B backlog** cleared the +70% bogey and **TPU-system revenue started recognizing** — but management **RAISED FY26 capex to $195-205B (from $180-190B)** and guided FY27 up "significantly," so the capital-intensity / FCF debate (**FCF −$5.9B** in Q2) stays the swing factor. **Post-print (07-22, stock −3 to −4%): the reaction was "clean quarter, but where's the incremental upside" — GOOGL is now the funding short of the megacaps.** The sell-off triggered on the *surprise* capex-guide raise (most expected a reiteration) + no EPS-upside revisions, compounded by a decelerating backlog (+11% q/q vs +96%), a ~$900M/month SPCX 3rd-party-compute margin drag into Q3/Q4, and the **Gemini 4 delay (4-6 months)** with 3.5 Pro AWOL. PT moves were still up: **Barclays/Sandler $405→$425 (OW), Jefferies/Wong BUY $445**, DB/Black Buy $430 (pre-print, FY27 capex →$325B). The one unambiguous positive: the capex raise + bullish-2027 tone is a **+ve read for the AI-semi value chain** (compute/memory/optical/semicap; JPM tags APH/ANET/CLS/CIEN/CSCO/COHR/GLW/CRDO/LITE). **07-23: MS (Nowak) crystallised the funding-short read — PT cut $415→$400, '27/'28 EPS lowered 7%/5%** because higher investment (MS now models '27 capex $375bn, up from $350bn, + the ~$1bn/$3bn SPCX opex drag) outweighs the faster Cloud revenue; the offsetting positives were the TPU debit/credit accounting confirmation (GOOGL builds inventory, sells at a gross margin, majority ~3.5GW '27 / ~4GW '28) and mgmt's flagged monthly model-ship cadence at Gemini 4. Watch: C27 capex quantification (buyside now $325B+; MS $375bn), the TPU-revenue ramp (2027-weighted), the Q3 3rd-party-capacity/SPCX margin bridge, and Gemini 3.5 Pro / Gemini 4 cadence vs the acknowledged coding gap. **07-26/27 adds two things on opposite ends of the story.** On demand, **UBS (Keirstead, 07-26)** gives the first independent decomposition of the +82%: core demand **plus** Gemini model-API access, Gemini Enterprise subscriptions **and** TPU hardware — and notes the acceleration is direct evidence the token-optimization drag is not yet visible at Google, while cautioning that the optimized workload (coding) is precisely where Gemini is "less dominant." The same note makes Google the sector's cautionary tale on capital intensity — the Street "is not rewarding the stocks of AI infra providers that signal higher capex spend (see Oracle and Google)" — with the one offset that the June equity came "at what was an all-time share price high." On supply, **UBS's Asia packaging team (Lin/Abrams/Arcuri, 07-27)** opens a genuine three-way disagreement over TPU v9 packaging: UBS makes v9 (MediaTek) Intel's flagship external EMIB-T product with ~90% packaging yield already demonstrated on prototype runs and Ibiden capacity — not yield — the gate, against Fubon's (07-08) "falls back to TSMC 2028 CoWoS" and JPM/Gokul's (07-08) "stays on CoWoS." UBS's own semis desk sits on the Fubon side the same day. **Do not resolve it — the 2028 v9 packaging path is now an explicit open question, and UBS itself flags the EMIB-T win may last one generation ("TSMC's share could further increase if Google's new TPUs (eg. v10) return to CoPoS and adopt CPO integration").** **08-03 (run-inbox, the Fubon PRIMARY call transcript) does not move the 12-15mn 2028 target or the 30-35mn compute-die figure — both reconfirmed verbatim — but it changes the shape of the risk in three ways. (1) It DATES the roadmap: V8 in production Q4'26, V9 in Q1'28, with the V9 chip not yet taped out (expected end-2026) — so every 2028 number on this page, including the ones the sell-side is now underwriting at $35-52bn of custom-ASIC revenue, is pre-tape-out. (2) It splits the target on a DEMAND basis only — 4-5mn to MediaTek — and Fubon refuses to convert that into production ("we don't yet have the real data point for 2028 capacity allocation"). (3) It adds a THIRD supply gate this page had never logged, and it is the least glamorous one: L11 TPU rack assembly. Hon Hai's capacity learning curve is too slow, so it takes the V8 rack in 2027 and Google "needs more supplier" for 2028. That matters because the page's whole 2028 debate has been fought over wafers (TSMC N2) and packaging (CoWoS vs Intel EMIB-T) — and Fubon's checks say the constraint chain extends one step further downstream, into who bolts the racks together. On the constructive side of the ledger the same call is the clearest statement yet that the supply chain is being asked to build for MORE Google capex, not less, and the ODMs report no 2027 memory spec change for Google — a direct rebuttal of the "hyperscalers will despec to absorb memory inflation" objection. One tension left explicitly unresolved: how Google can be supply-constrained and sell TPUs externally. Fubon does not answer it from his own checks; he defers to TSMC management's overbooking/double-booking assurance, which is a foundry's account of its own order book relayed second-hand — recorded as the answer given, not as an answer. **RE-EVALUATED 2026-08-12 (/run-inbox, hyperscaler cluster — six back-dated sources folded, 07-22 → 08-12).** Three things changed. **(1) The TPU-margin bear is being retired without a disclosure.** Management still won't break it out, but the Street has moved from "mid-teens EBITDA feared" to **30-40%+** in three weeks (BofA IR callback 07-23 at ~30%; GS 08-12 at "mid-thirties, probably closer to 40%+") — which removes the most-cited reason to discount Cloud's reported growth. What replaces it is a narrower, more tractable question: **how big was 2Q TPU revenue** — BofA's $0.5-0.6bn plug against Capstone's $1.2-1.3bn cost-side triangulation. That gap is now the highest-value reconciliation item on this page. **(2) The capex debate flipped from "how high" to "what is it FOR."** Nowak's callback (07-23) is explicit that $350bn→$375bn for 2027 is a **margin-of-safety** adjustment (only 10-15% of 2027 capex was pre-building for post-2028 chips, "much lower than the other hyperscalers") **and partly an assumption that Google sells FEWER TPU gigawatts externally than previously modelled (the 4GW of 3P TPU in 2028 may go on-prem instead)** — i.e. the capex raise is, at the margin, a merchant-TPU *negative*. MS's 08-04 ROIC webcast then puts Google at **25-26GW of incremental capacity, the largest of the big five, eclipsing Azure's installed base this year or early 2027**, and flags **2027 as the last significant second-derivative step-up of the cycle.** **(3) The customer side finally corroborated the TPU thesis in its own words** — an [[ANTHROPIC]] infrastructure practitioner (07-28) puts GCP TPU rental at **"less than half the price" of GPUs**, "80% of performance for 40% of the price," with stability/reliability/software now good enough for multi-week training runs, and credits the closed loop (training Gemini on TPU is what improves the merchant product). ⚠️ **What got worse is the model franchise, and it now has a third independent voice: Nowak says management "talked down 3.5 Pro" and pushed the re-rating model out to Gemini 4 in "November, December"; Mat Velloso (07-31) says Meta's newest models have "passed Google" on some tasks. Set against the Similarweb share loss (08-11), the frontier question is no longer single-source.** Near-term Search is also being marked down mechanically — Nowak models **deceleration to ~13% by 4Q on comps**, BofA ~1pt in 3Q, and both stress there is "nothing busted" in the click data (+13% in the Q).

**08-01 research-process signal:** Jeff Dean’s YC interview excerpt is a qualitative positive for the pace of agentic capability and automated ML experimentation, but it is not Gemini-specific evidence and therefore does not change the standing verdict on Google’s coding/product gap. The supplied artifact covers only 1:51 of the 57-minute episode.

**Quarter synthesis:** the fundamental thesis only got stronger (Search re-accelerating, Cloud +63%, TPU won the "AI Game of Thrones"), but the debate has shifted to *capital intensity* — the $84.75B equity raise and ~$300B FY27 capex push FCF negative, and part of the record backlog is gross TPU that only converts to revenue in 2027; bears see the multiple stuck at a $5T cap despite the best asset. **06-29: MS Nowak raises PT to $415** and publishes the most granular '28 model on the Street — 9 GW incremental capacity, 4 GW 1P TPU sales, Cloud $308B/'28 (+44% y/y), EPS $19, capex $375B/'28. The TPU revenue line is the new debate: GOOGL booking 4 GW of external TPU sales at ~$50/W implies ~$7–8B incremental cloud revenue from compute resale alone. AVGO's role (~14 GW shipped, ~9 GW TPU) confirms the scale of the Google-AVGO dependency. Maintains OW. **06-30:** the MS desk (Risom) fleshes out the bottoms-up build — ~7 GW TPU + 2 GW NVDA GPU, GOOGL retaining ~5 GW / selling ~4 GW, Core Cloud at $15/$18 per watt; Macquarie's MediaTek PT to NT$10,000 (Google TPU + a 2nd US-CSP project) adds a second ASIC-vector confirmation. **Late-June Barron's batch (06-22/06-29):** the DJIA addition (06-23, replacing Verizon), Cloud +63% (strongest since 2019), Gemini 900M users and TD Cowen's $480bn-by-2031 cloud call are all **more bullish than management's own framing** on the franchise/Cloud axis — but the AI brain-drain (Jumper→Anthropic, Shazeer→OpenAI), the $225bn worst-ever one-day loss and the $85bn raise are **more bearish than management on the talent/capital axis**. **Net read: the batch is net bullish on the business (Cloud + index recognition outweigh) but net bearish on the near-term stock/positioning** — the ~15% drawdown to $341.77 and de-rating to 23.5x fwd (from ~30x in Feb) reflect the market pricing the talent/capital risk faster than the cloud acceleration (Barron's, 2026-06-29). **06-30 close / 07-01 open:** MS's own Internet team flags GOOGL as the **worst-performing internet name over the past month (-8%, 23x '26 EPS)** even as Nowak's model raises 2027 capex to **$350B** and frames the de-rating (talent exodus + Mag-7 AI-ROIC jitters) as sentiment layered on top of, not contradicting, improving fundamentals — corroborated by the reported **~$920M/month GOOGL is paying SpaceX/xAI for compute** (evidence of just how tight supply is). **UBS (Sunny Lin, 07-01)** raises TPU unit forecasts higher than the Street's prior Jefferies-sourced numbers (4.1mn→9.0mn '26→'27 vs Jefferies' 3.1-3.2mn→5-6mn), while **JPMorgan (Hariharan, 07-01)** is the more conservative print of the batch — Google's own $7-12bn 2027 ASIC guide implies only ~10-15% TAM share, and v10 sticking with semi-CoT/no-CPO reads as an architecture lag, not a lead. **UBS (Dwyer, 07-01)** reports Google capping Meta's Gemini/Cloud usage on capacity constraints — the clearest evidence yet that "compute-constrained" is binding on paying customers, not just a guidance caveat. On the legal side, **Redburn (07-01)** flags a fresh $1.5bn Klarna antitrust judgment plus live AI-training fair-use litigation (UMG/WMG/Spotify) — incremental legal overhangs layered on the existing ad-tech/Search-distribution suits. Net: the fundamental TPU/Cloud buildout keeps getting bigger and better-monetized in the sell-side models, but supply tightness, legal overhangs and a still-unresolved brain-drain narrative keep the stock's multiple compressed even as estimates rise. **07-09:** SemiAnalysis published the most pointed *bear* framing of the quarter — an independent, explicitly-opinion take ("loser mentality from Google") arguing the majority of Google's incremental DC capacity over 2 years goes to IaaS/3p API (so DeepMind gets less training compute than OpenAI/Anthropic/MSL), that most of the $85B-raise compute is rented to customers "like Anthropic" (enabling competitors), that Gemini 3.5 Flash is "a benchmaxxed prop" and 3.5 Pro "not even Opus level on coding," and that Google's RL is "too decentralized" with talent lost to Anthropic — "the race for 3rd is Meta vs SpaceX, not Google." It's a single-shop opinion (not disclosed data) and sits *against* the sell-side's TPU/Cloud bull models, but it crystallizes the DeepMind-compute-allocation + coding-lag bear that the FundaAI (06-24) and SemiAnalysis-"EBTIT" (05-27) reads had already been building. **07-07/09 (TPU-supply-chain batch):** GS (07-09) confirms the demand side is only getting stronger — Google has been asking aggressively for TSMC wafer + CoWoS allocation over the past 3 months (driving GS's higher CoWoS/MediaTek-ASIC marks) — while the packaging path clarifies: Gokul (07-08) says the next-gen TPU **stays on CoWoS (not SOIC), Qualcomm/MediaTek-packaged**, and in-house ARM CPUs (incl. Axion) remain on simple BGA for now. On the supplier side, Hock Tan's 07-07 call insists the Broadcom-Google partnership is intact with Broadcom keeping majority volume share and dismisses the "V9"/MediaTek-A8 narrative as Taiwan PR — i.e. the two design partners are publicly talking opposite books on the Google-share split even as the underlying Google TPU demand accelerates. **07-15/07-20 (into the 07-22 print):** the sell-side setup is uniformly bullish on Cloud and rising on capex. **DB (Black, "Aiming for the Clouds")** is the meaty note — reiterate Buy/top pick $430 (29x FY27 GAAP EPS), base-case FY27 Cloud **~$190-195bn vs Street ~$142bn** (the widest house-vs-Street gap on the page), 2Q26 Cloud +70% FXN, backlog ~$462bn (+400% y/y), and **FY27 capex raised to $325bn (from ~$250bn) / FY28 $365-370bn**, with Google needing ~19GW to serve the FY27 backlog. **JPM's joint equity-&-credit call** quantifies the funding: +54% capex to ~$300bn '27 but **>$130bn of external capital ($85bn June equity + $20bn May debt + $51bn debt YTD/6 currencies) pre-funds 2027**, the $85bn raise derisking forward debt supply (credit OW, equity top pick). **BofA's Big-Tech-setups round** has Search +17.3% (street 16.8%), cloud +70%, backlog 230/8qtrs (models 290), an **$80bn Anthropic-stake revaluation → EPS $8 vs street $3**, and tags GOOGL the "safest AI play." The two-sided reads: **Octahedron (fund, 07-15)** calls GOOGL a capex-overhang name "hostage to FCF revisions" (Gemini Spark the product bright spot), and **Citrini (07-20)** frames Meta as extending its ad-monetization lead over Google. Barclays (07-17) reiterates OW/$405 with the GCP margin-agnostic read. **08-05 (FUNDA "Deep|LLM"), and it is the first item in this window that argues with the *test* rather than the *answer*:** the whole capital-intensity debate above — negative FCF, $195-205B, the payback that "does not close" — is run on an application lens (where is the next coding-like use case, does current revenue cover capex). FUNDA's counter is that capex should be read as a dual investment in current cloud returns AND an option on having enough compute when the RSI threshold arrives, with compute as the least compressible input and the deepest moat; and it supplies the two hardest Google-specific facts on the self-improvement loop anywhere on this page — **AlphaEvolve recovering 0.7% of Google's global compute continuously (plus a 23% speed-up on a Gemini training kernel), and AI-written code at Google going 25% → 50% → 75% in eighteen months.** ⚠️ **Net effect on the window: it does not move a number and it does not rescue the stock from the capex de-rating — FUNDA concedes explicitly that "strategic necessity is not the same thing as shareholder return." What it does is give the bull side a coherent reason the $5T-cap/no-re-rate framing may be measuring the wrong thing, and it hands the page a falsifiable tracking set (release interval, pre-training-to-ship, time-to-price-cut, METR doubling time) that is cheaper to monitor than another quarter of ARR. Against that set, Gemini 4 is the only Google-specific test that matters — which is exactly where the 08-05/06 leadership overhaul left the page.**

| Theme | Q2'25 (2025-07-29) | Q3'25 (2025-10-29) | Q4'25 (2026-02-04) | Q1'26 (2026-04-29) | Q2'26 (2026-07-22) |

Google-specific and net-new: **AlphaEvolve used Gemini to evolve algorithms, speeding a key Gemini training kernel by 23% and continuously recovering 0.7% of Google's global compute**; **75% of new code at Google is AI-generated, 25% → 50% → 75% over eighteen months**; the **Anthropic-Google compute partnership sized at "up to one million TPUs"** (cross-check vs the ~4GW / ~$40bn marks already on this page before modelling). Framework: capex as a dual investment in current cloud returns AND an option on sufficient compute at the RSI threshold, rather than an ARR-payback calculation; compute as the least compressible input; native self-improvement + owned experimental stack as the moat once distillation of black-box outputs stops replicating a lab's learning mechanism; "experiment compute" as a third demand category. Scenario ladder (RSI ~2027 / 2029+ / unsolved) and the explicit caveat that strategic necessity ≠ shareholder return. ⚠️ FUNDA's ~$725B four-hyperscaler 2026 capex aggregate is a secondary figure sitting below the desk trackers on this page; Google Cloud +82% is cited by FUNDA but is already on the page from the 2Q26 release (primary wins).

Silicon leg: >1 GW of TPU systems financed by $34.5bn of debt (Apollo-managed funds with Blackstone and global banks; $30bn Broadcom-backstopped, $4.5bn not) into the "AI XPV Platform" equipment SPV, on a FIVE-YEAR Anthropic lease, drawn in ~16 stages with ~$24bn out by summer 2027 (A1 $6bn UST+1pp / A2 $24bn 5.75% / B $4.5bn 8.5%) — read as TPU demand durability in financing form. Datacenter leg, and this is the item genuinely new to GOOGL: GOOGLE PROVIDES CONDITIONAL SUPPORT PROTECTING INVESTORS AGAINST PART OF THE LOSSES IF FLUIDSTACK STOPS PAYING RENT across five sites totalling 1,428 MW of critical IT and ~$15.183bn of project debt (Lake Mariner 378MW/$3.200bn · Barber Lake 207MW/$1.733bn · Abernathy 168MW/$1.300bn · River Bend 245MW/$3.250bn · Meridian Arc 430MW/$5.700bn), debt raised BEFORE construction; at Lake Mariner Google can pay missed rent and assume the lease or fund a termination payment applied to the project debt. Epoch: "Google places its credit behind a deployment from which it benefits commercially, while institutional investors provide most of the cash" — the mechanism named "VENDOR-SUPPORTED FINANCING".** ⚠️ **Logged as CONTINGENT AND UNQUANTIFIED — "part of" the losses, no cap/fee/accounting treatment in the source; explicitly NOT sized, flagged for the next 10-Q. ⚠️ UNIT DISCIPLINE: $15.183bn / 1,428 MW ≈ $10.6m of project DEBT per MW of CRITICAL IT (≈$10.6bn/GW) — a debt-per-watt figure on an IT-load basis, never to be compared with the $30–35bn/GW Alphabet capex-per-GW mark (DB · Black, 2026-07-20) or netted against any $/W revenue figure.**

SemiAnalysis quantifies it: "MORE THAN 20% OF TOTAL TPU SHIPMENTS FROM 3Q26 TO 4Q27 ARE BEING SOLD DIRECTLY TO [[ANTHROPIC]]" — excluding the hundreds of thousands already rented, and the many hundreds of thousands more committed to Anthropic and [[META]] over the next six quarters.**

NOTHING SUPERSEDED: no number, rating, PT, estimate or thesis on this page was changed or replaced by this ingest.** **Added: 1 `## Current state` bullet · 1 `## Debate / thesis` bull sub-bullet · 1 `## Catalysts / what to watch` bullet · 1 Full-log row (08-05, capital/bull) · appended text to the existing "Research-process automation" Signal-vs-management row and to the "Quarter synthesis" paragraph · 1 `## Sources` entry.**
  **THREE NET-NEW GOOGLE DATAPOINTS: (1) AlphaEvolve — Gemini used to evolve algorithms, "speeding up a key kernel in Gemini's own training by 23% and continuously recovering 0.7% of Google's global compute"; (2) 75% of new code at Google is AI-generated, a series that moved 25% → 50% → 75% over eighteen months; (3) the Anthropic-Google compute partnership sized at "up to one million TPUs."** ⚠️ **Item (3) is logged as FUNDA's citation and NOT adopted as a house number — this page already carries the same relationship at ~4GW (expert callback, 2026-04-30) and ~$40bn (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16), and 1mn TPUs at v7/v8-class power is materially more than 4GW. The two bases are both retained and neither was overwritten; resolve before either is used in a model.**
  **THE FRAMEWORK, which is why the note is on the bull side of the Debate section: capex should be read as a DUAL investment — current cloud returns plus an option on having sufficient compute when the RSI threshold arrives — rather than as an ARR-payback calculation; compute is the least compressible input because RSI makes talent, data and algorithms progressively endogenous to it; and in an RSI regime distilling black-box outputs cannot replicate a lab's full learning/data/experimentation stack, so native self-improvement capability plus owned compute becomes the differentiator (the strongest third-party statement of the TPU/vertical-integration case now on this page). Adds "experiment compute" as a third demand category absent from every capex model here.**
  ⚠️ **DELIBERATELY NOT ADDED TO THE BODY (primary source already covers it): Google Cloud +82%, which FUNDA cites as current-return support — this page carries it from the Alphabet 2Q26 release (2026-07-22), and the primary wins. FUNDA's ~$725B four-largest-hyperscaler 2026 capex aggregate (+~77% y/y, absorbing the majority of operating cash flow) is likewise NOT promoted to a body datapoint: it is a secondary aggregation that sits below JPM's ~$750bn desk tracker already logged on this page (2026-07-30); it appears only inside the framework bullet as FUNDA's own basis, explicitly labelled.**

NO number, rating or PT changed on this page in this flow — the drift is in the BURDEN OF PROOF, not the model.**
  **Facts logged: (1) Demis Hassabis steps aside as Google DeepMind CEO → CHAIRMAN of the AI unit + CHIEF SCIENTIST OF ALPHABET, with "few direct reports," and more time at Isomorphic Labs (where he stays CEO); (2) Koray Kavukcuoglu — GDM CTO / Google Chief AI Architect — takes GDM as SVP, running day-to-day Gemini model development and frontier research; (3) JEFF DEAN LEAVES to co-found a startup, reportedly with Sanjay Ghemawat, Oriol Vinyals and Quoc Le, named "Discovery Loop."** ⚠️ **Attribution discipline: The Information's own reporting names only Dean's departure; the four-person exodus and the "Discovery Loop" name are @firstadopter's relay of press reporting and are logged as press-relay, NOT company-confirmed.**
  ⚠️ **THE TWO-SIDED READ IS PRESERVED ON PURPOSE, because the same publication ran both sides on the same day. BEAR (The Information · Erin Woo): "likely to strike a blow to morale… could COMPLICATE Google's efforts to catch up in the race to develop the best AI models, particularly if more researchers leave… Google has fallen behind in the AI model race, PARTICULARLY IN CODING. Its next flagship model has been DELAYED." SHRUG (The Information · Martin Peers): "today's news doesn't mean much — Hassabis wasn't running DeepMind day-to-day, Kavukcuoglu was," and internal chatter is that "Dean had become LESS CENTRAL to Google's Gemini efforts." Peers's reframe is a genuine net-new item for this page: the real question is PICHAI SUCCESSION, where Hassabis had been on the short list alongside Cloud chief Thomas Kurian, and a scientist role arguably removes him from it.**
  🔴 **The one net-new MECHANISM, and it cuts into this page's TPU-monetisation bull case: CNBC (via @firstadopter) reports researchers "have grown frustrated over ACCESS TO THE COMPUTING CAPACITY they need to pursue ambitious projects WHILE WATCHING GOOGLE CLOUD SELL TPUs TO OUTSIDE CUSTOMERS, INCLUDING [[ANTHROPIC]]" — i.e. merchant-TPU revenue carries an internal-talent cost that the TPU-as-a-Service revenue models (Barclays/Sandler) do not capture. Logged as a NEW BEAR ARGUMENT, not as a resolved fact.**
  **Second net-new bear, and it is the falsifiable one: @alexeheath's sources (via @firstadopter) say internal sentiment on GEMINI 4 is "MUTED… not expected to push frontier AI forward the way Fable and Sol just did," with Google "currently running behind the frontier, especially on coding." This converts the page's existing soft flag (Jefferies 07-22: call responses on frontier/agentic coding "fairly disappointing," "silence on 3.5 Pro deafening") into a dated, testable event. Gemini 4 is now the highest-stakes catalyst on the page.**
  **Also added: Kavukcuoglu's first public appearance — The Information AI Agenda Live, SF, 2026-09-23 — whose billed agenda includes "Rationing the Backbone: how Google's highly exclusive executive council decides who gets access to scarce, high-stakes AI chip capacity," i.e. the internal-vs-merchant TPU allocation question on the record; and the attrition run-rate for tracking (Shazeer → [[OPENAI]], Jumper → [[ANTHROPIC]], Dean → own startup).** Desk colour retained: MS TMT (Wigg) frames the debate as **"frontier 'brain drain' vs infrastructure winner"** — the bull case being re-underwritten on TPUs/Cloud rather than model leadership; @dylan522p's DeepMind contact "coping so hard in my DMs"; @jukan05: "Google today feels like Meta when it was assembling MSL, while Meta today feels like Google when it was rolling out Gemini 3."
  **Separately (same window, unrelated to the shake-up): BofA's App Store tracker puts Google Search app in-app DAU share at 72%, DOWN FROM 88% IN JAN-2025, with ChatGPT July monthly revenue >$275mn vs Claude ~$65mn and "accelerating" (BofA · Wamsi Mohan on [[AAPL]], 2026-08-05) — filed here as a Search-substitution datapoint; cross-filed [[AAPL]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [[OPENAI]]. NO rating, PT, house estimate or BBG estimate superseded** — DB $430, Barclays $405, TD Cowen $475, MS marks all stand; Fubon carries no GOOG rating. **NOTHING overwritten: the 12-15mn 2028 TPU target, the four-compute-die V9, the 30-35mn total-compute-die figure and "Intel's supply is a must by 2028" were all already on the page from the 08-03 Jefferies desk relay and the 07-28 Fubon PDF, and the primary transcript RECONFIRMS every one of them verbatim with no revision. No number moved to Changelog because none was replaced.** **Net-new from the primary (absent before this run): (1) ROADMAP DATES — V8 in production Q4'26, V9 in Q1'28, and the V9 chip NOT YET TAPED OUT (expected end-2026), which means every 2028 estimate on this page is pre-tape-out. (2) The 2028 vendor split — 4-5mn of the 12-15mn to MediaTek — recorded strictly as a DEMAND-side allocation, since Fubon explicitly refuses to convert it into production ("we don't yet have the real data point for 2028 capacity allocation"). (3) 🔴 A THIRD SUPPLY GATE the page had never logged: L11 TPU RACK ASSEMBLY. Per Arthur's ODM checks, Hon Hai's capacity learning curve is too slow, so Hon Hai likely takes the V8 rack system in 2027 and Google "needs more supplier" to hit 2028 — a supplier-qualification constraint sitting downstream of both the TSMC-CoWoS and Intel-EMIB gates the page already tracks. (4) The ODMs' no-2027-memory-spec-change check covers GOOGLE BY NAME, not just NVDA — relevant as a rebuttal of the "hyperscalers despec memory to absorb cost inflation" bear objection. (5) The framing that the 12-15mn target exists "despite market concerns regarding Google's CAPEX and negative free cash flow" — i.e. the supply chain is being asked to build for more at the moment the equity market is punishing the capex line.** **Recorded as an OPEN TENSION rather than resolved: how Google can be supply-constrained while selling TPUs externally. Shang does not answer from his own checks — he defers to TSMC management's earnings-call assurance on no overbooking/double-booking and chips not becoming inventory. Logged as the answer given, with its limits stated (a foundry's account of its own order book, relayed second-hand, and it does not address whether external TPU sales compete with Google's internal allocation).** **Cross-linked rather than duplicated:** Intel EMIB yields/capacity → [[INTC]]; MediaTek share/ASP/gross margin and the 400G SerDes surprise → [[MEDIATEK]]; TSMC CoWoS capacity and capex → [[TSM]]; Broadcom's V10 design (8 [2]nm dies, 6,000W TDP) and the testing-capacity push-out → [[AVGO]]. **Unresolvable, left flagged:** "Weichuang" (ASR-garbled Taiwanese ODM) written as-is rather than guessed; "Zebrafish" quoted as-is per the run glossary.
- **2026-07-31 (wiki-ingest) — a new Gemini-optimised chip, the first TPU system-sale revenue recognition (with the accounting warning that follows from it), and Google as the named cause of Reddit's de-rating.** No rating or PT superseded. **① The scoop: Google is developing a new chip designed to run Gemini models FAR MORE EFFICIENTLY; Reuters, Bloomberg and CNBC followed, and the reporting was "credited with helping lift Alphabet shares 3%"** (The Information · Woo/Liu, 2026-07-31). **② ⚠️ THE MOST IMPORTANT BASIS POINT ADDED TO THIS PAGE: Google began recognising TPU SYSTEM-SALE REVENUE for the first time this quarter, "with the vast majority expected in 2027" (JPM · Cardoso) — and MS warns those sales book GROSS while AWS books Bedrock 3P consumption NET. Ex-TPU, MS estimates Google Cloud added only $3-4bn q/q, BELOW AWS and close to Azure's ~$3.9bn, despite Google Cloud adding the most headline revenue dollars (~$4.74bn vs AWS ~$4.65bn). AWS added more EBIT dollars ($2.5bn vs $2.2bn) off the smaller revenue add, because "TPU sales have a structurally lower margin." Barclays adds that GCP's largest headline acceleration (+21pp vs AWS +9pp, Azure +4pp) also benefits from a smaller base and an easier comp.** Net: **Google's cloud line now mixes a services business and a hardware business at different margins, with the hardware piece scaling into 2027 — so revenue-growth comparisons against AWS and Azure will become LESS meaningful over time, not more.** **③ Capex: C2Q26 +26% q/q and +100% y/y to $45bn (~60% servers); FY26 guidance RAISED to $195-205BN from $180-190BN = 115%+ y/y or ~+$105BN in dollars, the largest dollar increase of any hyperscaler, with a significant 2027 increase reiterated. ⚠️ Note the reason differs from peers — Google attributed it to "an ACCELERATION IN THE DELIVERY OF CAPACITY," whereas AMZN's $20bn raise was attributed to memory costs and META's to component/memory pricing. Cloud backlog +$50bn q/q to $514bn, the largest of the four.** **④ Vendor-financed AI capacity is becoming a category worth tracking rather than a set of one-offs: Google is reportedly providing a CREDIT BACKSTOP plus CHIPS for an Anthropic 1.6GW / $15bn Texas campus (WSJ, 2026-07-30), structurally the same move NVDA is making with neoclouds (see [[NBIS]]). Related: Anthropic renegotiated its Amazon agreement around TOKENS rather than COMPUTE HOURS, "a change that could raise Amazon's costs"** (The Information · Cathy Gao). **⑤ Google as the named cause of [[RDDT]]'s 23% fall on 07-31 — a ranking change down-weighting machine-translated content plus weak CTR conversion in AI Overviews, with GOOGL at ~40-50% of Reddit's inbound traffic, framed by Redburn as Search moving "towards a ZERO-CLICK WORLD."** ⚠️ Regulatory corollary logged alongside: **Jefferies flags US antitrust decisions beginning to bite [[AAPL]] Services partly through a LOWER REVENUE SHARE WITH GOOGL** — the same dominance that captures Reddit's traffic is what is under litigation. Cross-filed [[AMZN]], [[MSFT]], [[RDDT]], [[AAPL]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[ORCL]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md), [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md).

Entering 2028, Google's TPUs will enter the V9 generation with FOUR COMPUTE DIES, suggesting that their capacity consumption will MORE THAN DOUBLE in 2028 vs. 2027"** — a four-die package does not fit inside TSMC's book at that volume, regardless of what EMIB-T yields do. Those cannot both be right at face value (12-15mn dies ≈ 3-4mn packages; 12-15mn packages ≈ 48-60mn dies). Both readings kept verbatim; for calibration UBS's 07-27 EMIB-T model has **2.3mn Google chipsets in 2028** at four XPUs per package, which is consistent with the package count being low-single-digit millions. **Third, and the sharpest cross-check against this page's own equity narrative: Fubon calls Google's 2028 target "very aggressive" EXPLICITLY "despite market concerns on Google capex and negative FCF"** — i.e. the packaging supply chain is being told to build for a plan the equity market punished with a -7.8% week on the first negative-FCF quarter. That is the supply-side/equity-side divergence stated by a source that has no equity axe on GOOGL (its top TPU pick is MediaTek). **On the MS note: the ROIC framework is the material addition, not the PT.** All three models standardise on **1GW of GB300 (410,256 chips, $39bn all-in capex/GW, ~$6bn/yr depreciation, 75% utilisation, $7.6bn/GW opex, 21% tax)**, and **GOOGL is the only name MS puts on both sides** — bullish "hyperscalers AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT" *and* "model developers GOOGL and META." GCP leg (**~67% incremental EBIT margin, ~31% ROIC** at $8.50/hr) plus Gemini leg (**~75% margin, ~46% ROIC** on owned infra at 2,750 tok/s/GPU and $1.75/mn) is the first published statement that Alphabet captures both the landlord and the tenant spread on the same gigawatt — the quantified form of the vertical-integration argument Google's own Principal Architect made on 06-23. The rented-infra framework (**~25% NOPAT margin** at $7.75/hr) prices the ~$50/W SpaceX arrangement as a bridge cost. **Two caveats logged rather than buried:** MS states reported near-term incremental margins run **below** the band (prior-gen mix, traditional-cloud mix, unmodelled opex), so this does not contradict the 35.6% reported Cloud OI margin; and MS explicitly notes the frameworks are **GB300-based** — "while custom ASICs and prior GPU generation have lower costs… we believe chip pricing/hour and token throughput are also lower" — so **these are not TPU economics and should not be quoted as such**. Risk-reward detail added (all net-new except the $400): bull **$450**, bear **$225**, the **~1.6x PEG / ~35% peer-median premium** framing, **spot $319.74**, mean consensus PT **$420.48** (range $220-$515), **89% OW / 11% EW**, options-implied **P(>$400) ~23.5% vs P(<$225) ~15.3%** — **the only one of MS's four internet names where the base case is more likely than the bear**, which reads the 07-22 drawdown as capex de-rating rather than thesis impairment. Two anomalies logged, not corrected: MS shows **two operating-income series** in the same table (129,039→280,882 and 137,054→287,432), and its FY26e **net income exceeds its own EBITDA** (255,100 vs 241,667) — plausibly the Anthropic revaluation BofA models (07-16), but MS does not label it. Constructive counterweight recorded alongside: AI debt financing modelled to **~$7.1T outstanding by 2029** (vs ~$13T US MBS) on **>$2T annual AI capex in 2028**, constraint sequence capacity → chips → financing.
